# Session history

Historical record of shipped features and session notes. Not needed at conversation start — only useful for tracing why something was built a certain way.

---

## Session summaries (prepended — newest first)

**2026-08-10 (Windows, web, session 2) — Taste page UI/UX rebuilt as an editorial data-story: personalized aurora hero, numbered scroll-revealed sections, real hover tooltips on every chart, era band, ramp-colored score bins, canon gauge.**

- **Why:** the report read as a flat stack of 8 identical white cards — no hierarchy, no motion, charts interactive only via native `title` tooltips (invisible on touch), the canon stat a bare progress bar, the dumbbells unlabeled, and the 12-month sparkline crammed inside a stat tile.
- **New `components/sj/TasteCharts.tsx`** — chart/motion primitives for the report: `Reveal` (IntersectionObserver fade-up, fires once), `CountUp` (rAF ease-out), a shared pointer-tracked bin-hover layer + ink tooltip (`useBinHover`/`BinTooltip` — real tooltips on year/score/activity charts, replacing native `title`), `YearChart` (accent bars + 5-yr trend + shaded mean±sd "your era" band), `ScoreChart` (half-star bins wearing the app's OKLCh score ramp — color restates the x-axis, never sole encoding, with the 1→5 `RampLegend` stated), `SceneBar`, `CanonGauge` (radial donut, dashoffset sweep on reveal), `DumbbellAxis`/`DumbbellRow` (shared 1–5 tick header; CSS-only value tooltip on hover/focus), `ActivitySpark`. All motion collapses under `prefers-reduced-motion` via `TASTE_MOTION_CSS`.
- **`app/(main)/taste/page.tsx` rebuilt:** hero header with an aurora gradient mixed from the user's own top-3 world ramp colors (separate light/dark layers via `color-mix`), 30/38px display headline, count-up stat row (ratings · genres · worlds); numbered `Section` cards (sequential numbering survives conditional sections) with interpretive leads promoted from muted to `text-mid`; top-album score now a spectrum-fill badge; 12-month activity promoted out of the tile into its own labeled strip; standings sorted by user-vs-community divergence; page widened to `max-w-4xl`. Refresh/cache semantics untouched (refresh remains the only `?refresh=1` path).
- **`components/sj/TasteGraph.tsx`:** map canvas up to 500px on a dot-grid field, bubble hover brightening, legend pill now names the scale ("avg score"), side-panel world list gained per-world share bars; dead `YearHistogram` removed (superseded by `YearChart`). Layout/zoom/interaction logic untouched.
- **i18n:** 11 new `sj.taste.*` keys added to both `en.ts` and `ko.ts` (statGenres/statWorlds/eraBand/mapLegendScore/standingsSub/notYourThingSub/canonInCanon/activityHeader).
- **Palette validated** (dataviz six-checks script) for the scene series on both surfaces — passes; the light-mode contrast WARN is relieved by the existing named legend + percentages. API route untouched — pure front-end rebuild.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, `next lint` (3 touched files), and full `next build` all clean. Not yet walked through live in the browser — worth a hands-on pass (aurora legibility in dark mode, reveal timing feel, tooltips on touch).

**2026-08-10 (Windows, web) — Rating-gauge polish + an app-wide ratings store so a rated album shows its score everywhere and one rating updates every instance at once.**

- **New `components/sj/RatingsStore.tsx` (`RatingsProvider` / `useRatings` / `useRating`), mounted in `AppShell` inside `SessionProvider`.** Loads the signed-in user's full manual-rating set once per sign-in (paginated in 1000-row pages so a heavy rater isn't truncated at Supabase's default cap) into a `Map<release_group_id, number | null>` where a *present* key = "known" (a score, or `null` = known-unrated) and an *absent* key = "unknown, fall back to whatever prop the caller has." `setRating(id, score|null)` is the single optimistic write path (upsert for a score, delete for null); `applyLocal(id, score)` updates the cache only, for pages that own their DB write.
- **`AlbumRateButton` is now store-driven.** It reads its score from the store (falling back to `initialScore`/`score` only until the store learns the album), and writes through `setRating`. Net effect: a rated album shows its score on surfaces that never fetched ratings (notably `FeedCard`, which passed no `initialScore` at all and always rendered empty before), and rating one cover updates every other instance of that album on the page immediately — no per-page plumbing.
- **Wired the other bespoke rating surfaces into the same store so writes propagate app-wide, not just within their own page state:** the album detail page pushes its resolved score via `applyLocal` in `loadRatings` (so its inline editors sync to cards elsewhere and vice-versa); `search` routes `saveQuickRating` through `setRating` (keeping its own `ratedIds`/`sessionRatedIds` sets for card UI); `quick-add`'s `rateAlbum` goes through `setRating` (songs stay on `track_ratings`, local).
- **Drag-to-null now voids an existing rating.** `FlowerRateControl.onRate` widened to `(score: number | null)`. A drag that ends back inside the dead zone used to just cancel; now, if the album is already rated, it commits `null` → the rating is deleted. Unrated albums still plain-cancel (nothing to void). All the direct-`FlowerRateControl` callers (search/quick-add card components) and their handlers were widened to accept null and delete accordingly.
- **Translucent fade for gauge visibility.** The drag overlay (`DragGauge`) now paints a soft radial vignette centred on the stationary flower — darkest across the gauge band, clearing beyond its reach — so the arc, ticks and live score read against busy album art. Fades in on mount (rAF → opacity). The dead-zone ring turns a warm red with a "Remove" cue when releasing would void an existing rating.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean and `next lint` clean across all touched files. Not yet walked through live in the browser — worth a hands-on pass to feel the void gesture and confirm the scrim reads well in both themes.

**2026-08-07 (Mac, session 2) — User reported slow tab/page loads; found and fixed real serial-await waterfalls, then fixed two false-negative UI bugs the user reported in follow-up (`95e561d`).**

- **Perf investigation started from "loading is taking a lot of time, especially browsing tabs and new pages."** Surveyed every ViewModel's `load()` (Home, Charts, Discovery, Profile, Quest, AlbumDetail, MixLibrary, UserProfile) — most were already well-optimized from prior sessions (lazy per-tab loading, `async let` fan-out, a two-tier `ImageCache` with disk persistence keyed pre-redirect, an enlarged `URLCache`). Found the one recurring gap that survived those passes: social-data lookups (likes/comments/saves) firing as sequential `await` calls instead of concurrent ones.
- **Fixed 4 spots, all converted to the same `async let` idiom already used elsewhere in these files:** `AlbumSocial.loadCounts`/`loadMyLikesAndSaves` in `AlbumDetailView.swift` (2 call sites, hit on every album-page visit — likely the single most-visited "new page" in the app), `HomeViewModel.loadSocialData` (5→parallel) and `loadMixShareSocialData` (3→parallel) in `HomeView.swift` (both gate Home's cold-launch first paint), `ProfileViewModel.loadMixShareSocialData`/`loadSongRatingSocialData` in `ProfileView.swift` (3→parallel each). Home's load path alone goes from ~8 sequential round-trips to ~2. Module builds with `-default-isolation=MainActor`, so the post-`await` dictionary mutations stay safe as single-threaded.
- **User then reported a second bug: the Mixes subtab in Profile occasionally showed "No mixes yet" falsely.** Every account gets a default "Listen Later" mix via a DB trigger (`_create_default_mix`, `20260620000001_mixes.sql`, fires `AFTER INSERT ON profiles`) — a genuinely empty result should be impossible. Root cause: `MixLibraryViewModel.load()`'s primary fetch used `try? ... ?? []`, so a transient network failure was indistinguishable from "really zero mixes," and the `hasLoaded` guard (needed so the view model survives tab switches without refetching) then latched that false empty state until something else forced a reload. Fixed: fetch as `[Mix]?` (nil = the request failed, not "empty"), one retry, `hasLoaded` only set on success, and a distinct "Couldn't load your mixes" + Retry state in the view instead of falling into the "No mixes yet" + Create-a-Mix empty state. Mirrors a convention already established in `SearchView.swift`'s artist-releases loader (comment there: "a timeout must not masquerade as an artist with zero releases").
- **Third bug, same shape: Taste tab occasionally showed the "Rate N more releases to unlock" lock screen falsely.** `TasteViewModel.load()`'s album/song rating counts (feeding the `ratingCount >= 25` unlock gate) used `try? ... ?? 0` — a flaky count query could report 0 for a user with hundreds of ratings, briefly failing the gate. Same fix pattern: counts fetched as `Int?`, one retry each, a `loadFailed` state distinct from "below threshold," and a "Couldn't load your taste progress" + Retry view instead of the misleading lock screen. (Also parallelized the two count queries with `async let` while rewriting — they were sequential before.)
- **Verified via `xcodebuild -scheme sillajuku -destination 'generic/platform=iOS Simulator' build` → BUILD SUCCEEDED after every change (4 separate verification passes across the session).** Not walked through live in a simulator/device session — no way to force a transient network failure to visually confirm the new retry states render as intended, or to feel the actual before/after tab-switch latency. Worth a hands-on pass next session.
- Scope note: `.claude/scheduled_tasks.lock` and `apps/ios/LiquidGlassIconLayers/README.md` were already modified (uncommitted) in the working tree at session start, unrelated to this work — left untouched and unstaged, not part of this commit.

**2026-08-06/07 (Mac) — Continuation of the Guideline 5.2.1 screenshot response: fixed a wrong iPhone dimension assumption via a real ASC validation error, swapped one iPhone screenshot per user feedback, and captured the full iPad (13") set.**

- **iPhone dimensions were wrong despite matching Apple's own developer docs.** The prior session resized to 1260×2736 ("6.9-inch" per Apple's published spec sheet), but uploading to App Store Connect produced an actual validator error: this app's configured screenshot slot only accepts **1242×2688, 2688×1242, 1284×2778, or 2778×1284**. Re-resized all 4 iPhone screenshots from the original source PNGs (not the already-JPEG-compressed versions, to avoid double compression) to **1284×2778** and re-delivered to `~/Desktop/AppStore-Screenshots/`. Lesson: trust the platform's live validator over its own documentation when they disagree — ASC's actual accepted-slot list is apparently configured per-app/per-listing, not a single fixed spec.
- **User rejected the "Connected Accounts" screenshot on sight** ("doesn't look good enough for the preview") — pivoted to capturing the **login screen** instead (full branding + all three OAuth buttons), delivered as `final-2-login-alt.jpg` alongside the original so the user could compare; user's own read was the login screen was the stronger choice.
- **Captured the full iPad (13") set** on the "iPad Pro 13-inch (M5)" simulator, reusing the same disposable `orangeandmustard` account (signed in fresh via Google on this simulator). The simulator's native resolution (2064×2752) is an exact match for Apple's 13" iPad screenshot slot — no resizing needed, only PNG→JPEG conversion to strip alpha. Delivered to `~/Desktop/AppStore-Screenshots-iPad/`: already-rated album page, login screen, Instinct "Which do you prefer?" comparison, and the manual drag-to-rate flower widget mid-gesture (captured via an actual `idb ui swipe` across the flower row, landing on a real in-progress-looking 4/5 state rather than a static mock).
- **Real process lesson: a Settings toggle that visually appears to register doesn't mean it persisted.** Tapping "Instinct" in Rating Mode followed immediately by "Done" and navigating away silently failed to save on the iPad session — the next "Rate this Album" tap opened the ordinary manual flower-drag sheet instead of the Instinct bucket selector (BAD/NEUTRAL/GOOD), with no error surfaced anywhere. Root-caused not by inspecting the database (a direct `profiles.rating_mode` query was attempted but the user explicitly stopped that approach mid-tool-call) but by **re-opening Settings and visually re-confirming the selected state through the UI** — which showed "Normal" was still active. Re-tapping Instinct and re-verifying before proceeding fixed it. Takeaway for future simulator-driving sessions: after any settings change that gates subsequent UI behavior, re-open and visually confirm the setting stuck before building on it, rather than trusting a single tap-sequence.
- **Coordinate-math friction continued to be the single biggest source of wasted taps this session** — screenshots are pixels at the device's 2x scale factor, `idb ui tap` takes points, and the *displayed* (chat-rendered) image adds a third scaling factor on top of that. Several taps landed on the wrong element by using chat-display pixel coordinates directly as `idb` point coordinates without applying the ~0.688 combined conversion factor. `idb ui describe-all`'s real accessibility frames (already in points) remained far more reliable than any manual pixel-to-point math.
- **Side effect, noted for the next session touching this disposable account:** walking the full Instinct flow to reach the "which do you prefer" screen for the screenshot (bucket pick → continue → comparison) left a real rating behind on "Neon Halo" (3.6, same as its comparison partner "AXIS") even though the flow was only ever navigated for the screenshot, not deliberately completed. The account's rated count is now 12 again. Harmless for a disposable demo account, but worth knowing if the count looks off in a future session.
- **USER: decide on final iPhone screenshot set (original Connected Accounts vs. new login-alt for #2), upload both iPhone (1284×2778) and iPad (2064×2752) sets to App Store Connect, and resubmit** with a reply addressing Guideline 5.2.1.

**2026-08-04/05 (Mac) — App Store rejected build 14 (Guideline 5.2.1: real copyrighted album art in App Store screenshots) — built a fictional-catalog screenshot pipeline from scratch, then found and fixed a real OAuth bug along the way.**

- **The rejection was about marketing screenshots, not in-app display.** Displaying real album covers *within* a music-rating app is standard (RateYourMusic, Letterboxd-for-film do the same); Apple's objection was specifically about using recognizable copyrighted art in the App Store product page images. Investigated whether "other apps show real covers" meant anything (Musicboard) — concluded Apple's enforcement here is genuinely inconsistent/reviewer-dependent, not a signal of what's actually safe, especially with this submission already three guideline hits deep.
- **Chose original AI-generated fictional cover art over real content, with real thought put into variety.** First batch (4 covers) all came back as straight photography despite different artists — diversified the remaining prompts across four distinct visual languages (photography, painterly gouache illustration, flat vector graphic design, stylized 3D render) so the album set reads as genuinely varied rather than a template.
- **Built the seed pipeline in two phases**, since the iOS app is hardcoded to production Supabase (`Config.swift`, no offline/mock mode unlike web's `scripts/mock/`) and there's no `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` on this machine (only `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, sufficient for PostgREST + Storage, not the Management API):
  - **Phase A — `scripts/seed-demo-fictional-content.ts`:** 4 fictional artists, 12 fictional albums (~6 tracks each), tagged `source='manual'` for easy identification/cleanup, verified against the *live* schema by querying real rows first rather than reconstructing it from migration history (much more reliable — caught that `artists.id`/`release_groups.source` etc. had evolved past what the early migrations alone would suggest). Cover art uploaded to a new dedicated `demo-covers` Storage bucket (kept separate from the `avatars` buckets on purpose, so the whole thing can be dropped in one shot later).
  - **Phase B — `scripts/seed-demo-profile-data.ts`:** attaches ratings + `spotify_recently_played`/`spotify_artists` to a real, already-authenticated demo profile by username (no Admin-API user creation needed — the account signs in normally once, then this script retrofits fictional data onto it). JSONB inner keys are camelCase (`artistName`, `imageUrl`) — confirmed `SpotifyAlbumDisplay`/`SpotifyArtistDisplay` (iOS) have no custom `CodingKeys` and the SDK's PostgREST decoder doesn't do snake_case conversion, so camelCase is what actually decodes.
- **User picked "orangeandmustard" as the demo account, then flagged it wasn't actually empty** — 4 real ratings from 2026-07-23/24 testing. Confirmed disposable with the user before deleting; wiped and reseeded clean rather than assuming.
- **The user's 4 requested screenshots, in order, each with a real finding:**
  1. **Already-rated album page** — straightforward once the fictional catalog existed.
  2. **"Add page of a user connected to Spotify/Apple"** — this one had real teeth. `SearchView.swift`'s `loadSpotify()` hard-gates on the account's `appMetadata["providers"]` containing `"spotify"` (a previously-fixed bug guard against cross-account cache leakage) before ever reading the DB cache — a Google-only account never even reaches the JSONB I seeded. Tried making it work anyway: manually PATCHing `app_metadata.providers` via the Admin API (blocked once by Claude Code's safety classifier, approved on retry by the user) got the gate to pass — until the very next real sign-in, because GoTrue recomputes `providers` from the actual `identities` table on every genuine auth event, silently reverting the manual edit. Confirmed by re-fetching the user record after a real Google re-auth: back to `["email","google"]`. That's a hard dead end, not a solvable one — pivoted to **Settings → Connected Accounts** instead (showing Apple Music as "Connected," a real MusicKit permission grant, no server-side identity risk at all) with the user's sign-off.
  3. **Instinct mode "Which do you prefer?"** — needed an actual pairwise comparison pool, so cleared one album's rating, Instinct-rated it (bucket only, no comparison — nothing existed yet to compare against, landed on "Ranked! Rate 4 more to reveal"), then cleared a second album and Instinct-rated *that* one, which produced the real comparison screen against the first.
  4. **Drag-to-rate gesture** — the album-detail hero's own rate button was intentionally removed in an earlier session (redundant with the "Your Rating" post card); found the actual drag target on the post card's own cover thumbnail instead. Captured by backgrounding a slow `idb ui swipe` and screenshotting mid-flight — caught the dotted baseline + fading arc + live score bubble mid-gesture on the first real attempt (after one failed attempt that triggered iOS's edge-swipe-back gesture instead, from starting the drag too close to the screen edge).
- **Simulator driving used `idb`/`idb_companion`** (already installed via Homebrew/pip, not something built this session) — `idb ui tap`/`swipe`/`text`/`describe-point`/`describe-all` for navigation, screenshots via `xcrun simctl io ... screenshot`. Coordinate math was the main source of friction (simulator screenshots are in pixels at whatever scale factor, `idb` taps are in points) — `idb ui describe-point`/`describe-all` (returns real accessibility frames) turned out far more reliable than computing tap coordinates from screenshot pixel measurements by hand, which repeatedly missed. Also hit a Korean-keyboard-default issue that mangled typed search text into Hangul jamo — worked around by navigating via links/artist pages instead of typing search queries.
- **Final delivery:** all 4 screenshots resized from the simulator's native 1206×2622 to the exact required 1260×2736 (6.9" iPhone slot — aspect ratios are close enough, <0.15% difference, that a straight resize introduces no visible distortion), converted PNG→JPEG specifically to strip the alpha channel Apple's spec disallows, moved from the session scratchpad to `~/Desktop/AppStore-Screenshots/` since the user couldn't find the original path. **iPad (13", 2064×2752) set not captured** — would need the same walkthrough repeated on an iPad simulator.
- **Real bug found and fixed, unrelated to the screenshot task:** user reported that signing out and choosing the same OAuth button again auto-continued with the previous account, no way to pick a different one. Root cause: Google's OAuth silently reuses an existing trusted device session unless explicitly told not to (`prompt=select_account`), and Spotify does the same for its login/consent dialog (`show_dialog=true`) — GoTrue forwards arbitrary `queryParams` straight through to the provider's `/authorize` endpoint, so this is a one-line-per-call-site fix once identified. Applied to every OAuth entry point: iOS `AuthViewModel.signIn()` (initial login) and `ConnectedAccountsViewModel.link()` (Settings → connect an additional account), plus web's `login/page.tsx`. Apple deliberately left alone on both platforms — native `ASAuthorizationController` flow on iOS (account switching is a device Settings concern, not this app's), no documented equivalent param for Apple's web OAuth. `xcodebuild` and `tsc --noEmit` both clean after.
- **USER: decide on the iPad screenshot set, move the delivered screenshots somewhere permanent, upload to App Store Connect, resubmit with a reply addressing Guideline 5.2.1.**

**2026-07-30 (continued) — Vercel free-tier CPU exhausted; root-caused to launch-prep crawling, not a platform problem. User asked "AWS vs Vercel, should we switch?" then forwarded the actual Vercel notification (100% of included Fluid Active CPU used, auto-pause risk).**

- **Investigated rather than answering the platform question in the abstract.** No Vercel CLI available in this environment to pull live usage/analytics, so worked from static code review: no `middleware.ts` (which would run on every request and be an obvious culprit), no cron config in `vercel.json`. `jimp` (a pure-JS, CPU-heavy image library, unlike native-backed `sharp`) is in `package.json` but only used in a standalone pipeline script (`scripts/audit-caa-covers.ts`), not in any deployed route — ruled out. `/api/img` (the CAA cover proxy) just streams bytes through with no re-encoding, and is I/O-bound (fetch + backoff waits), which Vercel's Fluid Active CPU billing specifically doesn't charge for — ruled out despite likely being the highest-volume route. `/api/taste/profile` does real per-request compute (cluster cosine-similarity matrices, sorting/filtering up to 500 ratings) but is cached 60s and bounded — a contributor, not the dominant one.
- **Root cause found in `app/robots.ts` + `app/sitemap.ts`.** `robots.ts`'s own comment documents the history: robots.txt was a blanket `disallow: '/'` while pre-launch, and was relaxed to default-allow as part of App Store submission prep — meaning crawler traffic was only recently turned on at all. `sitemap.ts` lists **every album (~414k) and every artist (~49k)** — ~460k real URLs, each a Supabase-backed SSR render. Both files' own comments already flagged the underlying cost concern ("~2.3M dynamic pages... each running several Supabase queries") when deciding to exclude songs (3.16M) from the sitemap — but judged albums+artists "comfortably cheap" and kept them in. That assumption is exactly what the actual Vercel bill just disproved. **This isn't a Vercel-specific problem** — AWS would incur the identical per-page compute cost for the identical crawl volume, so switching platforms wouldn't fix anything.
- **User had already paid for Vercel Pro ($20/mo) before this was root-caused.** Confirmed that was still the right call independent of the underlying cause — an auto-pause while a build sits in active App Store review is a real risk, and $20/mo is trivial insurance against the site going dark at a bad time. Framed clearly: Pro raises the ceiling, it doesn't fix the driver.
- **User said "decide for me."** Chose and shipped the safest, zero-downside fix immediately rather than continuing to present options: `app/robots.ts` now explicitly disallows a list of known AI-training/answer-engine crawlers (GPTBot, ChatGPT-User, Google-Extended, CCBot, ClaudeBot, anthropic-ai, Bytespider, PerplexityBot, Applebot-Extended, meta-externalagent, Diffbot, Omgilibot, Omgili) via per-user-agent `disallow: '/'` rules — robots.txt matches a named User-agent block *instead of* the wildcard block, so Googlebot/Bingbot and everything else keep the existing default-allow rules unaffected. These bots are exactly the "well-behaved, obeys robots.txt" kind the file's own older comment already noted — which is what makes blocking them actually reduce load — and none of them send real visitors back the way search placement does, so this is pure load reduction with no SEO cost. `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Deliberately not done this session (bigger or externally-dependent):** Search Console crawl-rate limiting (needs the user's own GSC login, can't be done from here) and converting album/artist pages to true static generation with on-demand revalidation tied to real data changes instead of the current blanket `revalidate: 3600` (the real structural fix, but a bigger change across every catalog page — deserves its own session rather than folding in here).
- **USER: watch whether the AI-crawler block alone keeps future usage inside Pro's included allowance**, and decide whether the bigger static-generation change is worth doing once real post-launch traffic data exists.

**2026-07-30 (Mac) — App Store rejected build 13 (submission `16d56cd0`, reviewed 2026-07-31 on iPad Air 11" M3): Guideline 2.1(a) App Completeness, "an error message was displayed when using Sign in with Apple." First functional-bug rejection in this cycle (prior three were policy/UX points) — Apple gave no repro steps, error text, or screenshot.**

- **Discovered the repo's tracked build number had drifted from what was actually submitted.** `project.pbxproj` still said `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION = 12` (the value committed last session), but the rejection cites build `1.0 (13)` — the user must have bumped it out-of-band before archiving (Xcode's automatic versioning, or an uncommitted local edit). Bumped to **14** (past the burned 13, not a plain +1) to resync.
- **Investigated rather than guessed.** Read `AuthView.swift`'s alert wiring (any caught error in `AuthViewModel` surfaces verbatim via a "Sign-in failed" alert showing `errorMessage`) and `AuthViewModel.signInWithApple()`'s full catch path. Considered two hypotheses: (a) a Supabase identity-linking conflict from App Review's habit of reusing the same Apple ID across every resubmission, and (b) `AppleSignInHandler.presentationAnchor(for:)`'s window lookup. Favored (b) as the primary suspect because it's genuinely iPad-specific (the review device), sits exactly in the Sign-in-with-Apple code path, and an identity conflict wouldn't be expected to behave differently by device class the way a multi-scene windowing bug would.
- **Root cause (unconfirmed, best-available hypothesis): `.first { $0.isKeyWindow } ?? UIWindow()`** across every connected scene is a known-fragile pattern on iPad — Stage Manager/Split View can keep multiple `UIWindowScene`s alive simultaneously, a backgrounded scene's stale `isKeyWindow` can win the naive lookup, or none can be true during a scene transition. The old `?? UIWindow()` fallback then handed `ASAuthorizationController` a disconnected, scene-less window to present its sheet on. **Fixed:** prefer the foreground-active scene's key window → any key window across all scenes → any window at all → a disconnected `UIWindow()` only as the absolute last resort.
- **Also fixed, real regardless of whether it's the actual root cause:** checked the Supabase Swift SDK source (`AuthError.swift` in the SPM checkout) and confirmed it exposes `.errorCode == .identityAlreadyExists` on the `identity_already_exists` conflict — added a specific catch for it in `signInWithApple()` showing a clear message instead of a raw backend string, matching how web's `/auth/callback` already handles the same error code.
- **Closed a real diagnostic gap:** grepped the codebase and found zero `SentrySDK.capture()` calls anywhere — Sentry was only ever catching uncaught crashes, never handled/caught errors like this one, meaning there is almost certainly no Sentry event for whatever actually happened during this review pass. Added `SentrySDK.capture(error:)` to every catch block in `AuthViewModel` (Apple, Spotify, Google) so any recurrence — on iPad or otherwise — leaves an actual error object instead of another guess-and-ship round.
- **Verify:** `xcodebuild ... build` → BUILD SUCCEEDED after every change including the build-number bump. **Not tested on an iPad simulator/device** — no session available, and this is squarely a multi-scene/iPad-specific fix, so that's the one gap most worth closing before the next upload rather than trusting the hypothesis blind.
- **User asked to test it themselves; walked through it together.** Created a fresh "iPad Air 11-inch (M3)" simulator matching the review device exactly (the device type already existed in this Xcode's list — no download needed, only the iPadOS runtime was a 26.5-vs-26.6 point release off, which doesn't matter for this bug), booted it, and built + installed the app on it. User signed a real Apple ID into the simulator's Settings (required — this can't be done on their behalf) and confirmed Sign in with Apple completed successfully, landing on the real Home feed with actual data. **Base case confirmed working.**
- **Attempted to verify the actual multi-scene edge case, then corrected course.** First tried automating a Split View setup via `cliclick`/`osascript` — blocked immediately: System Events can't see the Simulator's windows at all (Accessibility permission not granted to this process), and even with permission this project's own history (the 2026-07-23 tab-switching investigation) already flagged pixel-coordinate automation as fragile enough to abandon in favor of a proper XCUITest harness. Asked the user to do the one manual Split-View drag themselves instead. **User reported they couldn't perform the drag gesture in the simulator** — which prompted a re-check of the underlying premise rather than pushing further on the gesture itself.
- **Caught a real error in the root-cause reasoning: `UIApplicationSupportsMultipleScenes` is not declared anywhere in this app** (checked `Info.plist`, the pbxproj, and every Swift file — absent, defaults to `false`). That means sillajuku can never have more than one `UIWindowScene` of *itself* alive at once — Split View/Stage Manager with a *different* app doesn't add entries to *our* `connectedScenes`, since each app is its own process with its own independent scene list. **The original "Stage Manager/Split View keeps multiple scenes alive" framing was wrong for this specific app** — there was never a way to reproduce that via multi-app multitasking, which is exactly why the user's attempted repro path wasn't working. The real, narrower risk the fix still correctly guards against: the app's *single* window can be transiently not flagged `isKeyWindow` during a scene activation-state transition — closer to a race condition than something forceable on demand. Corrected the comment in `AuthViewModel.swift` and the framing in this doc + README to say this accurately rather than leave the wrong mental model in the project record. The actual code fix needed no change — the fallback chain (active scene's key window → any key window → any window → blank `UIWindow()` last resort) is a strict improvement regardless of which of these two mechanisms is the real trigger.
- **Where this leaves things:** the base case is confirmed working; the specific edge case can't be forced on demand by design (it's a timing window, not a reproducible state), so there's nothing further to manually verify before shipping. `xcodebuild` reverified clean after the comment correction.

**2026-07-28 (Mac) — App Store rejected build 11 a second time (submission `16d56cd0`, reviewed 2026-07-27) on three points: Guideline 4 (Sign in with Apple still asks for name/email), Guideline 5.1.1(iv) (Apple Music permission screen still skippable), Guideline 2.3.6 (Age Rating missing "Messaging and Chat"). Two are code fixes (done, build-verified); one is an App Store Connect metadata change (not code, flagged for the user to do).**

- **Guideline 4, root cause this time: bringing the "What's your name?" step back at all was the violation, not just gating Continue on it.** Build 10 (2026-07-20) had fully deleted the step and was NOT rejected on Guideline 4; build 11 (2026-07-22/23) restored it as an *optional*, pre-filled-when-available step shown to every provider, believing "not required" was the compliant bar — Apple flagged it again anyway. Confirms Sign in with Apple's design rule is broader: don't show a name/email re-entry screen at all when Authentication Services already supplied that data, whether or not it blocks Continue. **Fix:** `OnboardingView.init` (`apps/ios/sillajuku/sillajuku/Onboarding/OnboardingView.swift`) now skips the `.name` step entirely when `provider == "apple"` — Google/Spotify sign-ins still see it, since they aren't governed by this SIWA-specific guideline. `finish()`'s existing username-fallback for an empty `displayName` needed no change. Comments in `OnboardingView.swift` and `StepName.swift` updated to record this history so a future session doesn't re-restore the step believing "optional" is enough.
- **Guideline 5.1.1(iv): the Apple Music pre-permission screen's "Skip for now" button let a user close the custom message and delay the real MusicKit permission request indefinitely** — Apple's rule is that a custom pre-permission screen must always lead into the system request, not offer an escape from ever seeing it. **Fix:** removed the "Skip for now" button from `StepAppleMusic.swift` entirely; "Continue" is the only action and always calls `requestAndFinish()`, which fires the real `MusicAuthorization.request()` dialog. A user who doesn't want library access still declines *there* (system "Don't Allow") — `requestAndFinish()` unconditionally calls `onFinish()` after the request resolves either way, so declining skips the permission, not onboarding. **Not touched (out of scope, flagged in text only):** `StepNotifications.swift` has the identical "Skip for now" pattern for the notifications permission — Apple's rejection letter only named Apple Music, but the same underlying rule could apply there too if a future review reaches it.
- **Guideline 2.3.6 (Age Rating — Messaging and Chat): not a code change.** Apple wants "Messaging and Chat" set to "Yes" in App Store Connect's Age Rating questionnaire, since the app has user-to-user comments (public/friends/private visibility) on ratings/reviews — grep confirmed there's no dedicated chat/DM feature in either the iOS or web codebase, so this is Apple's broader UGC-communication descriptor catching the comment system. **USER: update the Age Rating selection in App Store Connect** (App Information page → Age Rating → "Messaging and Chat" → Yes) — no PR/build needed for this one.
- **Verify:** `xcodebuild -project sillajuku.xcodeproj -scheme sillajuku -destination 'generic/platform=iOS Simulator' build` → **BUILD SUCCEEDED**. Not tested on a simulator/device interactively (no session available) — the Guideline-4 fix is a pure step-list change (same pattern as the build-10 fix that already shipped and passed review once), and the Guideline-5.1.1 fix removes one button with no new control flow, so risk is low, but a hands-on onboarding walk (Apple sign-in → confirm no name screen; Apple Music step → confirm Continue is the only button and the system dialog fires) is still worth doing before the next submission.
- **User then declared this the final submission and asked for a thorough guideline cross-check, not just the three named points.** Fetched Apple's current App Review Guidelines (1.2 UGC, 4.8 Login Services, 5.1.1 i–x Data Collection) and audited the live codebase against each:
  - **1.2 (UGC — filtering, report, block, contact info):** report exists (`HomeView.swift` "Report" flag action), block-user exists and is wired through `blocked_users` in `AlbumDetailView`/`HomeView`/`UserProfileView`, published contact exists (`SettingsView`'s "Help & Feedback" link). Compliant, no changes needed.
  - **4.8 (Login Services):** Apple Sign-In is offered alongside Spotify/Google with equal visual weight (`AuthView.swift` — all three are the same `Continue with X` button component/size), satisfying the "equivalent option" requirement for apps using third-party social login. Compliant.
  - **5.1.1(i) Privacy Policy:** linked in-app in both `AuthView.swift` (login screen) and `SettingsView.swift`. Compliant.
  - **5.1.1(iii) Data Minimization:** the Instagram-share gallery picker uses `PhotosPicker`/`PhotosPickerItem` (`SharePreviewSheet.swift`) — the out-of-process picker that needs no permission at all — and camera capture (`UIImagePickerController`, `.camera`) has a proper `NSCameraUsageDescription` in `Info.plist`. No IDFA/`ASIdentifierManager`/ATT usage anywhere in the codebase, so no tracking-consent gap either.
  - **5.1.1(iv) (the guideline actually cited in the rejection):** found the **exact same "Skip for now" pattern in `StepNotifications.swift`** — not named in the rejection letter (only Apple Music was), but structurally identical to what just got flagged. Given the user's explicit "align perfectly" ask for a final submission, fixed it the same way as `StepAppleMusic.swift`: removed the skip button, "Continue" is the only action, `requestAndFinish()` still calls `onNext()` unconditionally after the system dialog resolves either way.
  - **5.1.1(v) Account Deletion:** exists and is real — `SettingsView.swift`'s delete-account sheet calls `deleteAccount()` (`Services/WebAPI.swift`), not a stub. Compliant.
  - **Two items surfaced that are the user's call, not code fixes:** (a) Apple shipped a new **"Social Media" age-rating question** in App Store Connect on 2026-07-09 (mandatory from September 2026, optional now) — this app's activity feed/comments/follows plausibly qualifies under Apple's definition ("redistribute, amplify, or interact with user-generated content through a social feed"), and answering "yes" sets a minimum 13+ rating. Given this submission already has two age-rating strikes (Guideline 2.3.6 twice), flagged for the user to check whether it's visible in ASC yet and consider answering honestly now rather than risk a third rejection later — a product/rating decision, not something to decide unilaterally. (b) The 2026-07-10 App Privacy questionnaire completion note listed Name/Email/Photos/Other User Content/User ID/Device ID/Product Interaction/Crash Data/Performance Data, but not explicitly **Phone Number** — the app does collect/verify phone numbers for referral credit (`ConnectedAccountsView`, `PhoneVerificationView`, existing since before that questionnaire per migration `20260706000008`) — flagged for the user to confirm it's declared under Contact Info in ASC.
  - Bumped `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION` **11 → 12** in `project.pbxproj` (ASC requires a unique build number per upload; 11 is burned). `xcodebuild` reverified clean after every change including the bump.

- **User then asked if these changes were applied to web too.** They weren't — Apple's App Review only reviews the iOS bundle, so nothing about this rejection required a web change. But checking `apps/web/app/(main)/onboarding/page.tsx` while answering surfaced the same two shapes: a `name` step shown to every provider including Apple (and *hard-gating* Continue on it — stricter than even iOS's rejected build 11), and the identical "Skip for now" + "Allow Notifications" wording on the notifications step. User asked for cross-platform consistency regardless of App Store scope, so ported both fixes to web:
  - `name` step now skipped when `app_metadata.provider === 'apple'`, read via `supabase.auth.getSession()` (local, no network round-trip) inside a new `ready`-gated effect so the step list is correct before first paint rather than flickering from the default (all-providers) list to the Apple-trimmed one.
  - Continue on the `name` step is unconditional for the remaining providers (Google/Spotify) — matches iOS's `StepName`, which stopped gating on this after the build-9/10 history.
  - `finish()`'s empty-name fallback switched from the email's local part to the username, matching iOS's reasoning (an Apple private-relay email's random local part is a bad display name; the username is already validated by the time `finish()` runs).
  - Notifications step: removed the "Skip for now" button and the "Allow Notifications" button label, replaced with a single "Continue" (reusing the existing `sj.onboarding.continue` i18n key) that always triggers the real `Notification.requestPermission()` call.

Not browser-verified — worth a signed-in walkthrough (Apple OAuth → confirm no name step; notifications step → confirm only one button) before relying on it. The now-unused `sj.onboarding.allowNotifications`/`skipForNow` i18n keys were left in `en.ts`/`ko.ts`, matching this codebase's existing convention of leaving retired copy in place rather than deleting it.

**2026-07-28 (Windows) — "Skyminhyuk shows 0 releases": root-caused to a MusicBrainz coverage gap amplified by the `area` lane; 8,973 empty artists hidden from search; recoverability measured.**

- **The report:** user found artist **Skyminhyuk** rendering 0 releases on web, noting they in fact have a substantial discography.
- **Not a false match, not an ingest failure.** The artist row (`09e7861b…`, KR, `ingest_state='tracks_done'`, `source_status='mb_verified'`, ingested 2026-07-17) is linked to MBID `d99a84a4…`, and that MB entity is unambiguously the right person — Person, KR/Gyeonggido, alias 스카이민혁, with URL rels to their real Instagram/SoundCloud/Spotify/Deezer/Bugs. **MusicBrainz itself returns 0 release-groups and 0 releases for it** (only 6 standalone recordings). iTunes KR, meanwhile, has **25 albums** under 스카이민혁 (artistId `1475047483`). So the discography is real; MB has never catalogued it.
- **Sized the class: 8,973 / 67,518 artists (13.3%) have zero releases** — counting *both* link paths (`release_groups.primary_artist_id` and `release_group_artists`, which is what the artist page's `get_artist_release_groups` UNIONs). Overwhelmingly Korean: **KR 7,662 / 12,413 = 61.7%**, vs US 4.9% · JP 6.4% · GB 5.8%. **88% were created 2026-07-17/18** — precisely the `area` discovery sweep that took KR from ~1,293 to 12,413 artists.
- **Cause chain.** `discover-mb-area.ts` queues every MB `country:KR` / `area:<city>` **artist entity** by MBID; MB entities exist independently of releases (anyone can create one to hang a credit, a URL rel, or a standalone recording on); our ingest walks release-groups → releases → recordings, so zero RGs writes nothing; and **`mb-ingest.ts:485` sets `ingest_state='tracks_done'` unconditionally, ignoring `kept`** — so an empty artist is indistinguishable from a fully-ingested one.
- **Verified it's a coverage gap, not a bug, by re-browsing the live MB API for a random sample of 120** (70 KR + 50 all-country): **~98% have zero release-groups in MB itself**, ~2% features-only, and **0 cases where our own `shouldIngestRG` filter said "keep" but we stored nothing**. (A first pass wrongly reported 37% as ingest misses — the sampler used PostgREST `.in()` over id pages and hit the **1000-row response cap**, so prolific artists like BoA (124 groups) were truncated to "empty". Re-running the candidate query server-side via the Management API corrected it to ~0%. The same trap was then deliberately designed around in the shipped code — see `artists_with_releases` below.)
- **Why no existing lane can rescue them** — each needs an existing release to establish identity: `discover-itunes-backfill.ts:172` aborts on `'no owned release-groups'` (its identity gate is title-overlap against titles we already have); `discover-itunes-recency.ts:68-69` needs a seed title to resolve the streaming artist id; `resolve-thin-artists.ts` needs ≥1 MB feature credit as a corroboration anchor. Chicken-and-egg: you need a release to identify the artist and identity to get releases.
- **FIX SHIPPED — hidden from search (migration `20260728000000_search_hide_empty_artists.sql`, APPLIED).** `search_artists` now requires ≥1 release the artist page could actually render, testing **primary UNION credited** to mirror `get_artist_release_groups` exactly — filtering on `primary_artist_id` alone would have wrongly hidden **36,022** credits-only artists whose pages do render. Both EXISTS probes are index-backed (`idx_release_groups_artist`, `idx_rga_artist`). Signature unchanged, so no web/iOS client changes needed. Also added **`artists_with_releases(uuid[])`** for `/api/search/suggest`, which queries the `artists` table directly rather than through the RPC: answering that filter over PostgREST would have meant de-duplicating the pool's release rows client-side, where **one prolific artist can exhaust the shared 1000-row cap and silently truncate another artist's rows** — reading as "no releases" and hiding a real artist. The RPC returns one row per artist, so the cap is unreachable.
- **Deliberately NOT changed: `ingest_state`.** The obvious-looking fix (a distinct state when `kept === 0`) would have been actively harmful — `claimDueFreshness` (`pipeline.ts:258`) selects `ingest_state='tracks_done'`, so moving these rows out of it would stop the FRESHNESS lane re-polling them, killing the one mechanism that **self-heals** them the moment MB catalogs a release. They stay `tracks_done` with their `next_check_at` intact; the migration only stops us *showing* a dead end meanwhile.
- **Verified:** `search_artists('skyminhyuk')` no longer returns it (nor does the native-name query `search_artists('스카이민혁')`), while BoA (124) / Zion.T (21) and the credits-only "Lee MinHyuk" (1 page row) are all still returned — filter behavior matches `get_artist_release_groups` row-for-row. `/api/search/suggest` runtime-tested against a real dev server: `?query=boa` returns BoA normally, `?query=skymin` returns empty. `tsc --noEmit` clean (only the pre-existing untracked `backfill-rg-covers-kr.ts` error).
- **`PIPELINE_CHECKS.md` corrected.** Its "Reading the results" guide listed `N empty artist(s) to review` under **GOOD (no action)** as benign "features-only acts plus a few generic-name false-matches" — text written when N was a handful, predating the `area` lane. At 8,973 that reading is misleading, so the entry now carries the real cause, the real number, and the reason it's still not something to auto-fix.
- **New `scripts/audit-empty-artists.ts` (REPORT-ONLY, no `--ingest` flag by design)** measures how many of the 8,973 are actually *recoverable*, since the open question is identity confidence, not data existence. Verdicts, strongest first: **HARD_LINK** (MB carries a Spotify/Apple/Deezer artist URL rel — an unambiguous id, safe to auto-ingest), **LIKELY** (a unique exact Spotify name match on a distinctive name — same generic-CJK-name guard as `resolve-thin-artists.ts`), **AMBIGUOUS** (candidate exists but corroboration too weak — needs a human), **NONE** (no candidate; most likely a credited-only person with no discography anywhere). Resumable; `--all` produces the full worklist.
- **Measurement result (n=500, random across the whole population): HARD_LINK 8.4% · LIKELY 12.0% · AMBIGUOUS 38.4% · NONE 41.2% → 20.4% safely recoverable (~1,830 of 8,973).** Recoverability is **inverted against where the problem is**: KR is 61.7% of the empties but only **14.3%** recoverable, while non-KR is **53.2%** — Korean personal names collide (김형우-class) and KR MB entities rarely carry URL rels. Of hard links, Spotify is 35/42.
- **⚠ INCIDENT (self-inflicted): the shared Spotify circuit breaker was tripped.** A throwaway yield-measurement script fired ~95 `getSpotifyArtistAlbums` calls back-to-back **without calling `assertSpotifyCircuitClosed()` first** — the exact guard `scripts/spotify-circuit.ts` exists to provide, whose own header warns "a script burst can trigger an account-wide 429 that breaks production for hours." Spotify returned 429 with `Retry-After: 86400`, and `lib/spotify.ts:78` published it to the shared Redis key `spotify:rate-limited-until`, so **production app-credential Spotify calls fail fast until 2026-07-30 04:50 UTC** (user OAuth unaffected). Left closed deliberately — clearing it would resume hammering an API that is explicitly refusing us. The offending script was deleted. **Lesson: any script touching Spotify must assert the breaker and throttle; `resolve-empty-artists.ts` now does both.** The audit sweep itself was verified uncontaminated (50 of its final 60 classifications required successful Spotify searches).
- **New `scripts/resolve-empty-artists.ts` — the recovery half. DRY-RUN BY DEFAULT (`--write` required).** Implements the **hard-link tier only**: MB's URL relationship gives an explicit streaming artist id, so there is no name matching and no review queue. The name-matched tier is deliberately left unbuilt. Providers: **iTunes** (via ingest-core, `UNIQUE(itunes_id)`), **Deezer** (direct inserts), **Spotify** (direct inserts keyed on `UNIQUE(spotify_id)`).
- **Four defects found in a pre-write adversarial self-review, all fixed before any write:**
  1. **Deezer had NO cross-source duplicate safety net.** `reconcile-itunes-mb.ts` selected `source='itunes'` only. `mb-deezer-fallback` never needed coverage because *its* artists have no MBID (MB can never re-ingest them). Ours **do** have MBIDs and **are** re-polled by the FRESHNESS lane, so every Deezer album written would have become a duplicate the moment MB catalogued it. Fixed by widening reconcile to `in ('itunes','deezer','spotify')` — its matching logic was already source-agnostic, only selection changed.
  2. **A crash window created unreconcilable rows.** `source` was tagged per-group right after insert; an interrupt in between stranded a `source=NULL` release_group, invisible to reconcile forever → a guaranteed future duplicate. Now an artist-scoped sweep after the loop, which also self-heals rows left by earlier interrupted runs.
  3. **Whole-artist abstention on any provider ambiguity** — discarded Skyminhyuk's single clean Spotify id because its Deezer rel had two. Now judged per-provider.
  4. **`groups++` counted `ingestEdition`'s `'skipped'` as gained**, inflating reported yield.
- **Also added: a name-corroboration guard.** The MB rel *is* the identity, but a mis-entered rel would attach a stranger's entire discography — the highest-cost failure available. Every provider's artist name must now corroborate ours, `name_native`, or an MB alias (romanization-tolerant, since iTunes/Deezer list KR acts natively while we may store the Latin form); no match → `name-mismatch`, skipped and reported. Verified it doesn't over-reject: the same 4 artists pass.
- **`20260729000000_spotify_source.sql` (APPLIED)** — `release_groups`/`releases`/`recordings` all CHECK `source` against a fixed list that **excluded 'spotify'**, so every Spotify write would have thrown mid-run. Caught before the bulk run, not during. Widened with `NOT VALID` + `VALIDATE` to avoid a long exclusive lock on ~474k/471k rows; all three verified `convalidated=true`.
- **CONTROLLED WRITE TEST (the only catalog change this session): Nawhij, 7 release groups via Deezer** — the *weaker* path chosen deliberately. Verified: 7 groups / 7 releases (1:1), 34 recordings / 34 release_tracks (1:1), 0 wrong `source`, **0 orphan groups, 0 groups without exactly one canonical**. **Idempotency proved empirically, not argued:** a re-run reported "No matching zero-release artists" — the candidate SQL itself excludes non-empty artists, so a second run cannot even reach the ingest code (plus `stillEmpty()` re-checks inside a run for the FRESHNESS race). Row counts unchanged at 7/7/34. Reconcile's widened filter picks up all 7 (129 unlinked Deezer rows now in scope, up from 0). End-to-end: the artist is now returned by `search_artists`, passes the typeahead filter, and its page renders 7 releases.
- **Spotify ingest path is WRITTEN BUT UNTESTED** — the breaker blocks any live call until 04:50 UTC. It guards the silent-failure shape too: `getSpotifyArtistAlbums` swallows every error and returns `[]`, indistinguishable from "genuinely no albums" (the same bug class as the `getReleaseTracks` fix on 2026-07-22), so an empty result re-checks the breaker and reports `provider-unavailable` rather than recording the terminal verdict "nothing to find".
- **Coverage ceiling of the hard-link tier, stated plainly:** ~8.4% (~750 of 8,973). ~91% have no hard link at all.
- **TIER 2 (2026-07-29, opt-in `--tier=alias`): MB-alias-corroborated iTunes resolution — and a case study in why dry-run inspection is not optional.** Since Spotify carries most hard links and its breaker was open, this path resolves iTunes by name but requires TWO independent gates: the name must be **unique** among exact-name iTunes artists AND **distinctive**. Probing real iTunes data before wiring it in already showed both gates are load-bearing — "이민영" returns 3 exact-name artistIds (uniqueness catches it) while "김형우" returns exactly **one**, so uniqueness alone would have **accepted** it (only distinctiveness rejects it).
- **The first build of that tier was UNSAFE and the dry run proved it — 3 of 10 "recoveries" were wrong (~30% false positives).** Four distinct defect classes, each found only by eyeballing real output, each fixed:
  1. **Whitespace-deleting name key** made `"Marineboy" ≡ "Marine Boy"` → matched a *different*, 196-album hip-hop act. **199 groups on an artist MB has nothing for was the only visible symptom** — the other false positives looked completely normal in the log.
  2. **MB `sort-name` treated as an alias.** It's a sorting key, not a performing name: MB stores "INE" for 아이네, which matched an unrelated **Japanese** artist (hence Japanese titles). `sort-name` is now excluded outright.
  3. **Generic-name guard covered only CJK**, so short Latin handles ("INE", "exci") passed freely.
  4. **Romanized Korean given names.** After fixing 1–3, "한솔" still matched via MB's alias **"Hansol"** (6 chars) — but Hansol/Jihoon/Minjun are as collision-prone romanized as in Hangul. Single-token Latin now requires ≥8 chars ("Skyminhyuk" 10, "Briakitten" 10, "LILMONEY" 8 pass; "Hansol" does not).
- **Effect of the fixes on the same 60-artist sample: 10 recoveries / 253 groups → 5 / 29.** The guards removed half the "recoveries" and **88% of the claimed release groups**, because the false positives were the prolific ones.
- **⚠ Judgement: `--tier=alias` must NOT be bulk-written blind.** Four tightening rounds each uncovered a *new* false-positive class, which is strong evidence more remain undiscovered. The hard-link tier is verified safe for auto-write (Nawhij); the alias tier should produce a dry-run list for human review first. Its coverage (~5–8%, a few hundred artists) is small enough that reviewing is realistic.
- **Skyminhyuk survives every tightened guard: 21 release groups via `itunes-alias`**, matched on MB's genuine alias 스카이민혁 with correct spacing — and needs no Spotify, so the breaker no longer blocks it. **Written live and audited** (see below) — 21/21 groups/releases, 126/126 recordings/tracks, correct provenance, idempotent re-run confirmed.

**2026-07-30/31 (Windows, overnight → morning) — Hard-link tier bulk-written: 260 artists, 3,099 release groups. One real data-integrity bug found and fixed (isolated to 1 artist); the Spotify breaker tripped a SECOND time; a dry-run/write accuracy gap found and fixed. Full catalog-wide safety sweep clean.**

- **Bulk write, round 1 (overnight, `--all --write --resume`): 232 artists, 2,775 groups** (source split emerged: itunes/deezer roughly even). Outcomes: 8,075 no-link · 444 provider-unavailable (Spotify breaker, tripped partway — see below) · 232 ingested · 128 no-albums · 51 name-mismatch · 20 error · 19 ambiguous-link.
- **Found auditing round 1: "Lino" had 63 release_groups stuck at `source=NULL`** — invisible to `reconcile-itunes-mb.ts` (which selects by source) and would have become an unreconcilable cross-source duplicate the moment MB catalogs any of them. Root cause: a `recordings insert` statement timeout on ONE album threw out of the whole per-artist ingest loop, aborting BEFORE the end-of-loop sweep-tag (added 2026-07-29 to close exactly this crash window) ever ran — every EARLIER album that same call had already written was left untagged too, not just the one that failed. **Verified isolated**: a catalog-wide scan (`release_groups.source IS NULL`, no other filter) found this exact ONE artist and nothing else. **Repaired via direct SQL** (safe — Lino had zero releases before this run, so 100% of its rows are known-itunes). **Fixed at the root in all three ingest paths** (iTunes, Deezer, Spotify — the last one pre-emptively, before its first live run): each album's insert sequence now runs in its own `try/catch` so one album's failure can't abort its siblings; the iTunes path additionally wraps its sweep-tag in `try/finally` as a second layer, since it depends on a post-loop batch update rather than tagging inline per-insert like Deezer/Spotify do. Also fixed: transient failures (iTunes 403-streak disable, Deezer/Spotify "every album this call attempted errored out") were being misclassified as the terminal `no-albums`/`no-albums`-shaped outcomes instead of retryable `provider-unavailable` — found the same way, by reading the actual output rather than trusting the summary counts.
- **The Spotify path's first-ever live exercise ("Briakitten") found a real accuracy bug, not a data bug.** Dry-run reported 6 "would ingest" groups; the actual write produced 2. Not wrong data — `getSpotifyArtistAlbums` surfaces some releases (compilations/OSTs) without marking them `appears_on` the way it marks true guest features, but those releases' own metadata credits a DIFFERENT artist id; the per-album artist-match check correctly excluded the other 4, but it ran AFTER the dry-run's `!write` shortcut had already counted them. Moved the check before both paths so the two numbers agree — worth fixing given the session's running theme of inflated dry-run counts (Marineboy, 아이네, 한솔, and now this). **Briakitten written + audited clean**: 2/2 groups/releases, 33/33 recordings/tracks, correct `source`/`spotify_id`, idempotent re-run confirmed (0 change).
- **Bulk write, round 2 (`--all --write --resume` after the overnight state file was lost to interleaved single-artist tests, so this was a full re-scan of the ~8,735 remaining candidates — safe but slower, since already-written artists no longer match the candidate query at all): 22 artists, 248 groups (153 spotify, 95 itunes) before the Spotify circuit breaker tripped AGAIN.** New block: ~23.4h, until **2026-08-01 03:27 UTC** — nearly a full day, this time triggered by THIS script's own sustained volume (~630 artists in) despite the 250ms per-album throttle added after the FIRST trip (2026-07-29, from an unrelated diagnostic script). The per-album isolation fix held under this failure too — round 2's writes audited **completely clean**: 0 null-source, 0 orphans, 0 bad canonicals, 0 zero-track groups, every row carrying its provider id.
- **Strengthened Spotify throttling — proactive, not reactive.** New `spotifyPace()`: every Spotify-bound call (artist lookup, album listing, per-album detail) now goes through a shared minimum spacing plus a longer forced cooldown every 40 calls, active BEFORE any 429 rather than only backing off after one. **UNVERIFIED LIVE** — the new breaker window blocked testing it. The next Spotify run should start with `--limit` on a small batch and confirm zero 429s before trusting it at bulk scale a third time.
- **Final catalog-wide tally (all resolver writes, `created_at >= 2026-07-29` to isolate from older recency-lane itunes/deezer rows): 260 distinct artists, 3,099 release groups — 1,365 itunes · 1,578 deezer · 156 spotify.** Full safety sweep across every one of those 3,099 groups (not a sample): **0 orphan groups, 0 bad-canonical, 0 missing-provider-id, 0 null-source anywhere in the catalog.** 11 groups (0.35%) have 0 tracks — all the same class as Lino's one incomplete release (a transient track-fetch failure that the per-album isolation correctly contained to just that release, rather than losing the whole artist), matching the codebase's own pre-existing "lazy track hydration" convention, not corruption.
- **Reconcile widened + exercised**: `reconcile-itunes-mb.ts` now covers `itunes/deezer/spotify` (was `itunes` only until this session — see 2026-07-29's commit). A full dry pass across everything written so far found **0 already-duplicated rows** and 20 confident links (pre-existing rows MB has since caught up on); applied.
- **Alias tier (`--tier=alias`) full sweep COMPLETED** — launched report-only once the Spotify breaker blocked further hard-link progress (this tier never touches Spotify), and turned out to finish in real wall-clock hours rather than the 60-70h+ initially extrapolated from an early-run rate sample. **Result: 8,712 candidates classified — 551 would-ingest / 4,019 groups, 7,584 alias-rejected, 340 provider-unavailable (itunes), 131 no-albums, 105 name-mismatch.**
- **⚠ Spot-checking the 551 before handoff found a CONFIRMED false positive — the 5th distinct failure class in this tier's development, and the reason the whole session kept insisting on human review rather than trusting the guards.** "Christina" (52 groups) passed both the uniqueness gate and the ≥8-character distinctiveness gate — but its one iTunes artistId turned out to itself be an Apple-side merge of unrelated people sharing that mononym: German Christmas harp music, Spanish pop, a Korean kids' song, Russian pop, spanning **1991–2026**. Uniqueness only proves one iTunes id exists for a name; it says nothing about whether that id's own catalog is internally coherent. Compared against genuinely long, coherent careers in the same 551 (Zack Knight 11yr, Rajvir Jawanda 11yr, Pepe Blanco — a real Spanish singer, 1955–2021), catalog span was the one reliable discriminator.
- **Fixed**: `resolveItunesByAlias` now fetches the resolved artist's discography once (only on the accept path, so cost is paid only where it matters) and rejects if the release-year span exceeds 25 years. **Verified**: Christina now rejects with the span reason; Zack Knight and Rajvir Jawanda still resolve at their original 61/77 group counts, confirming the guard doesn't over-reject genuinely coherent long careers within the threshold. **Retroactively applied to the completed sweep** (no re-run needed — same title/date data already collected): **515 cleared / 3,361 groups, 36 flagged / 658 groups** (same class as Christina; some, e.g. Pepe Blanco, are plausibly real long careers rather than confirmed-bad, but `missing > wrong` argues for review over auto-accepting a guess either way).
- **This guard is explicitly NOT claimed to be a complete fix.** A spot-check of the "cleared" 515 (not just the flagged 36) found a different-shaped red flag the new guard doesn't catch: **"DJ Silent"** (12 groups, all dated 2026 — so it passes the year-span test cleanly — but bilingual Spanish/English with eclectic, disconnected-feeling content). A same-era variant of the identity-merge problem, distinct from the decades-spanning one just fixed. Documented plainly in the review artifact rather than overstating verification. **`scripts/data/alias-tier-review-2026-07-31.json`** is the reviewable output: both buckets, full per-artist title lists, and this exact caveat stated in the file itself. **Nothing from the alias tier has been written to the catalog** — dry-run → human review → `--only-ids` write remains the rule, now with five confirmed reasons behind it rather than a hypothetical one.
- **Commits this session**: `6e52a13` (per-album isolation, Spotify dry-run/write parity), `36ea230` (proactive Spotify pacing), `a0dc5ce` (year-span coherence guard + review artifact) — merged with a parallel Mac session's unrelated iOS/onboarding/robots.ts work (`4ddd609`) and pushed as `0e7f2b5`.

**2026-07-24 (Mac) — Rated-album flower bug fixed (Home + album page), redundant hero rate button removed, and a real performance investigation of "tab switching feels slow" — both confirmed regressions found, fixed, and reverified live; two remaining flagged as follow-ups.**

- **"NOWITZKI shows the flower button, not my score, even though I already rated it."** User found this scrolling Home in Manual mode. Root cause: `AlbumRateButton`'s cover-corner instance (`HomeView.swift`'s `albumSection`) was given no score at all, so it always rendered unrated — the number visible in the feed row (`item.displayScore`) is the *post author's* score, since Home is an activity feed of other users' rating posts, not a "have I rated this" view. Fixed: `HomeViewModel` gained `myScores: [UUID: Double]`, populated in `loadSocialData(for:)` alongside the existing `rating_likes`/`saved_releases` fetches (one more `ratings` query filtered to the viewer + the releases on screen), threaded through `FeedCard` → `AlbumRateButton`'s `initialScore`/`onScoreChange`. `xcodebuild` clean.
- **User: "same for the post in the album page."** Same gap, three more call sites: the album page's "Ratings & Reviews" horizontal carousel (other users' posts about this release), the "See all ratings" full list (`AlbumAllRatingsView`, its own view model), and even the viewer's *own* "Your Rating" post card. Since every post on an album page is about that one release, this was simpler than Home's per-item map — just `viewModel.userScore` (a single value) threaded into each `FeedCard` call, plus a new `myScore` fetch added to the standalone `AlbumRatingsListViewModel` (which didn't previously know the viewer's own rating at all).
- **User: "since there's already a post in the album page, get rid of the flower button in the hero."** Agreed and removed the `AlbumRateButton` overlay from `compactHeader`'s cover — the "Your Rating" section (a full `FeedCard` once rated, a "Rate this Album"/"Add to Rankings" button otherwise) was already the page's one complete rating surface; the hero's own flower was a second, redundant way to do the same thing. `ratingStep`'s only other reference (`ManualRatingSheet`) still compiles fine.
- **User: "browse through the tabs, loading feels too slow — investigate, don't fix yet."** Rather than guess, added temporary timing prints to every tab's `load()` (Home, Charts, Discovery/Add, Taste, Profile) and to `WebAPI.get`, then drove a real cold-launch tab walk with a screen-independent XCUITest harness (an `xcodegen`-generated throwaway project attaching to the real installed app via `XCUIApplication(bundleIdentifier:)`, console captured via `xcrun simctl launch --console-pty` since plain `print()` doesn't route through the unified log / `log stream`). Measured: **Home 3.6s, Profile 2.4s** (fine) vs. **Charts 8.7s, Add 9.1s, Taste 3.4s** (not fine — screenshots confirmed Charts/Add sit on a full-screen spinner with nothing progressive for the whole window). All instrumentation reverted immediately after measuring; the throwaway XCUITest project deleted.
  - **Charts' breakdown nailed it exactly:** 7 of 10 concurrent loaders finish in <0.6s (pulse, unlock status, top/most/trending albums, rating mode); the **3 song-chart loaders** (`loadTopRatedSongs`/`loadMostRatedSongs`/`loadTrendingSongs`) each took **~8.4–8.7s** — 15–20x their album counterparts — and the whole tab gates on all 10 together, so even a user who only looks at "Albums" (ready in 0.4s) waits out the slow song query.
  - **Root cause: a previously-shipped fix for this exact bug got silently reverted.** `20260703000005_song_charts_lateral_fix.sql`'s own comment already diagnosed it once: the song RPCs' `loc` CTE did `DISTINCT ON (recording_id) ... ORDER BY rtk.recording_id, rel.is_canonical DESC` across `release_tracks JOIN releases` — a sort key spanning two joined tables, which no index can satisfy, forcing Postgres to materialize and sort *every track of every release in the catalog* before picking one row per recording. That migration fixed it with a `LATERAL JOIN ... LIMIT 1` scoped to only the (far fewer) rated recordings, using the existing `idx_release_tracks_recording` index. Then **`20260706000002_charts_release_type.sql`** (three days later, adding an `album_release_type` column) redefined the same three functions again — but was evidently written against the pre-fix version, since it reintroduced the identical slow `DISTINCT ON` structure. Confirmed by reading both migrations directly. **Fix:** new migration `20260723000000_song_charts_lateral_fix_reapply.sql`, re-applying the LATERAL structure verbatim while keeping the newer `album_release_type`/native-name columns. **User applied it via the Supabase SQL editor same day.** Re-verified with a fresh, repeated live measurement (same XCUITest+console-pty method, temp instrumentation added back and reverted again after): first run showed the 3 song RPCs down to ~0.55s (from ~8.4s) but a one-off ~4.2s blip on the two album RPCs (untouched by this migration); a second clean relaunch showed all 10 loaders landing between 0.4–1.6s with no blip, confirming the first run's album-RPC spike was cold-connection noise, not a regression. **Charts' total load: 8.7s → 1.58s.** Curl-testing the RPC directly with the anon key first gave a false alarm (`57014` statement timeout) — turned out to be the `anon` role's own tighter statement_timeout, unrelated to the fix; the real test needed a genuine authenticated session, i.e. the app itself.
  - **Add's breakdown showed two stacked issues.** `/api/recommendations` (Vercel) took 4.5s vs. `/api/discovery`'s 0.17s for a comparable call — flagged as a follow-up, not root-caused (server-side TypeScript/DB, different codebase area). Separately, `resolveRecentlyPlayedIfNeeded()` (matching Spotify recently-played tracks against the catalog) ran **sequentially after** `reloadDiscoverySections()` in `DiscoveryViewModel.load()`, adding ~2.8s on top, despite depending on none of that data (only the recently-played lists, already populated by the earlier Spotify/Apple Music loads). **Fixed:** folded into the same concurrent `withTaskGroup` as `reloadDiscoverySections()`. **Verified live with a repeat measurement: 9.1s → 7.35s.**
  - **Taste's 3.4s is almost entirely the `/api/taste/profile` call itself** (2.8s of 3.4s) — same class of problem as `/api/recommendations`, flagged as the same follow-up rather than fixed here.
- **Verify:** all three iOS/DB fixes committed separately (`ec6dcad` Home, `adf20c6` album page, `5e81c22` Charts SQL + Discovery ordering) per no explicit "together" instruction this time; `xcodebuild` clean after each. Both perf fixes are now live-verified: Discovery's ordering fix (9.1s → 7.35s) same day it was written, and the Charts SQL fix (8.7s → 1.58s) after the user applied the migration and asked to close the session — re-measured rather than taking "I applied it" as proof it worked. iOS working tree confirmed clean of all temporary timing instrumentation before each commit (verified via `git diff`/`git status` each time; one accidental full-file `git checkout` briefly wiped the real Discovery fix along with its instrumentation and was caught and redone).

**2026-07-23 (Mac, continued) — Three more direct user reports on iOS, each root-caused and fixed: Explore/Following tab switching, stale Instinct-mode flowers, and drag-rate ignoring precision.**

- **Home tab: switching from Explore to Following subtab did nothing — the liquid-glass bubble at the top moved, the feed underneath didn't.** User reported it had "worked perfectly fine" before, so the first pass looked for a same-session regression: a `git worktree` checkout of the last commit before this session's work reproduced the bug identically, ruling that out. Real root cause: `TabView(.page)`'s `selection` binding doesn't reliably respond to programmatic (button-driven) changes — a genuine, confirmed SwiftUI/platform limitation (gesture-driven swipes work fine; `Button`-triggered state changes don't, regardless of animation wrapping). Three fix attempts failed before finding the real one, each properly tested rather than guessed at: removing the button's `withAnimation` wrapper, deferring the mutation via `DispatchQueue.main.async`, and removing the `TabView`'s own `.animation(value:)` modifier. **Real fix:** replaced `TabView(.page)` in `HomeView.swift` with `ScrollView(.horizontal) + LazyHStack + .scrollTargetLayout() + .scrollTargetBehavior(.paging) + .scrollPosition(id:)` (iOS 17+), which supports genuine two-way state↔view binding. Per explicit instruction to "verify it yourself through taking screenshots before you call it fixed," built a screen-independent verification harness: an `xcodegen`-generated throwaway Xcode project (`ProbeHost` app target + a UI-test bundle) driving the real installed app via `XCUIApplication(bundleIdentifier:)`, with screenshots pulled from the resulting `.xcresult` via `xcrun xcresulttool export attachments`. Confirmed working in both directions. (A `cliclick`/`osascript` coordinate-clicking approach was tried first and abandoned — the user was using the Mac for other work simultaneously, and a Space/window change silently redirected clicks onto an unrelated open project; flagged transparently the moment it happened.)
- **Flower/drag-to-rate buttons kept appearing on the Home tab after switching to Instinct mode**, where they shouldn't exist at all (a direct score doesn't fit the pairwise/Elo model). Root cause: `HomeViewModel` fetched `rating_mode` once at launch and never again, so flipping the setting in Settings didn't invalidate the cached value already driving Home's UI. Fix: `SettingsView.saveRatingMode(_:)` now posts `.sjProfileUpdated` after the DB write; `HomeView` already had listeners for this notification pattern (used elsewhere for Spotify/Apple Music reconnects) and now also calls a new `HomeViewModel.refreshRatingMode()` on it.
- **The drag-to-rate flower ignored the user's manual rating-precision setting** — a half-star user could still drag to a decimal score (e.g. 3.7), even though the same setting was already respected by the tap-to-open precise modal. `RateGaugeGeometry.distanceToScore` was hardcoded to snap to 0.1 regardless of caller. Fixed by adding a `ratingStep` param that the snap rounds to, threaded from `FlowerRateControl` → `AlbumRateButton`'s existing `ratingStep` prop (iOS). **User then explicitly asked for the same fix on web** — web's `FlowerRateControl.tsx`/`AlbumRateButton.tsx` got the analogous change, plus `ratingStep` (from `profile.manual_rating_step`, already centrally available via `useSession()`) threaded through `search/page.tsx` (`SearchResults`, `Discovery`, `AlbumCard`, `SongRow`) and `quick-add/page.tsx` (`CandidateCard`, `SongRow`, `SeeMoreModal`) — the web fix ended up more complete than iOS's, since iOS still has known (flagged, not fixed) gaps: `SongRateButton`/`TrackRatingSheet` and the Home/Search/Rankings `AlbumRateButton` call sites don't fetch or thread a real `manual_rating_step` at all.
- **Verify:** all four fixes committed together in `2acfe15` per explicit "together" instruction (contrasting with the build-11/deadlock work earlier the same day, which was split into separate logical commits). Not re-run through the XCUITest harness after the final commit — the harness itself was torn down (`rm -rf` on its scratchpad project) once the TabView fix was screenshot-confirmed; the Instinct-mode and rating-precision fixes were verified by direct code inspection (cached-value/step-parameter tracing) rather than a fresh simulator pass, since no further interactive session was available.

**2026-07-23 (Windows) — Web Instinct-mode parity: cover buttons, drag-disable, and the album-page score gate.** User reported three Instinct-vs-Manual gaps on web vs the iOS app.

- **Cover rate buttons are now mode-aware (`components/sj/AlbumRateButton.tsx`).** It always rendered the drag-to-rate flower regardless of mode, so in Instinct mode every album cover (feed, charts, artist discography) still showed a flower and let you drag a *manual* quick-score straight into `ratings.score` — bypassing the pairwise flow entirely. Now, when `profile.rating_mode === 'instinct'`, the flower is replaced by a **plus button** that opens `InstinctModal` (drag disabled — ranking is the only path), turning into a **check** after a rank lands, mirroring iOS `AlbumCard` (plus / checkmark). Because `rating_mode` comes from the session profile (Settings' `patch()` calls `refreshProfile()`), the swap is **immediate** on mode change with no reload. The album *detail* page's own cover overlay was already gated out in Instinct mode (`ratingMode !== 'instinct'`) since it has a dedicated inline rating section — left as-is to avoid two rate affordances.
- **Album page now hides the Instinct score below the reveal threshold (`app/(main)/album/[id]/page.tsx`).** The profile already hid un-calibrated Elo scores (`instinctCount >= INSTINCT_REVEAL_THRESHOLD`, =5) but the album page rendered `eloToScore(userElo).toFixed(1)` unconditionally, leaking a meaningless number. Added an `instinctAlbumCount` fetch (count of the user's `ratings` rows with non-null `elo_score`, refreshed inside `loadRatings` so it updates after each rank) and gated: below 5, the score number is hidden and the label becomes the shared **"Rate {n} more to reveal"** string (`sj.instinct.rateMoreToReveal`) — same rule/UX as the profile. Also suppressed the **community histogram's user-bucket highlight** from an un-revealed Elo, which otherwise leaked the hidden score's position. `rerank` / delete affordances unchanged.
- **Follow-up (same session), two more reports:**
  - **Home-tab plus button navigated away + lagged.** The new Instinct plus button in `AlbumRateButton` only stopped the click, not the pointer-down — but on the feed/charts/artist surfaces the cover sits inside a Next `<Link>`, so the press reached the anchor and navigated to the album, tearing down the `InstinctModal` it had just opened (the "lag" was the modal loading opponents while the route changed under it). Added `onPointerDown={(e) => e.stopPropagation()}` to the plus button, matching how `FlowerRateControl` and `AlbumBookmarkButton` already guard against the same wrapping link.
  - **The Add tab is `/search`, not `/quick-add`** (`AppShell` nav: "Add" → `/search`; `/quick-add` is only reachable from the in-page banner). `/search` had a *partial* Instinct implementation — a tap on the flower opened `InstinctModal`, but it still rendered the draggable `FlowerRateControl`, so the user still saw flowers and could **drag a manual score** in Instinct mode.
