Threaded `isInstinct` (from the session profile) through `SearchResults` / `Discovery` → `AlbumCard` / `SongRow`; in Instinct mode the flower is replaced by a plus button (new local `InstinctAddButton`, drag disabled, `onPointerDown` stop-prop so the wrapping album `<Link>` doesn't fire) that opens the pairwise sheet. Session-rated cards keep their existing check.
  - **Also fixed `/quick-add`** (the secondary "Quick Add" page) the same way — it likewise hardcoded `FlowerRateControl` + `ManualRateModal`. Made it mode-aware (`InstinctAddButton` + `openRateTarget()` routing + a session `ranked` set for the plus→check), covering album cards, song rows, and the See-more grid.
  - **Root-caused the real Home-tab bug: `Modal` never portaled.** `components/sj/Modal.tsx` rendered `fixed inset-0 z-50` *inline*, so a Modal opened from inside a feed card (`AlbumRateButton`'s `InstinctModal`, nested in the card's `<Link>`) became a DOM descendant of that `<a>`. Two consequences the user saw: (1) the card's own score badges/title painted **over** the modal (trapped in the card's stacking context), and (2) every click *inside* the modal — a bucket, an opponent, the backdrop — **bubbled to the wrapping `<Link>` and navigated to `/album/[id]`** (the top "loading gauge that never completes", and why the sheet closed itself). Fix: `Modal` now `createPortal`s to `document.body` at `z-[120]` (above the hover-peek `z-90` / bookmark dropdown `z-110`). This lifts *every* modal out of its render location, so card-nested modals escape both the stacking context and the click-bubbling. **Browser-verified** with the browse skill (dev server on **:3001**, not 3000 — 3000 is a different local app): triggered the shared `Modal` via the logged-out like→auth-prompt, confirmed `dialog.parentElement === document.body` and that it renders on top of the dimmed feed with no badges punching through. (Couldn't drive the exact logged-in Instinct plus flow — Supabase session lives in localStorage via OAuth-only login, so the headless browser can't authenticate — but it's the same `Modal`, so the proof transfers.)
  - **Closing the sheet still jumped to the album page — React portals bubble through the *component* tree, not the DOM tree.** The `createPortal` moved the modal DOM to `<body>` (fixing z-index), but a click inside it still bubbles up the React tree — through `AlbumRateButton` → the FeedCard's `<Link>` → Next's navigation. So the backdrop/X/Done click that closed the sheet also navigated. (This is also why *every* in-modal click — buckets, opponents — mis-fired: same portal-bubbling path.) Fix: `Modal`'s portaled root now has `onClick={(e) => e.stopPropagation()}` — the same guard `AlbumBookmarkButton`'s portaled dropdown already uses — so no modal leaks interactions to whatever rendered it. **Browser-verified** (auth modal via logged-out like): opens, content-click keeps it open, backdrop-click and Escape both close it — no behavior regressed, and clicks are now contained.
  - **The top "loading gauge" that persisted after the portal fix = `nextjs-toploader` false-starting** (portal fixed the visual order + modal-internal clicks, but the *initial* plus click still lit the bar). `nextjs-toploader` (`app/layout.tsx`, 2px `#2979B7`) registers a **`document`-level click listener** that walks up from the click target to any `<a>` ancestor and calls `nprogress.start()`. The plus button is inside the FeedCard `<Link>`, so it found the anchor and started the bar; `preventDefault` meant no navigation ever called `done()`, so it filled and hung — opening nothing. A React `onClick` can't stop it: React delegates to `document`, the *same node* toploader listens on, so `stopPropagation`/`stopImmediatePropagation` from a React handler is a coin-flip on listener order. Fix: new hook **`components/sj/useNavSafeClick.ts`** attaches a **native `click` listener directly on the button node** — it fires at the target *before* the event bubbles to `document`, so `stopPropagation` there stops both Next's Link handler and toploader, order-independently, while still opening the sheet. Applied to `AlbumRateButton`'s plus + both `InstinctAddButton` helpers. **Browser-verified the mechanism against the live toploader**: native-on-button stop → `{toploaderBar:false, modalWouldOpen:true}`; plain child-of-anchor click → `startedBar:true`; capture-phase `document` stop kills the bar *but also* the button's own handler (why that route was rejected).
- **Verify:** `tsc --noEmit` clean for all changed files (the one remaining error is the pre-existing untracked `scripts/backfill-rg-covers-kr.ts`, out of scope). **Not browser-verified** — worth a signed-in click-through in both modes: flip Settings → mode and confirm covers swap live on Home + Add tabs; tap a Home-tab cover plus and confirm the sheet opens *without* navigating; rank <5 albums and confirm the album page shows the countdown, not a number.

**2026-07-22 (Windows) — Missing album covers: diagnosed, built + ran a KR-scoped release-group cover backfill (accuracy-verified, --write pass in flight).**

- **Trigger:** user reported Beenzino → "Blurry" shows no cover. Diagnosed: the album **is** in the catalog (`release_groups` `2f5d83c2…`, single, 2019-10-19, `source='musicbrainz'`) but `cover_url` is **null** — Cover Art Archive has no image for its MB release. It's a cover-gap, not a missing album.
- **Sized the class:** **108,766 / 474,940 release groups (22.9%) have null `cover_url`, 100% MusicBrainz-sourced** (iTunes/Deezer bring their own CDN art). User-facing slice = **3,662 KR-artist** groups (rest is deep Western catalog); **0 rated albums lack a cover.**
- **Tooling gap found:** the existing `scripts/backfill-cover-art.ts` writes **`releases.cover_url`**, but every surface (artist page, album page, song rows) reads **`release_groups.cover_url`** — so it never fixed the visible problem.
- **Built `scripts/backfill-rg-covers-kr.ts`** (targets `release_groups`, KR-scoped, null cover). **Defaults to DRY-RUN** (`--write` to commit), resumable, `--limit`/`--types`/`--reset`.
- **The iTunes pivot (the session's real lesson):** the original chain led with iTunes, but Apple **persistently 403-throttles this box's IP** — a 45-min cooldown *and* an overnight rest didn't clear it; the run itself keeps the throttle alive. First real dry-run got only 164/3661 (84% throttled) at ~4 min/row. Empirically tested the other tiers: **Spotify + Last.fm alone recover ~72%, unthrottled and fast.** Reworked the chain to **CAA(group) → Spotify → Last.fm → iTunes(fast-fail, auto-disables after 15 consecutive 403s).** Added **match-validation guards** (Spotify: strict title+artist; Last.fm: artist-match, title relaxed since its getInfo is a direct-lookup that echoes the queried title — needed for romanized-vs-CJK titles) so a wrong-album cover can't slip in.
- **Full dry-run: 2,534 / 3,662 = 69%** (CAA 612 · Spotify 217 · Last.fm 1,705 · iTunes 0 auto-disabled · 1,128 no-match — the misses are correctly OSTs/multi-disc/compilations). **Accuracy spot-check (40 rows): 0 name/artist mismatches; two covers visually confirmed** (the Lemon Tree art literally reads "LEMON TREE / PARK HYE KYUNG" = 박혜경; YooA "Selfish" matched its real art).
- **`--write` pass COMPLETE: 2,482 covers written across the 3,662 (68%), 0 failures** (CAA 617 · Spotify 0 — fully rate-limited this run, Last.fm caught them · Last.fm 1,865 · iTunes 0 auto-disabled). KR null-covers 3,662 → 1,180. Additive/reversible (nulls only).
- **✅ Cover defect (dead Last.fm links) — found AND repaired.** Last.fm's `album.getInfo` returns image URLs that don't always resolve; the script trusted them blind. Swept all 1,864 Last.fm covers with a liveness check (GET + `image/*` content-type): **5 dead (0.3%), nulled** (back to no-cover; a future validated `--write --reset` can re-attempt them). **Beenzino/Blurry** (a 6th, separately) was fixed with its real iTunes art (`Blurry (feat. Dbo)`, visually confirmed) on both the release-group and release rows. **Recurrence prevented:** added `urlIsLive` to `backfill-rg-covers-kr.ts`'s Last.fm tier so it only accepts a URL that actually serves an image. (Sampling had over-estimated at ~16; true rate was 5.) A later `--write --reset` pass would also idempotently re-attempt the 1,180 no-matches + the Spotify-throttled stragglers.
- **Safety-critical fix to the Mac's `cleanup-video-tracks.ts` (data-loss bug found while vetting its dry-run).** The tool diffs stored tracks against a fresh MB re-fetch and deletes anything not in the re-fetched list. But `getReleaseTracks` returns `[]` on a *failed* MB fetch (exhausted 503 retries) identically to a genuinely-empty release — so under MB throttling (the ~33k-release scan 503'd on nearly every batch), any release whose re-fetch failed would flag **every** stored track as "bad" → `--write` would delete real audio + cascade user `track_ratings`. The dry-run's 44,300 "bad rows" was therefore untrustworthy. Fix: new `getReleaseTracksOrNull` (mb-client) returns `null` on fetch failure vs `[]` on empty; the cleanup script now **skips** (never flags) a release when the re-fetch is null or empty, counts `skippedFetchFailed`/`skippedEmpty`, and warns when releases went unverified. `getReleaseTracks` unchanged (`?? []`) so `mb-coverage-gate` is unaffected. Verified: StarRingChild (already cleaned → 0) and two legit multi-disc audio box sets → 0 bad / 0 skipped (no false positives). **The full dry-run must be re-run with the fix before any `--write`.**
- **2nd cleanup fix — discovery rewritten (it kept crashing before the run started).** The candidate-discovery paginated ~849k `release_tracks` rows client-side with OFFSET; deep pages (offset ~800k) exceeded PostgREST's ~8s statement timeout and crashed at ~page 800 — repeatably, even solo (so it wasn't contention with the cover pass, as first assumed). Replaced the entire disc>1 scan + chunked candidate lookup (~1000 queries) with **one server-side Management-API query** (`select distinct r.id, r.mb_release_id … where rt.disc_number>1 …`, ~35s; the Management API tolerates the long DISTINCT scan that PostgREST won't). Requires `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` (present on Windows; Mac would need it or the SQL editor). Verified: fetches all 33,681 candidates in ~35s and the ~10-hr dry-run is now running clean (task launched 2026-07-23). Also cleaned up: a self-matching process watcher (its own cmdline contained the pattern it polled for → never fired) and 6 orphaned `backfill-rg-covers` worker processes left by killed/finished cover runs.
- **Also (small parity fix): web count formatting now floors like iOS.** `lib/sj/display.ts` `formatCount` used `(n/1000).toFixed(1)` which *rounds* (2,967 → "3.0k"); iOS's gauge `formatCount` floors ("2.9k"). Changed web to `Math.floor(n/100)/10` so the charts gauge ("{n} / {target} ratings") and every other count match the app. Its doc comment already claimed "matches iOS formatCount" — now true.

**2026-07-23 (Mac) — App Store build 11 (Guideline 4 + 5.1.1(iv) fixes); Quick Add genre explorer + "Connect Apple Music" in Settings; rating-write error hardening; Profile-tab launch deadlock found + fixed.**

- **App Store build 11.** Guideline 5.1.1(iv): `StepAppleMusic`/`StepNotifications`' pre-permission screens now read "Continue" instead of "Allow X"/"Skip for now" — the real Allow/Don't Allow choice stays in the system dialog. Guideline 4: restored an optional "What's your name?" step (build 10 had deleted it outright after two prior rejections for being a blocking gate) — pre-filled/editable when Sign in with Apple supplies a name, empty-with-placeholder when it doesn't (which is every authorization after an Apple ID's first-ever grant, including every App Review pass reusing the same Apple ID), Continue never gated on its contents; `finish()` falls back to the username on an empty submit. `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION` 10→11. Resubmitted; Apple's reply also flagged nothing new.
- **Quick Add ("Add" tab) — accurate empty state + full genre-explorer port.** A seedless account (no Spotify/Apple Music history, no ratings) was sharing the same "You're all caught up" message as a genuinely-exhausted list — opposite situations, same UI. Now distinguished (`QuickAddViewModel.hasSeed`), with a seedless nudge (search & rate a few albums / connect Spotify or Apple Music, both real actions) and a full port of web's "Explore other genres": same 16-genre list, same `get_quick_add_genre_candidates` RPC, same two-action chip (label opens a shelf, heart likes it → persists to `profiles.preferred_genres`, the column the home feed's category resolver already reads). One simplification vs. web: a single ~20-item page per genre, not web's shelf/"See more" pagination, since iOS Quick Add is a flat list.
- **Settings → Connected Accounts gained a real "Connect Apple Music" row**, independent of the social-login section (MusicKit authorization is a device permission, not a Supabase-linked identity). Handles not-yet-asked (in-app request) and denied/restricted (deep-link to the Settings app, since iOS won't re-prompt once denied) — previously there was no way to grant this after skipping it once in onboarding. `DiscoveryViewModel` gained `refreshAppleMusicIfNeeded()`, wired to the same scenePhase-active hook + a new `.sjAppleMusicAuthorized` notification, mirroring the existing Spotify-reconnect pattern.
- **Rating-write error handling.** `AlbumQuickRate.saveManualScore`/`saveManualTrackScore` (`AlbumContextMenu.swift`) used `try?`, silently swallowing any failure while still posting `.ratingChanged` unconditionally — every listener got told to refresh for a write that never happened. Both now do a real `do/catch`, log the failure, return success/failure, and only post the notification on success; `QuickAddViewModel.rate()`/`rateSong()` roll the optimistic held score back on failure with a new user-facing alert instead of failing invisibly.
- **Profile-tab "slow to load" investigation → found a real launch deadlock, not a performance issue.** User reported Profile felt slow on cold launch. A temporary timing probe (added, tested on-sim via the `run` skill, reverted) showed `MainTabView`'s own `.task` eagerly preloading Home + Charts + Discovery + Quest **all at once regardless of which tab is selected** — Charts finished at +8.67s, Discovery at +11.56s, and Profile's own `.task` (lazy, loads only when its tab first appears — the only one of the five that already worked this way) couldn't even start until ~11.6s in, despite its own 5 queries taking just 0.65s once given a clear runway. **Fix:** removed `chartsVM.load()`/`discoveryVM.load()` from that eager task group — `ChartsView`/`SearchView` already have their own `.task { await viewModel.load() }` guarded by the same `hasLoaded` flag Profile uses, so they now load lazily on first visit for free. Home stays eager (it's the landing tab); Quest stays eager (`shouldOfferBadgeRedeem` needs to be known at launch regardless of tab, and it was never a meaningful contributor — fastest of the four).
- **That fix then broke launch entirely — caught via user report, not verification, and this is the more important bug of the two.** `MainTabView`'s body gated the *entire app UI* on `homeVM.isLoading || chartsVM.isLoading || discoveryVM.isLoading` — a leftover from before the lazy-load change. Once Charts/Discovery stopped being preloaded, their `isLoading` flags never flip to `false` (nothing calls `.load()` on them anymore until their tab renders), but their tab can never render because this exact condition is still blocking the whole `TabView` from appearing at all. A genuine deadlock: the tabs need to be visible to load, and loading is what was supposed to make them visible — no code path could ever break it. Diagnosed the hard way: two rounds of dead ends (a `emitLocalSessionAsInitialSession` auth change tried and reverted after it introduced its own regression; network/Wi-Fi/VPN/Docker/Simulator all suspected and ruled out one by one) before ticker-based debug logging (added across the auth path, `MainTabView`, and `HomeViewModel`, all since removed) proved auth + Home + Quest completed in under 3 seconds every time, yet the screen never moved — pointing straight at the gating condition itself. **Fix:** the splash now only waits on `homeVM.isLoading`, since Home is the only view model still eagerly loaded at launch. Confirmed fixed live.
- **Verification:** `xcodebuild` clean after every step. The deadlock fix specifically was confirmed against real repeated user testing (cold launches, Spotify sign-in, a real iPhone), not just a clean build — this is the one that actually mattered.

**2026-07-22 (Mac, session 2) — Drag-to-rate workflow migrated from web to the entire iOS app + OKLCh color-ramp parity closed.**

- **The ask:** migrate web's drag-to-rate gauge (`FlowerRateControl.tsx` — press a flower button and drag outward, distance maps to a 0.1-step score in a live circular gauge, tap-with-no-drag opens the precise modal instead) to iOS, on every surface web has it. Scoped via a clarifying round: everything, decide the technical approach, port the color ramp too, cover both albums and songs.
- **The blocking design question:** web's gauge escapes any container's clipping via `createPortal(document.body)` — SwiftUI has no equivalent within a single view tree. Chose the more faithful option: a floating `UIWindow` (`RateGaugeOverlay.swift`) sitting above everything (including sheets, `.alert + 1` level), non-interactive so the real button underneath keeps owning the actual `DragGesture`. This is what lets the gauge render un-clipped regardless of which List/ScrollView/horizontal-shelf the rate button sits inside.
- **Core new files:** `FlowerRateControl.swift` (the gesture + `Canvas`-drawn gauge — dead zone, whole-star tick marks, dotted baseline, a fading arc centred on the drag angle; geometry constants ported verbatim from web since they were already touch-tuned there), `FlowerGlyph.swift`, `AlbumRateButton.swift` / `SongRateButton.swift` (self-contained data wrappers mirroring web's `AlbumRateButton.tsx` — own their `ratings`/`track_ratings` upsert-or-delete, open `ManualRatingSheet`/`TrackRatingSheet` as the tap fallback, support both an uncontrolled `initialScore` and a controlled `externalScore: Binding<Double?>` for pages that render a second rating surface for the same release, e.g. the album hero alongside its full rating section).
- **OKLCh ramp ported into `ScoreSpectrum`** (`ScoreBadge.swift`) — replaced the old HSL hue-sweep (a parity gap flagged back on 2026-07-17 when web moved off it) with the same perceptually-uniform deep-red→orange→yellow-green→teal→blue ramp, matched stop-for-stop (hue/chroma anchors, OKLab→linear-sRGB matrices, gamut-fit-by-chroma-reduction). Purely internal to the enum — every existing `ScoreBadge` call site picks up the new colors automatically, zero other changes needed.
- **Wired into every web surface:** album hero (cover-overlaid, `externalScore` bound to the page's own `viewModel.userScore` so the two rating surfaces never drift), song hero (same pattern), home feed post cards (`FeedCard`), search (album grid + song rows), Quick Add (**replaced the linear `HalfStarRow` outright** — matches web's own prior migration off a similar linear control onto this exact gauge), the artist page's album list (overlaid on the row's small cover, alongside — not replacing — the existing trailing score chip, matching web's actual layout once double-checked against the real source), and ranking charts' big grid cards ("Most Rated" albums, "Top/Most Rated" songs).
- **Instinct-mode gating.** A direct score doesn't fit the Instinct/Elo rating model, so every wiring point needed to know the *viewer's* `rating_mode` before rendering the button. Screens that already tracked it (`AlbumDetailView`, `SongDetailView`, `SearchView`, `ArtistPageView`) just threaded it through; `HomeViewModel` and `ChartsViewModel` had zero prior rating-mode awareness and got a new lightweight `profiles.rating_mode` fetch added alongside their existing personalization/load queries. Quick Add was left ungated (confirmed it has no Instinct branching anywhere already — implicitly manual-only by design).
- **Scope calls made, not silently:**
  - Skipped the small dense-list ranking rows (leaderboard podium/ranked-list rows, chart-detail rows — all reuse the same `RankedListRow`/`TrendingRow`-style components with ~38–82pt covers): too small for a usable drag target to add blind, without a live device to check against. Those rows still navigate to the album page to rate.
  - Search's "song rows" quick-rate the *parent album*, not the individual recording — traced this back to existing `addRelease(pr)`/`ratedReleaseIds` wiring that was already album-keyed before this session; preserved rather than silently switching to true per-song `track_ratings` writes.
- **One real mid-build mistake, caught and fixed:** initially wired a `ratingMode` fetch into `RankingDetailView` (the Silla leaderboard) assuming it owned the `HorizAlbumCard` call site — it doesn't (that call site actually lives in the separate, reusable `ChartHorizSection`, which `RankingDetailView` doesn't even use). Build failed on the undefined property; traced the actual struct ownership and fixed both sides — `ChartHorizSection` got its own `ratingMode` param wired from `ChartsViewModel`, and `RankingDetailView`'s already-correct fetch was left in place (harmless, just not yet connected to a UI element — a natural hook if its own `PodiumRow`/`RankedListRow` ever gets the same treatment later).
- **Verification:** `xcodebuild` clean after every incremental step (built ~10 times through the session, not just once at the end) and once more at the very end across the whole diff. **Not interactively tested** — no simulator/device session available to actually confirm gesture feel, the floating window's real screen alignment, or that a nested `DragGesture` inside a `NavigationLink`'s label correctly wins touch priority over the link's own tap (this relies on SwiftUI's standard, well-established but here-untested "child gesture wins over ancestor" behavior).

**2026-07-22 (Mac → Windows) — StarRingChild EP duplicate-track investigation → MusicBrainz video-track ingest bug fixed + cleanup tool; song-level Mix support shipped; several iOS UI fixes.**

- **The bug:** user spotted a duplicate "StarRingChild" track on Aimer's *StarRingChild EP* — investigated by pulling the release group/release/release_tracks/recordings chain directly, then fetching the same release from the live MusicBrainz API (`inc=recordings+media`) to compare. MB's data was fine: the release has two media, a CD (6 audio tracks) and a bonus DVD-Video containing the music video for the title track, with `recording.video: true`. Our ingest (`scripts/mb-client.ts`'s `parseMedia()`) flattened every medium into one track list with **no filter on the `video` flag**, so the DVD's music-video recording got written into `recordings`/`release_tracks` exactly like a song — and because its underlying MB recording title happened to be the bare "StarRingChild" (no disambiguating suffix), it rendered as an exact duplicate.
- **Scope, sized empirically (not guessed):** ~33,408 releases (of 471k) have a track on disc ≥2 at all. Sampling showed the overwhelming majority of "disc 2" content is video/bonus material (Music Video / Dance Shot / OPENING / Close-up / making-of clips, not real audio) mislabeled as songs — most just happen to carry a disambiguating suffix in their title so they don't look like an *exact* duplicate the way StarRingChild did. Extrapolating the sample: roughly ~65-70 releases show the precise bare-duplicate-title symptom; a much larger population (most of the ~33k "has disc 2" releases) has some video content quietly sitting in its tracklist as a fake song.
- **Fix (future ingests):** `parseMedia()` now skips any track whose `recording.video === true`. `mb-ingest.ts`'s `ingestEditionFromPrefetched` also switched `total_tracks` from MB's raw (video-inclusive) media count to the filtered track list's length, so the stored count stays consistent with what's actually in `release_tracks`. Verified live: re-fetching the StarRingChild release now returns exactly the 6 real audio tracks.
- **Cleanup tool for existing bad data:** new `scripts/cleanup-video-tracks.ts` (dry-run by default; `--write` to delete; `--start-after <release-id>` to resume a long run; `--only <release-id>` to spot-check one release without the full scan). For each MB-sourced release with a disc-≥2 track, it re-fetches the release from MB (now video-filtered) and diffs against what's stored — anything no longer in the corrected list gets deleted (`release_tracks` row, then the `recordings` row too if no other release still references it, which cascades to any `track_ratings` — the dry-run reports how many ratings are at risk before anything is touched), and `releases.total_tracks` gets corrected. Verified end-to-end with `--write --only` against the real StarRingChild release: duplicate gone, `total_tracks` corrected to 6, and the shared video `recordings` row was correctly *not* deleted because it's also referenced by a second release ("BEST SELECTION 'noir'", same bug, not yet cleaned).
- **Full catalog-wide dry-run**: kicked off on the Mac against all 33,681 candidates. MusicBrainz's ~1 req/s rate limit (not local hardware) is the throughput ceiling — throttling (`503`s) made the pace ~2s/release rather than the theoretical 1.1s. Ran for ~10 hours (to 18,700/33,681 checked, 54%, 24,743 bad rows flagged) before the Mac needed to shut down. **Moved to Windows to finish**, resuming via `--start-after <last-checked-id>` rather than restarting from 0 (the candidate list is id-sorted, so this genuinely skips already-checked releases). **`--write` has not been run anywhere** — the user needs to review the complete dry-run report and explicitly confirm before any deletion happens; this is real, irreversible production data.
- **Song-level Mix support (separate, user-requested feature).** Built while testing an unrelated iOS UI request ("apply the same UI for songs" — Share-in-navbar / bookmark-in-hero, mirroring the album page) surfaced that songs had no mix membership anywhere in the app at all. User chose to build it out rather than skip it. New migration `20260722000000_mix_song_items.sql`: `mix_song_items` table mirroring `mix_items` but keyed on `recording_id`, plus `release_group_id` (carried alongside so a song row always has a cover/title/artist to show without disambiguating which of a recording's possibly-multiple releases to use — the same context `SongDetailView` already requires), RLS mirroring `mix_items`'s policies, and `get_profile_mixes`'s `item_count` updated to sum both tables (a mix holding only songs was undercounting to 0 otherwise). **Applied to the hosted DB** (verified: `mix_song_items` exists, `get_profile_mixes` callable). iOS: new `SongMixPickerView` (song counterpart to `MixPickerView`), `MixDetailView` now fetches+renders a songs section (small "Song" badge, swipe-to-delete, navigates to `SongDetailView` via a new `selectedSongItem` item-binding), profile Mixes tab item counts include songs. Added `Hashable` to `MixRelease`/`MixSongRecording`/`NativeArtistRef` (needed for the new item-based navigation; purely additive). `xcodebuild` clean after every change; **not interactively tested** (no simulator/device driving available in this session — worth a manual add-a-song-to-a-mix pass).
- **Known gap, deliberately not fixed:** `get_mix_covers` (stacked-cover visual in the mix share composer) still only reads `mix_items` — a mix with only songs shows no covers there. Resolving "the" cover for a song (when its recording could theoretically span multiple releases) needs more design than this session had room for.
- **Smaller iOS fixes, same session:**
  - Post card sizing reduced twice per user feedback (iterating "not so dramatically" → "slightly more"): cover 80→72→66pt, title 17→15→14pt, artist/type line 14→12.5→11.5pt, across `FeedCard`/`ProfilePostCard`/`ProfileSongPostCard` (kept in sync per an earlier session's consistency fix).
  - Artist page hero's 3-stat row (community avg / ratings / releases) had `HStack(spacing: 0)` with full-width left-aligned columns — the divider had zero gap on the side facing the *next* column's number, so "ratings"/"releases" sat flush against the line. Fixed to `spacing: 12`, matching the spacing convention already used by the page's other stat row (`coverageView`, `spacing: 20`).
  - Post overflow menu's "Add to Mix" used `plus.square`; web uses a bookmark icon for the same action everywhere (2026-07-19's #17 change). Fixed the post-menu instance (`HomeView.swift`) to `bookmark`. **Not fixed:** the identical inconsistency in `AlbumContextMenu.swift`'s long-press quick-action menu (still `text.badge.plus`) — out of the scoped ask ("post"), flagged for a follow-up.
  - Album + song page hero iterated per user direction: first added Share + bookmark inline in the hero, then repositioned — **Share moved to the nav bar** (`ToolbarItem(.topBarTrailing)`, next to the title) and **bookmark moved to the hero's top-right corner** (trailing edge, top-aligned with the cover/title via `HStack(alignment: .top)` + `Spacer()`). Song page's hero got the identical treatment once song-mix support existed to back it.
- **Everything committed + pushed** (`dfe2cfc`) before the Mac shutdown — this was necessary specifically so Windows could pull the work to resume the cleanup scan; not routine mid-session practice.

**2026-07-20 (Mac, session 2) — App Store rejected the Guideline 4 fix a second time; real root cause found; name step removed entirely; build 10.** Apple rejected build 9 (reviewed on iPad Air 11-inch, submission from 2026-07-18's fix) with the same Guideline 4 text, now saying "name and/or email address." Investigated rather than re-guessing: confirmed there's no separate email-input field anywhere in onboarding (grepped) and no re-ask of data already supplied via OAuth scopes — the "and/or email" is Apple's standard boilerplate for this guideline class, not a second literal violation. The real bug: the 2026-07-18 fix (`OnboardingView.init`) only omitted the `.name` step *when the current authorization's metadata happened to include a name* — but **Apple only ever sends the user's name on an Apple ID's first-ever authorization to a given app**; every authorization after that (including App Review reusing the same Apple ID across build 6/7/8/9's review rounds) comes back with no name at all. So the "conditional skip" degraded to "show it basically always" in practice — and `StepName`'s Continue button was disabled until text was typed (`canContinue = !displayName.isEmpty`), making it a genuine **mandatory** field, which is exactly what Apple's guideline forbids regardless of whether this particular authorization happened to supply the data. Fix: removed `.name` from `Step` and from `OnboardingView.init`'s step list **unconditionally, for every provider** — there is no code path left that can ever show a name-gate screen. `StepName`/`StepProfile.swift` deleted outright (was its only remaining use); its two now-orphaned strings ("What's your name?" / "Your name") removed from the string catalog. The pre-fill logic (silently seeding `data.displayName` from provider metadata when present) stays — still used by `finish()` when building the profile row, still editable afterward in Edit Profile, just never a blocking UI step. `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION` 9→10 (7/8/9 all already consumed by prior uploads). Verified on-sim via a temp probe presenting `OnboardingView(provider: "apple")` directly (same entry point App Review hits): opens straight to "Create a username." with 4 progress dots, confirming the gate can't resurface no matter what the Apple credential contains this time. Probe reverted, build clean. **Lesson for future App Review debugging:** when a fix that looked logically correct gets rejected again for the same reason, don't assume incomplete testing — check whether the *external system's own behavior differs on repeat interactions* (here: Apple only grants PII once per Apple-ID/app pair, ever). "Skip when we have it" and "never require it" are different specs, and only the second one is what Apple's guideline actually says.

**2026-07-20 (Windows) — Taste page rebuilt (#6 + #7): the 18-item web change list is complete.**

- **#6 — the Taste graph is an interactive map now** (new `components/sj/TasteGraph.tsx`; the page's old `TasteTerritory` + `packCircles` deleted). Key calls:
  - **No second taste model.** The map draws the *same clusters the report computes* — a world is a bubble, its tags are the sub-genre bubbles inside it. That's what made #6 a payload extension instead of new taxonomy.
  - **Area ∝ share** (`r ∝ √share`, numerically verified: 7× share → 7× area), **colour = the app's OKLCh score ramp** (`spectrumColor`, same meaning as every score badge; direct labels + panel + 1→5 legend keep it from being the sole encoding), **position = embedding similarity**: a deterministic stress-relaxation over small cosine matrices shipped in the payload (`sim`, `tagSim`), so similar worlds sit adjacent, nothing overlaps, the 300-dim vectors stay server-side, and — because the init is index-based with no RNG — **a refresh never reshuffles the user's map**.
  - **Prezi zoom = one CSS transform on one `<g>`** (760ms ease, `willChange`), bubble drift = per-bubble CSS keyframes; no per-frame React renders, so it stays smooth on a phone, and both animations die under `prefers-reduced-motion`. Escape / Back pill walk out one level. Sub-genre layouts are scaled by the furthest bubble *edge* (not the box half-width — a corner sits 1.41× further out) so children never poke through their parent.
  - **Side panel**: focus's era/scene note + average, the user's rated albums in the focus (client-filtered from a new `graph.albums` array, ≤400 score-descending — no per-click fetch), and recs. Unfocused, it's a clickable world legend.
  - **Year histogram + trend** (`YearHistogram`): per-year bars from new `charts.years` (contiguous, zero-filled) with a centred 5-year moving average whose window shrinks at the edges. One measure, one axis — the line is the same series smoothed.
- **#7 — Refresh + report reconstruction, same payload.** Header **Refresh** button (`FlowerSpinner` + "Recomputing…") is the **only** caller of `?refresh=1`; the default load stays cached per the 2026-07-15 Vercel-CPU rule, restated in the page's doc comment. A failed refresh keeps the on-screen report. Cache key **v4 → v5**; everything new is **additive** (`charts.years`, `graph.{worlds,albums,recs}`). Report reordered header → map → numbers → years → scores → scenes/canon → community → dislikes; the five per-world cards folded into the map's panel (their i18n keys left in place, unused). Vector math stayed in Node; the route still upserts `user_taste_profiles` for iOS.
- **Recs**: one prestige pool per leading world (same query shape as `/api/recommendations`, reused rather than called — that route `strip()`s `genres` so its output can't be filtered to a sub-genre), one album per artist, already-rated excluded server-side, keyed `world:{i}` / `tag:{tag}`. ⚠ 3 extra index-backed `release_groups` queries on uncached loads; worlds past the third just show the user's own ratings. All queries capture `error` (#10's lesson).
- **#12 closed for real:** `taste/page.tsx`'s hand-rolled `animate-pulse` became a layout-matched skeleton; `grep -rn "animate-pulse" app components` now hits only `Loading.tsx`.
- ⚠ **New intentional parity gap:** iOS `TasteReportView` (2026-07-18) still renders the v4 report with the old territory pack; additive payload keeps it working until a Mac session ports the map.
- **Verification:** `tsc --noEmit`, `next lint` (pre-existing `<img>` warnings only), `next build` clean; layout math sanity-checked in Node (area ratios, no overlap, in-box, similar-nearer-than-dissimilar, camera transforms). **Not browser-verified** — the whole 2026-07-19/20 batch now needs one signed-in click-through; ordered checklist at the bottom of `WEB_CHANGE_PROMPTS.md`.

**2026-07-19 (Windows, pt 4) — web change list finished except the Taste page: #8, #13, #14, and #12's rollout.** The 18-item list in `WEB_CHANGE_PROMPTS.md` is now 16/18; #6 + #7 (Taste graph rebuild + report refresh) are the only remainder and are deliberately one session, with a paste-ready prompt appended to that file.

- **#8 — Profile Rated tab: `list` and `table` were the same rows twice.** `table` was `hidden md:` *and* gated on `isSelf`, so it was unreachable on a phone or on anyone else's profile, and the two modes each owned a different sort model — switching modes silently threw your sort away. Merged into one responsive grid in a new `components/sj/ProfileRatedList.tsx`: below `md` it's the old list (cover, title + type chip, artist, score chip), from `md` up the same fields become the table's sortable **Title · Artist · Type · Score · Date** columns, one shared `GRID` template keeping header and rows aligned without a `<table>`. **One sort model** (`sortCol` + `sortDesc` in `ProfileView`) with the headers and the `<select>` as two controls over it, so ordering survives a resize; the select names the six orderings worth naming. **Filters** (`RatedFilterBar`): kind pills widened from All/Albums/Songs to **All · Albums · EPs · Singles · Songs**, empty buckets hidden, plus a score-range disclosure with two 0–5 sliders that clamp each other — and they apply to the **posts** mode too, since a filter describes which ratings you're looking at, not how they're drawn. `posts` stays a separate mode (review text/likes/comments is a genuinely different presentation), so the toggle is two buttons with no dead third. CSV export survived the merge (folding the table in would have deleted it) and now exports the *filtered* rows. Row links are one stretched `absolute inset-0` anchor rather than an anchor wrapping grid cells — the latter needs `display: contents`, which hit-tests unreliably. `RatedTable` deleted; `ProfileExtras.tsx` is now just `SavedLibrary`. **⚠ The profile still loads only the most recent 60 album + 60 song ratings**, so every filter/sort operates on that window — unchanged, but far more noticeable now that filtering exists.
- **#13 — Quick Add rebuilt as horizontal shelves, one per seed artist.** The page was one flat prestige-ordered infinite list, which buries every artist after the first; but "do I remember this record" is a question you answer *per artist*. Each shelf is its own `get_quick_add_candidates` call with a **single-name** `p_artist_names` array — **no new SQL** — so the RPC's prestige ordering and both exclusions (rated / not-interested) apply per shelf, and each resolves independently so a slow artist never blocks the rest. New `components/sj/CandidateRow.tsx`: paging arrows driven by **measured overflow** (`ResizeObserver` for the scroller + a `MutationObserver` because children arriving changes `scrollWidth` without resizing anything), **hidden rather than disabled** at each end, revealed on row hover / keyboard focus, positioned outside the scroller so they never cover a cover; native swipe stays on with `snap-x`. **"See more"** opens a modal grid paging 24 at a time through the same RPC at a higher offset, keeping its **own** array — paging 100 deep shouldn't leave a 100-card horizontal scroller behind on the page. Infinite scroll now reveals four more *shelves* at a time instead of appending albums. Cards carry the full album vocabulary (AlbumPeek → hover peek + right-click menu free, bookmark, overflow menu, always-visible rate gauge). **Songs mode deliberately keeps the flat vertical list** — a shelf per artist of near-identical text rows buys nothing and songs get rated in runs.
- **#14 — "Explore other genres", the outward half of Quick Add.** Its seeds are the user's own Spotify history and own high ratings, so the page structurally could never show them something new. New migration **`20260719000001_quick_add_genre_candidates.sql`** (applied via `scripts/db-exec.ts` and verified live — 20 rows for `jazz`): `get_quick_add_genre_candidates(p_user_id, p_genre, p_lim, p_offset)`, the same body as the artist RPC with the seed predicate swapped for the charts' `_rg_primary_matches` (primary_genre first, raw `genres` array as fallback) and the same two exclusions; **identical return columns** is what lets a genre shelf and an artist shelf render through one code path. Ordering is prestige-first — there's no seed order inside a genre, and an unfamiliar genre is only ratable from memory if it leads with its best-known records. The chip UI keeps two actions apart on purpose: tapping the label **opens** a shelf (a look), tapping the heart **likes** the genre (a preference) — browsing jazz once shouldn't claim you like jazz. Likes persist to **`profiles.preferred_genres`**, the existing comma-separated column `lib/category-resolver.ts` already reads for the homepage rows, so a like reshapes home too; a failed write rolls the toggle back rather than lying about what's saved. ⚠ That column is shared with (older) onboarding labels — the picker only reads back values it recognises, so an unknown old value survives in the column but doesn't show as liked. ⚠ The seedless state (brand-new account) now falls through to the genre explorer instead of a dead end; least-exercised path, worth a look.
- **#12 — rollout finished.** Converted to `Loading.tsx` primitives: charts (albums pane, songs pane, the ranking `<ol>`), notifications, home `FeedSkeleton`, `post/[id]`, search, `song/[id]`, `HeaderMenus`' notification popover, `AlbumPeek`'s stats line + tracklist, `ProfileView` (page load, Mixes tab, `FollowListModal`), `ProfileExtras`' `SavedLibrary`, and Quick Add wholesale. **`taste/page.tsx:122` deliberately left** — #6 rewrites that page, so converting it now is churn; it's called out in the next-session prompt. `grep -rn "animate-pulse" app components` returns only `Loading.tsx` and `taste/page.tsx`.
- **Verification:** `tsc --noEmit`, `next lint` (only the pre-existing `<img>` warnings) and `next build` all clean. **Nothing browser-verified** — same standing caveat as the rest of 2026-07-19.

**2026-07-19 (Windows) — KR discovery drain complete + thin-artist resolver (the hard residual):**

- **The 2026-07-16 discovery gap is closed.** The 11,750 `source='mbid'` KR artists queued via `discover:area` fully drained over ~2 days: **KR artists ~1,293 → 12,413** (near-complete vs MB's ~12,533 usable), total artists **67,518**, `release_groups` **474,940** (513 `source='itunes'` from the backfill/recency lanes, incl. P-Type's 3). Queue down to ~46 pending; `fail` held at 8 the entire run (no new failures). Pipeline + backfill sweep both stopped by the user after the 2-day drain — restart cleanly (startup `resetStale` flips the 4 mid-flight `processing` rows back to pending).
- **Built `resolve-thin-artists.ts` (dry-run reporter) for the empty/thin-MB residual** (아카네 리제 / Minsu — MB knows them as 0–1 releases, so the backfill's ≥2-owned-overlap identity gate can't touch them). Safer signal = **ANCHOR CORROBORATION**: the artist's full MB footprint (incl. the feature credits the composition filter drops) are anchors; an iTunes SONG search by native name corroborates identity when a distinctive anchor title shows up, and the solo iTunes artistId is the clean-single-credit id **named inside that anchor's collab credit** ("Akane Lize" ⊂ "Airi Kanna & Akane Lize"). Generic-name guard (short single-token CJK name → needs ≥2 anchors) + no-native-name → abstain. **Validated:** 아카네 리제 → **CONFIRM** (iTunes 1738889565, 3 albums, anchor "Hit on Shot"); Minsu → **NONE** (safe — romanized generic name, nothing distinctive to anchor on). Report-only; **the CONFIRM→ingest step is NOT built yet** (next: prove on 아카네 리제 like P-Type, then a breadth sample to size the win + catch false-CONFIRMs, best run when the iTunes-hammering backfill sweep isn't competing for the IP).
- **Merged origin (16 commits, iOS/web UI)** cleanly — only README/SESSIONS conflicted (both devices appended notes), resolved keeping all content.
- **Session-close open items** (also in README START HERE): (1) 아카네 리제 live-ingest + wire the thin-resolver CONFIRM→ingest path; (2) on next pipeline restart the new **`area` lane** activates (first sweep ~2026-07-24) and **drop `RECENCY=0`** so the recency lane resumes; (3) the pre-existing systemic catalog-quality gaps further down.

**2026-07-18 (Mac, session 6) — two archive/upload warnings triaged; ITMS-90892 fixed, build bumped to 8.** (1) "Upload symbols failed … dSYM for Sentry.framework": non-blocking — sentry-cocoa 8.58.3 ships as a prebuilt binary via SPM, so its dSYM can't be in the archive; only Sentry-internal crash frames lose symbolication in Organizer/ASC. Ignored for now (fix later = drop the release's published dSYM into the archive, or sentry-cli upload for Sentry-side symbolication). (2) **ITMS-90892 "missing 152×152 alternate icon for iPad" — real gap, fixed**: the 7 alternate icons were iPhone-only loose PNGs (120/180px) and Info.plist had no `CFBundleIcons~ipad`, which also means alternate icons never resolved on iPad at all. Generated `AppIcon-*@2x~ipad.png` (152×152, sips-downscaled from the 180px @3x) for all 7 + added the mirrored `CFBundleIcons~ipad` dict. Verified: plutil lint OK, built bundle contains the ~ipad PNGs and the key. `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION` 7→8 (build 7 was already delivered). **Follow-up:** the user had already burned build 8 on an intermediate upload, so App Store Connect rejected the next archive as a "Redundant Binary Upload" — bumped again to **9** (lesson: every upload permanently consumes its build number, even unsubmitted ones; bump before every archive). Also created `INTERVIEW_PREP.md` (+ .docx/.pdf via pandoc + headless Chrome) — personal interview-prep material, deliberately left untracked, not committed.

**2026-07-18 (Mac, session 5) — artist-page RPC root-caused (needs SQL-editor paste) + cover fetch parallelism.** User: artist page still "extremely slow", covers slow in general, "are we using lower resolution?" **(1) RPC:** timed `get_artist_release_groups` live — ~0.8s warm / ~1.8s cold for 12 rows. Root cause in the SQL: `WHERE rg.primary_artist_id = $1 OR EXISTS (SELECT … release_group_artists …)` — the OR defeats `idx_release_groups_artist`, forcing a full scan of ~413k release_groups with a per-row EXISTS probe. Rewrote as a UNION of two index-driven branches (**migration `20260718000000_artist_releases_rpc_union.sql`, ⏳ NOT APPLIED — this Mac still has no `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN`, user must paste into the SQL editor**; also fixes the ordering to newest-first — the old DISTINCT ON form was id-ordered). **(2) Covers:** traced the CAA chain fully — it's **3 serial hops** (coverartarchive.org 307 ~0.5s → archive.org/download 302 ~0.6s → ia-node bytes ~0.5s), and 99% of sampled cover_urls are CAA. Resolution is NOT the issue (we already request 250px CAA / 300px iTunes thumbs, ~10KB payloads) — it's pure redirect latency, unfixable client-side for first-ever fetches (server-side cover mirroring/proxy remains the real fix, parked for pipeline). What IS fixable: parallelism — `ImageCache` gained a **dedicated URLSession** (`httpMaximumConnectionsPerHost` 6→12, system URLCache disabled since the class owns its own disk tier and redirects are uncacheable anyway) used by both CachedImage and prefetch, and prefetch's barriered batches-of-6 became a **worker pool of 8 pulling in visible-first order** — one slow redirect chain no longer stalls the other lanes (60 cold covers: ~16s → ~7s expected). Build clean; RPC speedup verifiable by re-timing after the user applies the migration.

**2026-07-18 (Mac, session 4) — four user-reported polish/perf issues fixed (Charts bubble, cover loading, Artist page, Profile cold open).**

- **Charts Albums/Songs toggle now has the Home-style Liquid Glass bubble** (`RankingsView.chartTabButton`): same `.glassEffect(.regular, in: Capsule())` + `matchedGeometryEffect` sliding capsule as Home's Explore/Following, closing the inconsistency flagged 2026-07-16. Spacing 28→4 to match (buttons now carry their own capsule padding). Verified on-sim.
- **Cover loading root-caused + two-tier image cache built.** Measured live: CAA covers (~95% of the catalog) 307-redirect to archive.org taking **~1.6s per image**, return **no Cache-Control**, and the redirect itself is never HTTP-cacheable — so every app relaunch re-paid the redirect hop per cover even when URLCache had the bytes (the NSCache memory tier dies with the process). `ImageCache` gained a **persistent disk tier** (Caches/CoverImages, SHA256-of-URL filenames, original encoded bytes, pruned oldest-quarter past 4,000 files ≈ ≤100MB): `image(for:)` = memory→disk(→promote), `store()` writes both, `CachedImage` checks disk before network. **Prefetch rewritten**: the 60-URL cap and unbounded concurrency are gone — deduped, order-preserving (first-listed = first-visible sections land first), batches of 6, disk-aware. Verified on-sim: after one Add-tab visit, a full app relaunch renders every cover instantly (zero shimmers). First-ever fetches still pay CAA's ~1.6s (server-side fix = the parked cover-proxy/side-table resolver).
- **Artist page: false "0 releases" + slow render fixed** (`ArtistPageView`). Three real bugs: (1) the header stat rendered the live `releases.count` — i.e. **"0 releases"** — during the whole fetch (now "—" while loading); (2) a failed/timed-out releases RPC was `try? ?? []`-coalesced into a genuinely-empty artist (now Optional + one retry + a `loadFailed` state with a Retry button instead of lying); (3) `isLoading` stayed true through the follow-up all-ratings fetch, so releases sat hidden long after they'd arrived — now the page renders the moment releases land, community scores/my-ratings stream in after, and covers prefetch immediately.
- **Profile cold open parallelized** (`ProfileViewModel.load()`): was ~12 strictly serial round-trips with `isLoading = false` only at the very end. Now five independent chains (profile row / album ratings / song-ratings 2-step chain / follow counts / mix shares) all start concurrently via `async let` (extracted into typed fetch helpers), the page renders as soon as profile+ratings are in, and social counts (now 3-way concurrent themselves), songs, follows, and mixes stream in behind. Cover prefetch added for both ratings and song grids. Verified on-sim: full profile renders correctly (48 rated, counts, post cards) after the restructure.

**2026-07-18 (Mac, session 3) — Home header jammed into the status bar on device, fixed.** User report: Explore/Following/bell "suddenly shifted way too upwards" on their iPhone; sim screenshot showed it correct, which was the tell. Root cause: the 2026-07-16 Liquid Glass rework has `feedContent.ignoresSafeArea(edges: .top)` compensated by a `topSafeAreaInset` captured in a **one-shot** GeometryReader `onAppear` — on device the first layout pass can report inset 0 (insets propagate a frame late), and the one-shot froze the 0 → header padding collapsed to 12pt at the physical top. Intermittent by nature ("suddenly"). Fix (HomeView.swift): the capture now also has `.onChange(of: geo.safeAreaInsets.top)` so the real value lands whenever it arrives. Sim verified unchanged in the good case; the race itself isn't reproducible from here — user to confirm on device across a few launches.

**2026-07-18 (Mac, session 2) — iOS web-parity before resubmission: Taste page rebuilt as the chart report + progressive search.** User: "implement what has been done in the web but not in the app. namely, the taste page renovation and the search engine change." First had to `git pull` — the Windows 2026-07-17 batch (`57103de` + 4 more: drag-gauge, search relevance, artist SEO, taste bubbles, Library→Mix) wasn't on this Mac; the user's "the web search engine now uses google seo" was the tell (resolved the both-prepended README/SESSIONS conflicts by interleaving). **Scope findings:** the search-RPC changes (genre-aware, singles inclusion) already reach iOS automatically (shared DB function); artist SEO is JSON-LD for crawlers (no iOS surface); `/api/search/suggest` re-rank is server-side for the web omnibox iOS doesn't use. The iOS-portable search delta = **progressive results + stale-request guard**, now in `SearchView.search()`: results land as each of the three concurrent lookups finishes (albums+artists render, spinner stops, while the slow recordings-ILIKE song lookup streams in) and a monotonic `searchGeneration` token discards any superseded request's results (the debounce alone can't stop a slow old response landing after a fast new one); also fixed a latent spinner-stuck path when the query is cleared mid-search (the old `defer` reset was lost in the restructure). **Taste:** `TasteProfileResponse` now decodes the full v4 payload (clusters w/ per-world era-scene profiles + tags, disliked, decades, scoreDist, scenes, prestigeShare, full stats), and `TasteView`'s card reel is replaced by `TasteReportView` — a full port of web's report: header card, **territory bubble-pack** (direct port of web's greedy `packCircles` incl. the 2026-07-17 bubbles feature; ZStack of positioned circles scaled viewBox-style into a 260pt frame), per-world cards (dot+name, share %, "mostly 2010s · Korean scene" classification, avg-vs-usual sentence, sub-genre avg bars), decade + score `ColumnChartView`s (peak label, hairline baseline, mean marker), scene `StackedBarView` + legend, canon-reach meter, By-the-numbers (top album + 4 tiles incl. 12-month sparkline), community dumbbells, disliked chips via a minimal `FlowLayout`, and web's interpretation sentences (en+ko — 36 ko strings hand-added; generic keys You/Community/avg/ratings already had ko). `TasteViz` mirrors web's validated `--viz-1..5` palette with light/dark variants via dynamic UIColor. **Also fixed: iOS was calling `/api/taste/profile?refresh=1` on every load — the exact cache-bypass Vercel-CPU bug removed from web on 2026-07-15; dropped.** Lock view untouched. **Verified on-sim against prod with the real signed-in account** (48 ratings): all sections screenshotted with live data — 5 packed bubble worlds (K-Pop 44% etc.), decade histogram avg-2019 marker, score histogram avg-3.70, Korean 60%/Western 40% scene bar, canon 21%, NewJeans top album, 3 dumbbells; disliked section correctly absent (user has none — still unverified visually). Search's progressive behavior not visually verified (needs live typing) but is the same data path re-ordered. Probes (tab default + scroll hook + diagnostic print) all reverted; build clean.

**2026-07-18 (Mac) — App Store rejection fixed (Guideline 4, Sign in with Apple asking for the name).** Apple rejected the submitted build ("The app requires users to provide their name after using Sign in with Apple. This information is already provided by the Authentication Services framework."). Root cause: `OnboardingView` always included the `.name` step and merely *pre-filled* it from the OAuth metadata — Apple requires not asking at all. Fix: `steps` and the metadata lookup moved into `init` (was `onAppear` — steps must never change after first render); the name step now only exists when NO provider name is available (`full_name`/`name` metadata keys, same lookup as before), otherwise the provided name is used silently and stays editable in Edit Profile. Applies to all providers (Google/Spotify supply names too), which is both consistent and what the guideline wants. `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION` bumped 6→7 for the resubmission. Verified on-sim via probe (signed-in user has name metadata → onboarding opens directly on "Create a username." with 4 dots); the empty-metadata fallback path still shows the name step by construction. **User actions for resubmission:** archive/upload build 7 (manual — no App Store Connect API key on this machine), and in App Store Connect reply to the rejection message noting the name step is now skipped when Sign in with Apple provides the name, then resubmit. Note the fix ships together with the whole uncommitted 2026-07-17 Instagram-share batch below.

**2026-07-17 (Mac) — Instagram share: optional photo/video background behind the share card. Uncommitted at write time (3 files: InstagramShare.swift, SharePreviewSheet.swift, Localizable.xcstrings).**

- User: when sharing a post to Instagram, give the option of adding a background photo or video behind the widget (the share card). Instagram's Stories pasteboard contract already supports this via `com.instagram.sharedSticker.backgroundImage` / `.backgroundVideo` — the sticker rides on top of the user's media instead of the flat `backgroundTopColor`/`BottomColor` cream that was hardcoded before (those color keys now only apply when no background is picked).
- **`InstagramShare.swift`:** new `ShareBackground` enum (`.photo(UIImage, jpegData:)` / `.video(previewURL:, data:)` — carries both a previewable form and the raw pasteboard bytes, resolved once at pick time); `shareToInstagramStories(stickerImage:background:)` switches the pasteboard keys on it; new `composite(sticker:over:)` flattens the sticker over a photo backdrop at 1080×1920 (backdrop aspect-filled, sticker centered at 82% canvas width) so **Save to Photos / More Options export the same thing the Instagram handoff produces — for photos.** Video backdrops are deliberately Instagram-only (compositing video needs an AVFoundation export session — parked; Save/More export the card alone, footnoted in the UI).
- **`SharePreviewSheet.swift`:** "Add a background photo or video" pill under the card (PhotosPicker, `.any(of: [.images, .videos])` — same out-of-process picker EditProfileView already uses, no photo-library permission needed). Once picked, the flat card preview swaps to a 9:16 story mock: media aspect-filled (photos via `Image`, videos via a new `LoopingVideoView` — `AVPlayerLayer` + `AVPlayerLooper`, muted, `.resizeAspectFill`, because plain `VideoPlayer` letterboxes and shows controls), card overlaid at the composite's exact 82% relative width so preview == export. Change/Remove chips are **overlaid on the preview bottom** (black-45% capsules, white text) — first version put them below the preview and the 9:16 frame pushed them under the scroll fold, caught via screenshot. Same reason the video-only footnote sits at the *top* under the header hint, and previewWidth is 216pt (240 overflowed once the note line existed; and the narrower width made the original "Change background"/"Remove background" labels wrap → shortened to "Change"/"Remove"). Photos re-encode to JPEG 0.9 before the pasteboard so Instagram never sees HEIC; videos load as `Data` (temp-file copy for the player), capped at 100MB (a Story is ≤60s; a multi-hundred-MB pick would stall the pasteboard handoff), load failure shows an inline note.
- **ko strings** hand-added to `Localizable.xcstrings` (CLI builds don't sync the catalog): 배경 사진·영상 추가 / 변경 / 제거 + the load-failure and video-footnote lines. Kept the diff to +50 lines, no whole-file reformat.
- **Follow-up 6 (same day, user: "is it possible to change the dark/light mode of the widget on click?"): yes — tap the card to flip it.** The card is built entirely from adaptive color assets (sjCream/sjInk/sjSurface/…), so one forced `.environment(\.colorScheme,)` flips the whole thing. New `cardScheme` state (defaults `.light`, independent of the phone's appearance): the preview card gets the environment override + an `onTapGesture` toggle (0.2s ease animation), and `renderStickerImage` gained a required `colorScheme:` param (the sticker re-renders via `onChange(of: cardScheme)`, so Instagram handoffs and all composites automatically follow the pick — the export param also makes explicit what was previously implicit renderer-default traits). Verified dark render on-sim via `SJ_PROBE_DARKCARD` env probe (dark surface/white text/badge legible over the cream backdrop; the tap itself isn't CLI-testable but is a plain onTapGesture). Probe reverted, build clean. Discoverability note: the toggle is invisible until tapped — if users don't find it, a small sun/moon chip near the card is the obvious follow-up.
- **Follow-up 5 (same day, user: "the color selection modal is way too large. we don't have to give that much options"): system ColorPicker replaced with curated swatches.** The native `ColorPicker` opens Apple's full grid/spectrum/sliders/eyedropper sheet — far more control than a story backdrop needs. Solid page now shows 7 preset color dots (cream/ink/brand-blue/soft-pink/butter/terracotta/sage), Gradient page 6 preset top→bottom pairs (cream→blue default, peach→sunset, indigo→teal, night, sky→deep-blue, pink→periwinkle), rendered as 22pt circles in the same black-45% capsule, tap-to-apply with a white selection ring (shared `swatchRow` helper). `Color` equality drives the ring, which works because selections only ever come from the same static preset instances. "Top"/"Bottom" xcstrings keys deleted with the wells. Both rows verified on-sim via probe (reverted).
- **Follow-up 4 (same day, user): header hint removed, preview + card enlarged.** Dropped the "This is what gets exported…" hint (key deleted from xcstrings) and gave the carousel its space: previewWidth 216→248. Card enlarged from 72%→78% of canvas width — in **both** the preview scale and `InstagramShare.composite`, so preview still equals export. The two conditional notes that lived under the hint (video-applies-to-Instagram, media-load-failed) became overlay capsules pinned to the top of the photo page itself (`pageNote`, black-45% rounded rect, white text) — they're photo-page-specific anyway, and keeping them out of the layout keeps every page a fixed height with no fold overflow in any state. Verified solid + video states on-sim via probe (reverted).
- **Follow-up 3 (same day, user: "we should build export to reels and posts as well if possible. look into it"): Reels + feed-post export built.** Researched Meta's current docs first (WebSearch/WebFetch — gstack `/browse` still not installed on this Mac): **Reels** has an official pasteboard handoff mirroring Stories (`instagram-reels://share`, `com.instagram.sharedSticker.backgroundVideo` REQUIRED — Reels are video-only by API design, no color/image keys exist — plus optional `stickerImage` + the same app ID; video 3–60s, H.264/H.265, ≤50MB recommended, enforced by Instagram not pre-validated by us). **Feed posts** have no pasteboard API; the documented route is Document Interaction: JPEG written with `.igo` extension + UTI `com.instagram.exclusivegram` (the `.ig`/`com.instagram.photo` variant would offer all Instagram-capable apps) opens the feed composer; photo-only. Implementation: `InstagramShare.canShareToInstagramReels`/`shareToInstagramReels`; new `InstagramFeedShare` (NSObject singleton holding the `UIDocumentInteractionController` alive for the interaction — it dies with the tap otherwise — presenting from the key window's root VC); `composite`/`gradientImage` gained a `canvas` param + new `postCanvas` 1080×1350 (4:5, Instagram's tallest feed ratio — handing the 9:16 canvas over would just get force-cropped) with a `postExportImage` computed on the sheet; `instagram-reels` + `instagram` added to `LSApplicationQueriesSchemes`. UI: the second Instagram button **swaps with the background type** (video → "Share to Instagram Reels", static → "Share as Instagram Post") instead of stacking, and Save/More merged into one two-up row, so the button stack stays 3 rows tall. ko added for both new buttons **and backfilled for the previously-untranslated "Share to Instagram Story"**. Verified both button layouts on-sim via the probe with a `SJ_PROBE_IG=1` gate-force (sim has no Instagram; handoffs themselves untestable here). **Parked:** Reels for static backgrounds would need synthesizing a 3s+ video from a still (AVAssetWriter) — noted as follow-up if wanted.
- **Follow-up 2 (same day, user spec): background rework — swipeable carousel, no "Remove", camera option.** User: removing the background shouldn't be an option; instead 4 customizable background styles swiped through like a carousel — solid color (customizable), gradient (customizable), blurred album cover, photo — and the photo page's Change button should drop down with "take a new photo / choose from gallery". Implementation: new `ShareBackgroundStyle` carousel (a `TabView(.page)` of 9:16 story mocks, custom dots + page-title row below since the built-in index dots would collide with the per-page control chips; the old picked-media enum renamed `PickedShareMedia`, the old "no background" state deleted). Solid/gradient pages carry `ColorPicker` wells (defaults: cream #F8F8F5, gradient cream→sjBlue; **fixed cream, not adaptive `Color.sjCream`** — the exported story must not vary with the phone's dark mode) and hand off through Instagram's native `backgroundTopColor`/`BottomColor` keys, which render a real vertical gradient when they differ — no image needed. Blurred Cover: `InstagramShare.blurredCoverBackdrop` (cover aspect-filled to 1080×1920, `clampedToExtent` + gaussian sigma 60, precomputed off-main in a `.task`); preview approximates it with SwiftUI `.blur(12)` + 1.15 overscale (sigma 60 at 1080px ≈ 12pt at the 216pt preview; the overscale hides the blur's faded edges). Photo page: `Menu` dropdown → "Take a Photo" (new `CameraCaptureView`, bare `UIImagePickerController`, **new `NSCameraUsageDescription` in Info.plist**; entry hidden where no camera, e.g. simulator) / "Choose from Gallery" (`.photosPicker` modifier, still images+videos). New resolved-handoff enum `StoryBackground` (.colors/.image/.video); `shareToInstagramStories` takes it non-optionally (always a background now) with a new `hexString` UIColor→"#RRGGBB" helper; **Save/More now export the full 1080×1920 composite for every style** (solid/gradient via new `gradientImage`) except video (still card-only + footnote). ko added: 단색/그라데이션/커버 블러/사진·영상/위/아래/사진·영상 추가/사진 촬영/갤러리에서 선택; obsolete keys ("Add a background photo or video", "Remove") deleted. (User follow-up "no videos?" — videos were always supported through the gallery path, but the page/chip labels said "Photo"/"Add Photo" and hid it; renamed to "Photo & Video" / "Add Photo or Video".) Verified on-sim via the same env probe extended to `SJ_PROBE_BG=solid|gradient|cover|photo|photo-empty`: all 5 states screenshotted correct (wells legible on the media, dots/title under the carousel, everything above the fold). **Not verifiable on sim:** actual swipe gesture (same standard TabView paging as everywhere), camera flow (no sim camera), Instagram handoff. One localization gotcha fixed in review: `Text(condition ? "A" : "B")` with string literals resolves to `String` not `LocalizedStringKey` — silently skips localization; wrapped both branches in `String(localized:)`.
- **Follow-up (same day, user on-device feedback): baked-in card shadow removed.** The sticker PNG carried `ShareCardView`'s `.shadow(0.18, radius 18, y 8)` + the 24pt padding that existed only to keep it from clipping at the export bounds — over a real photo/video it "looks weird" (a static shadow direction can't match the backdrop's lighting). Both removed; the sticker's natural width is now 320 (was 368), so `composite`'s canvas factor and the preview's `scaleEffect` moved 0.82→0.72 to keep the visible card size identical (0.82 × 320/368). Verified on-sim via the same probe (card renders flat over the photo backdrop, same size), probe reverted again, build clean.
- **Verified on-sim** (iPhone 17 Pro) via a temp env-driven probe (`SIMCTL_CHILD_SJ_PROBE=share` → ShareProbeView presenting the sheet with dummy data; `SJ_PROBE_BG=photo` seeded a gradient, `SJ_PROBE_BG=video` + `SJ_PROBE_BG_PATH` seeded a real 3s H.264 clip recorded off the simulator itself with `simctl io recordVideo`): default state (pill under card), photo state (backdrop filled, card centered, chips legible), video state (clip actually playing aspect-filled behind the card, footnote visible at top). 3 layout iterations caught by screenshots (chips below fold → overlay; footnote below fold → top; chip labels wrapping → shortened). Probe fully reverted (`sillajukuApp.swift` via git checkout, SharePreviewSheet block edited out), final build clean. **Not verifiable from here:** the actual Instagram handoff with a background (no Instagram on the simulator — the pasteboard keys are Meta's documented contract, and the existing sticker handoff was device-confirmed back on 2026-07-08) and the PhotosPicker interaction itself (out-of-process). Worth one real-device tap-through with Instagram installed before the next App Store build.

**2026-07-19 (Windows) — 18-item web change list kicked off: gauge redesign + OKLCh score ramp, shared loading vocabulary, raw-ID flash, "Go to charts", "Not interested" + the shared overflow menu, right-click menus, bookmark-icon unification, album-page flower rating (row + cover button), the mix-page bug, grouped artist discography, the Stats-tab rebuild, @username-first identity. 13 items touched, 12 finished.** The list itself was handed over as a prompt doc and is now persisted (with a per-item status board) as `WEB_CHANGE_PROMPTS.md`. Worked in the stated dependency order — #2 and #12 first because the rest consume them, then #1 because #3 and #17 need its menu, then #5 with #16 in the same pass since both are album-page rating surfaces that have to agree. `tsc --noEmit` + `next lint` + `next build` clean; nothing browser-verified. #1's migration was applied to the hosted Supabase project later the same day.

- **#2 gauge redesign (`components/sj/FlowerRateControl.tsx`).** The old design hid the flower during the drag and drew five concentric rings plus a dashed line to the cursor. Now: the **flower stays visible and stationary**, and the gauge anchors to the button's `getBoundingClientRect()` centre rather than the press point (so it doesn't shift depending on where on the glyph you pressed). The rings are replaced by a **single thin arc** at the current score's radius, centred on the cursor's angle, spanning 52° — built from 22 short line segments with a `sin(t·π)^1.4` opacity falloff, which fades it out along its length without needing gradient coordinates in arc space. Underneath it, a **dotted horizontal baseline** with ticks at each whole star, drawn as *two* halves starting at the dead-zone edge so nothing crosses the flower. The score **reveals in place over the flower** — the glyph fades and shrinks to 0.82, the number fades up from scale 0.6 on a `cubic-bezier(0.34, 1.56, 0.64, 1)` overshoot — replacing the floating readout that used to track the cursor. **Layout gotcha worth remembering:** the glyph and the number are stacked via CSS grid (`grid place-items-center` + both children on `gridArea: '1 / 1'`) rather than `relative`/`absolute inset-0`, because several call sites (`FeedCard`, artist page, charts) pass their own `absolute bottom-1 right-1 …` through `className` and a `relative` on the base would have fought it. Radii recalibrated for the 0.1 scale: `OFFSET` 44→36 dead zone, `STEP` 52→34 px/star, full 0.5→5.0 sweep at ~206px, ~3.4px per tenth; `distanceToScore` now rounds to **0.1**, not 0.5. Tap→precise-modal, dead-zone-release→cancel, pointer capture and `touch-action: none` all preserved.
- **#2 color ramp (`lib/sj/display.ts`).** The score spectrum was an HSL hue sweep (0°→205.7° at fixed S/L), which is not perceptually uniform — the yellows blew out and the blues went muddy. Replaced with an **OKLCh ramp**: five hue/chroma anchors (deep red 25° → orange 58° → yellow-green 126° → teal 196° → blue 262°), linearly interpolated, rendered at a **fixed OKLab lightness per variant** — `spectrumFill` L .92 / chroma ×.28, `spectrumNumber` L .45 / ×.95, `spectrumRing` L .63. Added a full OKLab→linear-sRGB→gamma pipeline (Ottosson's matrices) and, for out-of-gamut colors, **walk chroma down by ×0.92 until it fits** instead of clipping channels — clipping would have destroyed exactly the lightness constancy the ramp exists for. Verified numerically: fill-vs-number luminance contrast stays **5.6–6.3 across all of 0.5–5.0**. New `spectrumColor(score, lightness, chromaScale)` export for gradient/chart consumers (#4's histogram, #6's heatmap). Function names unchanged, so `ScoreBadge` and every badge site picked it up at once. **Two side effects flagged:** `spectrumHue` now returns OKLCh degrees, not CSS HSL degrees (nothing external called it — verified by grep — but it would silently produce wrong colors if fed to `hsl()`); and **iOS `Components/ScoreBadge.swift` still runs the old HSL sweep**, so web and iOS badges no longer match — a new parity gap, left alone under the web-only rule.
- **#12 shared loading vocabulary (`components/sj/Loading.tsx`, new).** `FlowerSpinner` (rotating `icon-flower.svg`) and `PageLoader` for the no-known-layout case, plus skeleton primitives `Skeleton`, `SkeletonLine`, `SkeletonBlock`, `SkeletonCover`, `SkeletonCard`, `SkeletonCardGrid`, `SkeletonRows`. Everything animates through `animate-pulse`/`animate-spin` paired with the `motion-reduce:` variant, so `prefers-reduced-motion` yields a static placeholder. **Rollout is deliberately incomplete** — only album and artist are converted; roughly a dozen pages still hand-roll `animate-pulse` (taste, song, charts ×3, notifications, search, quick-add, post, home, `ProfileView`, `ProfileExtras`, `HeaderMenus`, `AlbumPeek`). Exact line references are listed in `WEB_CHANGE_PROMPTS.md` so the next session doesn't have to re-find them.
- **#11 raw ID during load.** The culprit was one expression: `artist/[id]/page.tsx` rendered `<h1>{name || rawId}</h1>`, and for a UUID route `name` initialises to `''`, so the bare UUID painted until the `artists` row resolved. Now renders a `SkeletonLine` when `name` is empty, with a skeleton circle standing in for the avatar and `SkeletonRows` replacing the tab body's bare `…` placeholder. **The album page turned out not to have the bug** — it early-returns a skeleton until `release` exists and never touches `params.id` in JSX (checked every use) — so it only got its hand-rolled skeleton rebuilt on the shared primitives.
- **#1 "Not interested" + the app's first reusable overflow (…) menu.** Until now the only feedback a user could give a recommended album was to rate it, so an album they never intend to hear came back forever — `NOT EXISTS (… ratings …)` was the only exclusion Quick Add had. New migration `apps/web/supabase/migrations/20260719000000_not_interested.sql`: `not_interested(user_id, release_group_id, created_at)` with a composite PK (so marking twice is a no-op upsert, not a PK violation), an owner-only `FOR ALL` policy in the wrapped-auth-call style (`(select auth.uid()) = user_id`) — deliberately **not** readable by other users, since "things I don't want" isn't social data — and a `CREATE OR REPLACE` of `get_quick_add_candidates` that re-issues 20260717000000's body verbatim plus a second `NOT EXISTS` against the new table. Signature/columns unchanged, so the server-side exclusion needs no client change. New `lib/sj/notInterested.ts` (`fetchNotInterestedIds` / `markNotInterested` / `unmarkNotInterested`) is best-effort by design: a missing table or an RLS refusal resolves to "nothing dismissed" rather than throwing, so an unmigrated environment degrades to client-side-only filtering instead of a dead feed. New `components/sj/AlbumOverflowMenu.tsx` splits into a `MoreHorizontal` button (styled to match `AlbumBookmarkButton`'s cover-overlay pill) and an exported **`OverflowMenuSurface`**, positioned from a plain viewport point rather than from the button — that split is what lets **#3's right-click menu reuse it at the cursor**. The surface portals to `document.body` (a menu opened inside a list row or an `overflow-hidden` cover would otherwise clip), clamps itself to both viewport edges, closes on outside-mousedown/Escape/scroll/resize, and defers registering the outside-click listener by one tick so the opening click doesn't immediately close it. Callers pass extra actions via `items`; **Not interested** is the built-in one and always renders last. Surfaced on **Quick Add** rows (bottom-right of the cover, opposite the bookmark) and in **Home explore**'s `FeedCard` menu (the existing inline menu got one more `MenuItem` rather than being swapped out — smaller diff, same result). Both surfaces filter at *render* against a `dismissed`/`notInterested` `Set` rather than splicing the fetched array, which keeps Quick Add's `albums.length`-based pagination offset honest. **Deliberate scope call:** the Following feed is *not* filtered — those are people you chose to follow, not a recommendation. i18n `sj.notInterested.{action,done,undo}` in en+ko (`done`/`undo` are unused for now — no toast was built; they're there for the undo affordance if the user wants one). `tsc --noEmit`, `next lint`, and `next build` all clean. **Migration applied 2026-07-19** to the hosted Supabase project with `npx tsx --env-file=.env.local scripts/db-exec.ts supabase/migrations/20260719000000_not_interested.sql` (the Management API path — this machine has no direct DB URL; needs `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` in `apps/web/.env.local`). Verified afterwards: `not_interested` exists with `relrowsecurity = true`, 1 policy, 2 indexes (PK + release-group), and `get_quick_add_candidates`'s new body contains the `not_interested` exclusion. Dismissals now survive reload and Quick Add filters server-side.
- **#3 right-click context menus.** The point of the task was *not* to build a menu — #1 already shipped one — so `components/sj/ContextMenu.tsx` is deliberately thin: `useContextMenu(items)`, `useContextMenuFor(build)`, and a `<ContextMenu>` wrapper component, all of which capture `e.clientX/clientY` and hand them to #1's `OverflowMenuSurface`. Viewport clamping, the portal, and outside-click/Escape/scroll/resize dismissal are inherited, not reimplemented. Two decisions worth recording. (1) The handler calls `stopPropagation()`, so the **innermost** wrapper wins — a track row nested inside an album card opens the track menu only, instead of both firing as the event bubbles. (2) `wantsNativeMenu()` bails out (leaving the browser's own menu) over `input`/`textarea`/`select`/`contenteditable` and whenever there's a live non-collapsed text selection, because right-clicking a selection is essentially always "copy this". **Album covers were covered globally in one edit**: `AlbumPeek` already wraps every cover in the app (charts ×3, `charts/[slug]`, artist, search, home, Quick Add, `FeedCard`), so `useAlbumContextMenu` hangs off its *existing* root node — no new DOM that could disturb a cover's layout, and no per-page wiring. `AlbumContextMenu.tsx` is self-contained the way `AlbumRateButton` is: it owns the precise-rating modal, the mix picker, and its own `ratings` upsert/delete. It gained an optional `release` prop on `AlbumPeek` (passed where a full `SJRelease` was already in scope — FeedCard, search, artist; charts still synthesize one from display props, so their rating modal shows the generic "Release" type chip) and an `onNotInterested` passthrough so Quick Add and Home explore drop the card from the right-click path exactly as they do from the … menu. **The non-obvious bug this surfaced:** `Modal` is plain `fixed` markup, *not* a portal, and most covers sit inside a `<Link>` — rendering the rating dialog in place meant every click inside it bubbled to the anchor and navigated to the album page.
