The context menu's dialogs are therefore `createPortal`'d to `document.body`. (Portalling `Modal` itself would be the real fix but touches every modal in the app — noted, not done.) Also wired: **artist links** through a new `ArtistLink.tsx` drop-in that keeps a real `<Link>` underneath so middle-click/⌘-click still work natively (album page ×2, song page, search results), **album-page track rows** (one `useContextMenuFor` for the whole list rather than extracting each row into a component), and **mix rows** (Remove from Mix appears only for the owner, resolved per-open). New i18n group `sj.context.{openNewTab,rate,saveToMix,goToArtist,removeFromMix}` in en+ko.
- **#17 add-to-mix → bookmark icon.** Four affordances swapped to lucide `Bookmark`: `FeedCard`'s overflow "Save to Mix" (`ListMusic`), `PostRatingOptions`' add-to-list row (`PlusSquare`), the album page's header action (`ListPlus`), and `AlbumBookmarkButton`'s own "Add to another Mix" row (`ListMusic`) — which was the odd one out, since the button that opened it was already a bookmark. Behavior is untouched; they all still open `MixPickerModal`. **Left alone on purpose:** icons that denote *a mix* rather than *the act of adding to one* — `MixPickerModal`'s per-mix rows, the profile Mixes tab, the album page's "in public mixes" list, and the home empty states. Those stay `ListMusic`.
- **#5 album page: stars → flowers, click-to-rate at the manual step (`components/sj/InlineRatingEditor.tsx`).** The album page was the last rating surface still drawing lucide `Star`s, and its "half star" was a lie — the whole glyph rendered at `opacity-50`, so 3.5 and a dimmed 4 looked identical in the collapsed row. Both are gone. Each mark is now a **`FlowerGlyph` (`/icon-flower.svg`) with a real horizontal clip**: a base copy at `opacity-25` and, layered on top, a second copy inside an `absolute inset-y-0 left-0 overflow-hidden` span whose width is the fill fraction — so 3.5 draws literally half a flower and any tenth in between renders correctly, which matters because #2 made the drag gauge resolve to 0.1. **Click targets are derived from `step`, not hardcoded:** `segments = clamp(round(1 / step), 1, 10)` invisible absolutely-positioned buttons per flower (0.5 → halves, 1.0 → wholes, 0.1 → ten slivers, capped so an exotic step can't spawn hundreds of nodes), each labelled with its own value and each running the existing `clampSnap` before it commits. Hover state moved from `hoverStar: number` to `hoverValue: number` for the same reason — the preview has to be able to show a partial. `onFocus`/`onBlur` mirror the hover so the row previews under keyboard traversal too. Persistence is untouched: a click still routes into `openEditor(v)` / `update(v)`, so the existing debounced auto-save, undo, and remove-rating paths all apply and the album page's single `setRating` remains the only writer.
- **#16 flower rate button on the album cover.** `AlbumRateButton` (the #2 drag gauge) now sits at the bottom-right of the album-page hero cover — `Cover` was wrapped in a `relative` box sized `w-28 h-28 md:w-64 md:h-64` with the button absolutely placed inside it. It's hidden in **instinct** mode, where a 0–5 manual score isn't the page's rating model. **Keeping it in sync with #5's row needed two new optional props on `AlbumRateButton`**, both backwards-compatible so its ten existing call sites are unaffected: `score` (a controlled value the page pushes down, mirrored into internal state by an effect rather than read directly — otherwise the button would lag a drag while waiting on the page's refetch) and `onScoreChange` (fired **after** the write resolves, so the page's `loadRatings()` refetch sees the committed row). Its internal `score` state was renamed `shown` to make the "optimistic display value, not the source of truth" role explicit. `saveModal` was also restructured while adding the callback — the delete branch used to `return` early, which meant a rating removed through the precise modal would have skipped the new notification; it now falls through to a single shared exit.
- **#18 "Go to charts" removed.** Deleted the trailing `<Link href="/charts">` from `TrendingRail` in `app/(main)/page.tsx` and the now-dead `sj.home.viewCharts` key from `lib/i18n/en.ts` + `ko.ts`. `Link` and `t` are both still used elsewhere in that component, so no imports moved. Charts stay reachable from the sidebar.
- **#10 mix page — the bug was a PostgREST embed, not the UI.** Worth recording how it was found, because three plausible hypotheses were wrong first. The reported symptom ("clicking a mix shows nothing") suggested a stuck loading state, a bad id param, or RLS. The schema check ruled out the id: `mix_items.release_id → releases` from the original migration was replaced by `release_group_id → release_groups` in `20260624000001_db_renovation.sql`, and every reader and writer (`MixPickerModal`, `AlbumBookmarkButton`, the album page) agrees on the new column. The `20260708000001` RLS rewrite left the mix policies intact. Live data was healthy: 171 mixes, 14 items, zero null `release_group_id`, and all 14 join to a real release group. What actually broke it only showed up by replaying **the page's exact two queries through PostgREST with the anon key**: the mix query returned **`PGRST201` — "Could not embed because more than one relationship was found for 'mixes' and 'profiles'"**, status **300**, because `mix_likes` (`mix_likes_mix_id_fkey` + `mix_likes_user_id_fkey`) is a second `mixes ↔ profiles` path alongside `mixes_user_id_fkey`. So `.select('*, profiles(username, display_name)')` failed on *every* mix, public or private, and since the page destructured only `{ data }` the failure arrived as `data: null` and hit the `if (!m) { setLoading(false); return }` branch — rendering "Mix not found". The `mix_items` query, the one everybody suspected, was correct and returned both albums with covers and titles; it simply never ran. Fix is one FK hint: `profiles!mixes_user_id_fkey(username, display_name)`. **The same bare embed was live on `album/[id]/page.tsx:272`** for the "in public mixes" section, which had therefore been rendering empty for as long as `mix_likes` has existed — fixed identically, and in scope because it's a surface that links into the mix page. Both fixed queries were re-run against the hosted project and now return rows. Three pieces of hardening went in alongside: (1) **both queries capture `error`**, log it, and render a retryable error state (reusing the existing `sj.common.loadError` + `sj.common.retry` keys, driven by a `reloadKey` state) — dropping `error` is precisely what let a 300 masquerade as an empty result; (2) the fetch is now **gated on `useSession().ready`**, a genuine second bug — every mix but an explicitly public one is owner-only under RLS, so a fetch that raced session restore read as "not found" and, with deps of `[mixId]` only, never retried (home/search/taste already use this gate); (3) a `console.warn` when `mix_items` returns rows but every `release_groups` embed is null, since that state is otherwise pixel-identical to an empty mix. The bare `…` loader became a **#12 skeleton** (title line + meta line + `SkeletonRows`) shaped like the real page. `ProfileView`'s Mixes tab still has its own `…` — left for #12's rollout.
- **#15 artist discography, grouped.** The Albums tab rendered one flat list straight from `get_artist_release_groups` (the prompt's "backed by `/api/artist-albums`" was wrong — that route exists but this page doesn't use it), with a type label built by hand-capitalising the raw `release_group_type`, which meant it was never translated. Replaced with a `<Discography>` component: sections in a fixed order — **Albums · EPs · Singles · Compilations · Soundtracks · Other** — each with a release count, empty sections omitted. Those six come from checking the catalogue rather than guessing: `select release_group_type, count(*) from release_groups` returns exactly single (195,647) / album (139,670) / compilation (82,118) / ep (39,116) / soundtrack (18,389), so **Other** exists only as a catch-all for future or stray values instead of the code minting a section per unknown string. Two `Segmented` pill controls (plain `<button>`s with `aria-pressed`, so keyboard traversal needs no extra handling): group **By type** vs **All** (a single chronological list), and sort **Newest / Oldest / A–Z**. Both are a pure `useMemo` over the `releases` array the tab already holds — the controls never trigger a refetch, and the other tabs keep reading the same unsorted source. One judgement call in the comparator: **releases with no `first_release_date` sort last in both directions** rather than being treated as year 0, because "oldest first" opening with a block of undated rows reads as a broken page. Row markup was extracted verbatim into `ReleaseRow` (AlbumPeek hover bezel, hover bookmark, rate gauge, and score chip all preserved). `typeLabelKey` in `lib/sj/display.ts` gained `compilation` and `soundtrack` cases with matching `sj.type.*` keys in en+ko, so rows now go through i18n — that also fixes the label on the mix page and anywhere else `typeLabelKey` is used. New i18n group `sj.artist.{groupAlbums,groupEps,groupSingles,groupCompilations,groupSoundtracks,groupOther,groupByType,groupAll,sortNewest,sortOldest,sortTitle,oneRelease,nReleases}` in both locales. **⚠ Left alone:** the RPC still caps at `lim: 60`, so a prolific artist's discography is truncated before grouping ever sees it — grouping makes that ceiling more obvious than a flat list did.
- **Verified (pt 2):** `tsc --noEmit`, `next lint`, and `next build` all clean; #10's two corrected queries additionally verified live against the hosted Supabase project. #15 is not browser-verified.
- **#4 Profile > Stats rebuilt (`components/sj/ProfileStats.tsx`, new).** The tab moved out of `ProfileView.tsx` (which shed ~140 lines and now just renders `<ProfileStats items instinctCount />`); `Empty` and `StatCell` stayed behind because the Rated/Mixes tabs and the header still use them. `RatingHistogram.tsx` was **not** touched — despite the name it belongs to the *album* page's community distribution, a different component with a different data shape, so "polish the profile histogram" doesn't mean it. The audit found more than cosmetics. **Three different card treatments in one tab** (`rounded-xl bg-ink/[0.05]` strip → bare `<section>`s → `rounded-[10px] bg-accent/[0.08]` tiles) collapsed into one `StatCard` primitive plus a `KpiTile`; a stray **`max-w-lg`** pinned the dashboard to a narrow column while every sibling tab used the page's `max-w-3xl`. The substantive bug was **two undisclosed denominators**: the headline average ran over `items.map(i => i.score)` — manual scores only — while the histogram directly beneath it also included elo scores once `INSTINCT_REVEAL_THRESHOLD` was met, so the number didn't describe the chart. There is now a single `scoreOf()` applying the reveal rule once, feeding the average, the histogram *and* the new per-artist averages, with the count stated in the card header (`fromNScored`) so the denominator is visible rather than implied. Chart changes follow the `dataviz` skill: bar heights are percentages of the plot box (they were raw px against a fixed `h-24`, so the chart didn't respond to its own container), bars are coloured by #2's **`spectrumRing(bucketScore)`** — chosen over `spectrumColor` at a hand-picked lightness because L .63 is the one variant that holds on both the light and the dark surface without a second value, and because colour here is *redundant* with x-position, so a CVD reader loses nothing; counts became **selective labels** (tallest bucket always, every other on hover, in a fixed-height row so bars don't shift) instead of the anti-pattern of a number on every bar; and a dashed average reference line was added, positioned by `scoreToPct(s) = ((s*2 - 0.5) / 10) * 100` — bucket **centres**, not edges, which is the easy thing to get wrong here. Top artists: the bar track was `bg-transparent` so the bars floated with no reference, and the count column was `w-6` (clipping 3 digits) — now a real `bg-divider/60` track, `w-8` + `formatCount`, top 8 instead of 5, each row also showing that artist's average in the ramp colour, ties broken on average rather than insertion order. The rating-mode tiles became one legended `SplitBar` (2px segment gap per the mark spec, `bg-accent` + `bg-ink/40` so it's accent-plus-neutral and needs no palette validation), and its instinct count is now **elo-only** (`score == null && eloScore != null`) — the old one counted every row with an `elo_score`, including rows that also had a manual score, so the two "segments" overlapped. New low-data states: zero scored ratings renders a sentence instead of ten empty bars, and under 10 adds a `lowData` hint. New i18n `sj.profile.{avgShort,statArtists,fromNScored,noScores,lowData}` en+ko.
- **#9 @username over display name.** Audited every user-row surface before changing anything, and **most of the app was already correct** — `LikersModal`, `TrackLikersModal`, `CommentsModal` and `TrackCommentsModal` all render `@{profileHandle(...)}`, and `notifications.tsx` builds `@${actor.username ?? actor.display_name}`. The prompt's guess that search shows user results is wrong; `search/page.tsx` has no profile rows at all. Exactly two places were inverted, both in `ProfileView.tsx`: the **header**, which rendered `{displayName || \`@${handle}\`}` with the handle demoted to a trailing muted span — i.e. it treated the handle as the *fallback* — and **`FollowListModal`**'s rows, same shape. Both now lead with `@username` (16px / 13.5px semibold) and put the display name on a secondary muted line, omitted entirely when unset. In the modal the secondary line is also suppressed when `username` is missing, since in that case the handle slot is already showing the display name and repeating it would read as a bug. Presentation only — no query, column, or iOS payload touched.
- **Verified (pt 3):** `tsc --noEmit`, `next lint`, and `next build` all clean. Neither #4 nor #9 is browser-verified; #4's histogram colours, the average marker's placement, and the card grid at mobile width are the open visual questions.

**2026-07-17 (Windows, pt 2) — Drag-gauge refined + rolled out to every album surface; Quick Add consolidated onto the gauge.** User validated the gauge ("current version is okay") then asked for it everywhere + refinements. All `tsc`/`next lint`/`next build` clean.

- **`FlowerRateControl` refinements.** (1) The drag readout is now **concentric translucent rings** around the flower — 5 rings, one per whole star, filling outward (spectrum-colored band) as the cursor moves. (2) Score stays **small, inside the original icon circle** (no more enlarged 108px gauge). (3) **Travel distance doubled** (`MAX_DIST` 150→300px). (4) New `currentScore` prop → a rated control shows its score and **stays re-ratable** (press+drag again). (5) Uses the **clean rounded `icon-flower.svg`** (ported from iOS `Assets.xcassets/icon-flower`) instead of the halftone `logo-flower.svg` brand logo — `FlowerGlyph` gained a `src` prop. **Follow-up tuning:** ring radii are now the **literal drag distances** for each whole star (`OFFSET` 44px dead zone + `STEP` 52px/star → rings at 96/148/200/252/304px), a **center dead zone cancels** the operation if you release back inside it (score goes `null`, not 0.5), **only a short click** (movement < 8px) opens the precise modal, and the filled discs use **stacked translucent fills** so opacity steps up toward the center as more stars fill.
- **`AlbumRateButton` (new, `components/sj/AlbumRateButton.tsx`).** Self-contained drop-in: renders the gauge, owns its `ratings` upsert/delete + a `ManualRateModal` precise fallback, re-ratable, self-hides when signed out, optional `initialScore`. No per-page rated-set plumbing needed.
- **Rolled out "everywhere an album is shown":** artist-page Albums list, charts `AlbumHorizSection` rails (top-rated/most-rated/hidden-gems/controversial), the charts Trending list + Silla leaderboard, and the full `charts/[slug]` ranked list — each as a hover cover-overlay. Search results / explore rows / Quick Add already had it.
- **Quick Add consolidated onto the gauge (retires "keep both").** The user went all-in on the gauge, so `/quick-add` now **is** the circular-gauge page; the parallel `/quick-add-gauge` route and the Mac's 5-flower `FlowerRatingRow.tsx` were removed. (The Mac's `sj.quickAdd.settingUp/bannerBody/caughtUp/caughtUpBody/manualOnly` i18n keys are now unused but harmless.)
- **Gauge on the home feed too.** `FeedCard`'s album cover now carries the rate gauge (`AlbumRateButton`) on hover — the last "add-shaped" surface that was missing it.
- **New hover bookmark → Listen Later (`components/sj/AlbumBookmarkButton.tsx`).** One press saves the album to the user's default `is_default` "Listen Later" mix (resolved via `mixes` + upsert `mix_items`), then opens a portal dropdown: **Saved to {Listen Later}** with **Add to another Mix** (opens `MixPickerModal`) and **Remove**. Dropdown auto-dismisses on any other action (click-away / scroll, capture-phase) or after 4s; ignoring it leaves the album saved. Self-contained + signed-out-safe. i18n `sj.bookmark.*` (en+ko). Placed on album covers everywhere the rate gauge is: search grid, Quick Add rows, artist Albums, all charts album sections + `charts/[slug]`, and the home feed (bookmark top-corner, rate flower bottom-corner to avoid collisions).
- **Hover peek → large translucent tracklist bezel.** `AlbumPeek` (the existing desktop hover-preview, previously a small stats card used only on charts rails) rewritten into a **large glass bezel** that appears in the blank space to the **left** of the hovered cover (flips right only if cramped): album title/artist/meta, community rating, and the **tracklist** (canonical release → `release_tracks`, first 14 + "+N", durations) — both panel and text translucent (`bg-white/70 dark:bg-neutral-900/60 backdrop-blur-xl`, content `opacity-90`). Portalled + `pointer-events-none` so it escapes overflow rails and never blocks clicks; data cached per album per session; desktop-only. Wrapped around covers on **every** surface: search grid, Quick Add (album rows), artist Albums, home feed, charts grid/Trending/Silla + `charts/[slug]`.

**2026-07-17 (Windows) — Five web features built in one pass (web-only; iOS parity to follow): drag-to-rate, Quick Add page + algorithm fix, search relevance/speed, artist SEO, taste "territory" bubbles, profile Library→Mix.** User: "do all tasks for web only for now. I will implement them to app later." All `tsc`/`next lint`/`next build` clean.

- **Drag-to-rate flower control (`components/sj/FlowerRateControl.tsx`, new).** Reusable quick-rate affordance: at rest a small flower button; press-and-drag outward and the distance from the press point maps to the score (farther = higher, 10px→0.5 … 150px→5.0), shown live in a portal-rendered circular gauge that fills + shifts colour (reuses `spectrumRing/Fill/Number`). Release commits; a tap (drag < 10px) falls through to `onRequestPrecise` (the existing modal), so precision is never lost. Pointer events + capture + `touch-action:none` cover mouse and touch. Wired into the search page's album cards + song rows (replacing the `+` buttons) and the new Quick Add page.
- **Quick Add — algorithm fix + new web page.** Root cause of "recommends stuff I don't know": `get_quick_add_candidates` ordered each known artist's albums by `first_release_date DESC` (newest = often most obscure). **Migration `20260717000000_quick_add_album_prestige_order.sql` — ✅ APPLIED** (via `scripts/db-exec.ts` Management API, verified live: `pg_get_functiondef` now contains `prestige_score DESC`) re-orders by `prestige_score DESC` within artist (release date only as tiebreaker), matching the song RPC's existing ordering — same signature/columns/exclusion, no client change needed. New `app/(main)/quick-add/page.tsx` (web counterpart of iOS `QuickAddView`): seeds artists the user likely knows (Spotify top/recent from `profiles`, then own ≥3.5★ artists), Albums/Songs subtabs, infinite scroll, drag-to-rate rows (tap → `ManualRateModal`, held score → `ScoreBadge`). Entry banner added to the search Discovery. i18n `sj.quickAdd.*` (en+ko).
- **Search 1a — relevance + speed + progressive loading.** `app/(main)/search/page.tsx` `runSearch` rewritten: albums and artists now render the moment their own RPC returns (a slow song lookup — recordings scan + release_tracks join — no longer blocks them), plus a `runSeqRef` stale-request guard so a slow earlier query can't overwrite a newer one. `/api/search/suggest` over-fetches then re-ranks in JS for **intent**: exact > prefix match beats a merely-popular substring hit, with prestige (popularity) as the tiebreaker; artists gained an ordering at all (were arbitrary `limit(3)`) — exact/prefix then shorter name.
- **Search 1b — Google SEO.** Biggest gap was the **artist page had no structured data**: added `MusicGroup` JSON-LD (name/url/image) in `artist/[id]/layout.tsx` (complements the site-wide Organization + album/song AggregateRating markup already shipped). Enriched album JSON-LD with canonical `url` + `datePublished` + `albumReleaseType`; song JSON-LD with `url`.
- **Taste 2 — "territory" bubbles.** New `TasteTerritory` in `taste/page.tsx`: each world is a bubble whose **area ∝ its share** (r ∝ √share), greedy-packed (`packCircles`, deterministic, largest-centre-then-tangent) so big worlds crowd out small ones — "competing for land." Reuses the existing validated `--viz-1..5` categorical palette (fixed order, `stroke-surface` 2px gap, direct labels with a dark halo for contrast on any fill, legend + hover `<title>`). Replaced the old stacked composition bar + its prose paragraph (de-wordified per "too wordy/just listing genres"). i18n `territoryHeader/territorySub` (en+ko).
- **Profile 5 — Library→Mix (+ feed follow-up integrated).** Removed the "Library" (saved-to-listen-later / `saved_releases`) tab from `ProfileView.tsx`; tabs are now Rated · **Mixes** · Stats (renamed `lists`→`sj.profile.mixes`). The default mix (Clock icon) remains the listen-later surface, so saving is unified under Mix. **Feed repoint done** (user approved): `FeedCard.tsx`'s overflow "Save" (bookmark → `saved_releases`) is now **"Add to Mix"** opening the existing `MixPickerModal` (keyed on `rg.id`); removed the whole `saved_releases` plumbing from the home feed (`app/(main)/page.tsx`: `savedIds`/`toggleSave`/`mySavesRes`/`releaseIds` and the `isSaved`/`onSave` props). `saved_releases` table + the dead `SavedLibrary` export in `ProfileExtras.tsx` are now unused (harmless; left in place).
- **iOS:** untouched — all five need a Mac build to port (drag control, Quick Add ordering already matches on the song side, artist SEO is web-only, taste bubbles, Library→Mix in `ProfileView.swift`).
- **⚑ MERGE with Mac (`806b687`):** the Mac had independently built a web Quick Add page (5-flower `FlowerRatingRow`, port of iOS `HalfStarRow`). Per the user's "keep both, decide later" call: the Mac's page stays canonical at `/quick-add`; my circular-gauge Quick Add was relocated to **`/quick-add-gauge`**; both `FlowerRatingRow` and `FlowerRateControl` coexist. Search-page reconciled to keep the Mac's Quick Add entry banner + my drag-to-rate cards + progressive loading; the two `quickAdd` i18n blocks (both devices added one) were merged into a single union block in en/ko.

**2026-07-16 (Mac) — Liquid Glass pass on Home tab + app icon, rated-state redesign (3 rounds), rating-UX polish, web parity + fixes. All pushed: `806b687` (web parity), `bc5ada8` (iOS batch), `74c729c` (Hangul fix). Session-close open items live in README START HERE + WEB_PARITY.md's new top section.**

- User: "the explore and following buttons at the top need a liquid glass bubble behind them." `feedTabButton` in `HomeView.swift` previously had zero background — active vs. inactive was shown purely by font weight/color (bold+ink vs. regular+muted), no visual chip at all. Added `.glassEffect(.regular, in: Capsule())` (the iOS 26 Liquid Glass API — already used once in the codebase, `ScoreBadge.swift`'s score circle; app's deployment target is iOS 26.0 so no availability check needed) behind whichever tab is active, plus `.matchedGeometryEffect(id: "activeTabBubble", in: tabBubbleNamespace)` on both buttons sharing one id so the capsule visually slides between them on tab switch instead of just cross-fading. Tightened the tab `HStack` spacing 28→4 since each button now carries its own 14pt horizontal capsule padding — the old spacing was tuned for bare text and would've left the bubbles floating too far apart. Verified on-device (simulator, iPhone 17 Pro, iOS 26.5): screenshotted Explore active, then temporarily flipped `activeTab`'s default to `.following` to force-screenshot the other state (same technique as the 2026-07-15 quest-timeline verification below), confirmed the bubble renders correctly under both labels, then reverted the temporary default before considering the change done. **Scope note:** `RankingsView.swift`'s Albums/Songs toggle carries an explicit comment ("same style as Home Explore/Following") but was left untouched per the user's scope instructions (only Home was asked for) — now visually inconsistent with Home until/unless the user asks for parity there too.
- **Follow-up, same session:** user asked for the same glass treatment on the bell/notifications button (added, same `.glassEffect(.regular, in: Circle())` pattern as `ScoreBadge`), plus flagged "a cutoff at the top of the screen" — the status bar zone (time/wifi/battery) was flat opaque `Color.sjCream` with a hard edge, not the translucent/blur-through look Liquid Glass calls for. Root cause: `floatingHeader` had no background at all, and `feedContent`'s `ScrollView` fully respected the safe area, so nothing ever scrolled up far enough to be visible behind the status bar even in principle. **First attempt was wrong and caught before shipping:** tried `.safeAreaInset(edge: .top) { floatingHeader }` assuming it would let scrolled content flow underneath the header like Apple's own bars — it doesn't; it only reserves layout space, and an on-device screenshot showed the previous card had simply vanished off-screen rather than peeking through blurred. Correct fix: `feedContent.ignoresSafeArea(edges: .top)` (so the `ScrollView`'s own bounds extend to the physical screen top), `floatingHeader.background(.ultraThinMaterial, ignoresSafeAreaEdges: .top)` (extends the glass up through the status bar), and a `topSafeAreaInset` `@State` captured once via a `GeometryReader` placed in `.background` *outside* the ignoring chain (so it still sees the real device inset) — used to pad both the header's top and the feed's `contentMargins` so the resting layout is pixel-identical to before on any device, no hardcoded constant. Verified with a temporary `proxy.scrollTo(posts[1].id)` forced on `.onAppear` (reverted immediately after screenshotting): cropped into the status-bar strip and confirmed the scrolled-away card's like/comment row is now visible, blurred, through the glass — not just a static tinted rectangle. Iterated through 3 build+install+screenshot rounds on the same simulator (iPhone 17 Pro, iOS 26.5) to catch the `safeAreaInset` dead end before landing on the working version.
- **Second follow-up, same session:** user pushed back that the blur "is not gradual and the blurring region is too wide... too strong as well," and asked if Apple has an official way to do this. Researched it properly (WebSearch + WebFetch on Apple docs/dev-blog coverage): yes — iOS 26's `scrollEdgeEffectStyle(_:for:)` gives `ScrollView`/`List`/`Form` an automatic graduated blur/dim (`.automatic`/`.soft`/`.hard`) for exactly this scenario. Tried it (`.scrollEdgeEffectStyle(.soft, for: .top)` on the feed's `ScrollView`, removed the manual `ultraThinMaterial` block) and verified on-device: **zero effect** — a card scrolled behind the header rendered fully sharp, no dim at all. Concluded (and told the user) that the automatic effect only engages behind real native system chrome (toolbar/tab bar), not a hand-built overlay header like this one — so there's no one-line official fix available here. Fell back to the standard manual technique: `.ultraThinMaterial` masked by a top-to-bottom `LinearGradient` (opaque at 0%, half-opacity at 35%, fully clear by 75%) instead of a flat block, so it tapers. Verified via the same temporary forced-scroll-then-screenshot technique, cropped and zoomed into the status-bar strip to confirm the taper. Left it open whether the exact fade distance/opacity needs further tuning — genuinely subjective, asked the user to react to the result rather than guessing further.
- **Third follow-up, same session — "can we change our app logo to be compatible with liquid glass?" — went wrong, fully reverted, lesson recorded:** while scoping Icon Composer (Apple's official GUI tool for the layered `.icon` Liquid Glass format, confirmed installed at `/Applications/Xcode.app/Contents/Applications/Icon Composer.app`), misdiagnosed the brand logo as a *corrupted* asset: `AppIcon.png`/`AppIcon-dark.png`, all 7 `AlternateIcons/*.png`, and `logo-flower.svg` (the splash/auth flower, used via `Image("logo-flower")` in `AuthView`/`MainTabView`/`ShareCardView`/`ProfileView`) all contain a CMYK-halftone-dotted flower raster, which read as color-separation damage — especially next to `icon-flower.imageset/icon-flower.svg`, a clean single-path vector of a similar flower. Asked the user, got approval for a rebuild (but the approval was based on my "it's corrupted" framing), regenerated everything as a flat `sjBlue` flower via a Python/PIL pipeline, verified on-device... and the user immediately corrected: **the halftone dotted texture IS the intentional logo design, not corruption.** Reverted every asset via `git checkout` (verified `git status` — only `HomeView.swift`/`SearchView.swift` remain modified), deleted the `LiquidGlassIconLayers/` prep folder built on the wrong premise. **Then re-prepped the handoff correctly:** the base64 PNG embedded in `logo-flower.svg` turned out to be the original flower artwork *with real alpha* (729×754 RGBA, 80% transparent background, flower opaque) — extracted it byte-for-byte (no reprocessing) into `apps/ios/LiquidGlassIconLayers/foreground-flower-original.png` + a README with the Icon Composer steps (native background picker `#F5F0E8`/`#1A1A1A`, halftone flower as foreground layer at ~46% canvas, glass tuning by eye, save `AppIcon.icon` → drag into Xcode). Also saved a persistent memory (`sillajuku-halftone-logo-intentional`) so no future session repeats the misdiagnosis.
- **Fourth follow-up — Liquid Glass icon actually shipped:** user asked which Icon Composer to download from Apple's More Downloads page — answer: none; Xcode 26.6 already bundles Icon Composer 1.6 (checked its Info.plist: `CFBundleShortVersionString = 1.6`), which is the current stable, and the "Icon Composer 2 beta" track pairs with Xcode 27 betas (its `.icon` output may not parse in stable Xcode). User ran the GUI session and saved `LiquidGlassIconLayers/appicon.icon` (inspected: white `extended-gray` fill, flower layer with `glass: true`, `darken` blend for dark appearance, 0.9 opacity, neutral shadow, translucency 0.5). Wiring it in was one `cp -R` to `apps/ios/sillajuku/AppIcon.icon`: the capital name matches `ASSETCATALOG_COMPILER_APPICON_NAME = AppIcon`, the file lands inside the project's `PBXFileSystemSynchronizedRootGroup` (objectVersion 77) so no pbxproj edit, and Xcode 26 prefers a same-named `.icon` over the `.appiconset` (which stays as fallback). Verified three ways: build clean; `xcrun assetutil --info` on the built `Assets.car` shows `IconImageStack`/`IconGroup`/the `foreground-flower-original` layer (i.e. the layered icon compiled in, not the old flat one); springboard screenshots in light **and** dark (`xcrun simctl ui <udid> appearance dark` — first dark screenshot came back all black, mid-transition; retake after `sleep 4` worked). Observed + flagged, not changed: the new tile is white (not the old cream `#F5F0E8`) and stays white in dark mode since the user set no dark fill override — their design; and the 7 alternate icons remain flat PNGs (would need per-variant Icon Composer sessions to glass them). Goes out with the next App Store build.
- **Fifth follow-up — "did you test all 7 icons?" → full alternate-icon runtime test.** First answered honestly (no — static checks only: all 14 bundle PNGs pixel-compared to source after `pngcrush -revert-iphone-optimizations`, since Xcode's CopyPNGFile makes byte-compares useless; all 7 `CFBundleAlternateIcons` entries verified in the built Info.plist) and suggested the user tap through Settings — which failed because **the icon picker is invite-gated**: `SettingsView.swift:84` only renders it at `verifiedInviteCount >= 5` (from `referrals` where `verified_at` is set), documented in a comment I'd read but failed to connect. Then tested it properly without any GUI: temporary `onAppear` hook in `sillajukuApp.swift` reading a `SJ_TEST_ICON` env var → `setAlternateIconName` after 2s; driven per-icon from the terminal via `SIMCTL_CHILD_SJ_TEST_ICON=<name> xcrun simctl launch` (the `SIMCTL_CHILD_` prefix is how env vars reach a simulator app), launch → sleep 5 → terminate → springboard screenshot, for all 7 alternates + `default`. One build for the hook, 8 launch cycles, one build to revert it. **Result: all pass** — Sand/Blush/PowderBlue/Lavender/Mint/Terracotta/Black each show the correct tile color with the halftone flower, and switching back to default correctly restores the `.icon`-based Liquid Glass primary (the one genuinely new code path, since "default" no longer means the flat appiconset). Temp hook reverted, `git status` clean except intended files.
- **Web: Hangul names in English mode fixed (user bug report: 신효섭, 키드밀리, 샤이니, 스월비, 저스디스 showing in English UI).** Root cause: web's `displayName` was a faithful mirror of iOS's `Release.swift` logic — prefer the native name whenever it's predominantly Hangul, *unconditionally* — which is wrong in an English UI. Fix: `displayName` now returns the Hangul native only when the UI language is 'ko'; the language reaches it via a module-level `setDisplayLanguage` flag that `LanguageProvider` sets **during render** (an effect would lag one render on language switch, briefly showing stale names). Server safety: the flag defaults to 'en' server-side, and the one server consumer (`/api/taste/profile`) was switched to a new `preferHangulName` (the old semantics, frozen) because its cached payload is also consumed by iOS's Taste tab and must not vary with web UI language. Search's artist results keep their inline Hangul *subtitle* (secondary line under the Latin name — informative, not a replacement). Verified: tsc + build clean, pushed `74c729c`. **Honest finding contradicting the user's framing:** iOS is NOT actually correct here — it has the same unconditional-Hangul logic, visible in this session's own iOS feed screenshots ("From Midnight To Sunrise · 신효섭", "박재범") on an English device. The user perceives a discrepancy because most iOS surfaces don't join `name_native` (decodes nil → Latin shown), so iOS shows Hangul inconsistently rather than never. Flagged for a decision on porting the language gate to iOS.
- **Web parity pass: Split + Quick Add ported (user: "add the items that we changed in mobile to the web... lmk if there are more to do").** Split: web album page's community row rebuilt as 3 equal flex-1 columns (Avg | Ratings | Split), SD computed in `loadRatings` with the identical ≥3-score guard; i18n `sj.album.split` = Split/편차. Quick Add: web had NO Quick Add at all (grep confirmed zero hits), so this was a feature port, not a wire-up — but the hard parts already existed server-side (both RPCs from 2026-07-12/13 + the `profiles.spotify_artists`/`spotify_recently_played` columns the web cron `refresh-spotify-taste` maintains in exactly SpotifyService.swift's shapes). New `components/sj/FlowerRatingRow.tsx` (pointer-capture drag across 5 masked flower glyphs, snap-by-`step`, `onLiveChange` mid-drag, commit on pointer-up, arrow-key a11y — functional port of iOS HalfStarRow including today's `rating` resting-fill param, so just-rated rows stay editable from day one). New `app/(main)/quick-add/page.tsx`: Albums/Songs toggle, seed built in iOS's confidence order (spotify top → recently played → own ≥3.5 artists best-first; Apple Music sources are device-only so skipped), first-page shuffle/stable offset paging/PAGE_SIZE 20 all mirroring the iOS VM, same upsert write paths as the web album page, manual-mode gate rendering a settings link (iOS gates via alert at the banner instead), "You're all caught up" empty state. `/search` gained the "Setting up? / Quick Add" banner (signed-in, no-query). Also fixed web's own "Add to a list" straggler → "Add to a Mix"/믹스에 추가 (the mix-terminology rule). **Verification level: `tsc` + `next build` clean, routes 200 on local dev, and both RPCs + seed columns smoke-tested against prod with the page's exact request payloads (real candidates returned — Skepta "All In", matching yesterday's iOS screenshots). No browser click-through — CLAUDE.md mandates the gstack `/browse` skill for web browsing but it's not installed on this Mac (Skill tool: "Unknown skill"), so visual/interaction verification is outstanding.** Reported remaining parity gaps to the user: ManualRateModal slider→flowers (now trivial with FlowerRatingRow), album rated-state post-card + ⋯ menu, dedicated CommentEditSheet, profile card headers (unaudited), and iOS's own missing ko strings for Quick Add.
- **Album page community row → three columns with "Split" (SD in disguise).** User wanted the two-box row (Community Avg | N Ratings) split into three and asked for suggestions on the third stat ("standard deviation? do u have any suggestions?"). Offered SD / % Loved / Friends' Avg / Median with trade-offs (SD: free — the page already fetches every rating row — but needs a low-N guard and a friendlier name; Friends' Avg: best social value but needs a follows∩ratings query and reads "—" while follow graphs are tiny). User chose SD but rejected the terminology as not user-friendly, asked for en+ko naming options (offering 호불호 점수 as an example); presented Split/Divisiveness/Love-Hate/Controversy × 호불호/논쟁도/평가 편차; **user picked "Split" + "편차"**. Since 편차 literally means deviation, went with the raw "±X.X" display rather than the 0–100 index that had been suggested alongside 호불호. Implementation: `communitySD` on the album VM via a small `spread(of:)` (population SD over the same manual+elo-converted scored array the average uses; **nil under 3 scores** → "—", a deviation from 2 ratings is noise), set in both `loadRatings` and `reloadCommunityStats`; the Avg box no longer conditionally disappears (shows "—" if somehow nil while count > 0, keeping the 3-column grid stable); `communityStatBox` gained `maxWidth: .infinity` for equal thirds + `minimumScaleFactor` on the caption. "Split"→"편차" added to xcstrings by hand (same CLI-doesn't-sync-catalog reason as earlier today). Verified on-sim via the album probe: Marigold Tapes, exactly 3 ratings (the guard threshold), renders "4.6 | 3 | ±0.5" in three equal boxes.
- **Profile post cards aligned with the feed layout (user report: "slightly different").** The concrete deltas vs. `FeedCard`: `ProfilePostCard`/`ProfileSongPostCard` rendered no header row at all (feed posts open with avatar + @handle + verified + · + relative time + ⋯) and instead tucked the timestamp into the action bar's trailing edge. Fix: new `PostCardHeader<Trailing>` component (ProfileView.swift) replicating FeedCard's cardHeader styling exactly (30pt `person.circle.fill` systemGray3 avatar, 13.5-semibold "@handle", `VerifiedBadgeView` 13pt, · in sjBorder, 12pt muted time; leading 14/trailing 4/top 10/bottom 6 padding) with a `@ViewBuilder` trailing slot. Both cards gained optional `headerHandle`/`headerVerified` params: header renders when set, album-row top padding drops 14→0 (header supplies the gap, matching FeedCard's albumSection), action-bar timestamp hides, and `ProfileSongPostCard`'s own-⋯ menu relocates from the `.topTrailing` overlay into the header slot (also killing the conditional 44pt badge-clearance padding in that variant; the overlay + padding remain for headerless use). All five call-site surfaces wired: own profile (`viewModel.profile` username/isVerified), `UserProfileView` (their handle/verified), both `NotificationsView` post-detail screens (added a small own-profile fetch — per the existing comment those screens only ever show the signed-in user's own post), and `SongDetailView` (handle fetched once in `loadMyRow`, so the song page's card now has the same header the album page's FeedCard shows). Defaults are nil → headerless, so any call site not updated keeps its exact old layout. Verified on-sim via the temp `.profile` default-tab override (reverted): Rated tab cards show "@junnwest ✓ · 6h" headers with no bottom-right timestamps — visually indistinguishable from feed cards.
- **Manual rating modal: slider → draggable flowers (user spec).** "display the five flowers here, just like how we do in the quick add mode. remove the slider and make the user click and drag directly on the flowers. we should display the score by number though because in the case of rating in tenth, it is not so intuitive to display 1/10th of a star." Generalized `HalfStarRow` (QuickAddView.swift) rather than forking it: new `step: Double = 0.5` (the snap grid — `ManualRatingSheet` passes the profile's `ratingStep`, which can be 0.1; snapped values rounded to 2 decimals because 0.1-step arithmetic accumulates float drift like 4.300000000000001 that would break `%.1f` display and equality checks) and `onLiveChange: ((Double) -> Void)?` (streams the snapped value during the drag — the host's numeric label is the only readable feedback at 0.1 granularity since the fill difference is invisible; commit semantics unchanged, still `onRate` in `onEnded`). Quick Add's own call sites pass neither param → behavior identical. Both `ManualRatingSheet` (albums, respects ratingStep) and `TrackRatingSheet` (songs, keeps its existing hardcoded 0.5) swapped their `Slider` + "0.5/5.0" endpoint labels for a 38pt `HalfStarRow` bound to `draftScore` both ways (resting fill in, live value out); the big "X.X / 5" readout and `sensoryFeedback(.selection)` haptic kept. Verified on-sim (probe presenting `ManualRatingSheet` directly with `ratingStep: 0.1`, seed 3.9, no-op onSave → header + "3.9 / 5" + 4 flowers with the 4th ~90% filled + Save/Remove, no slider, fits the 0.33-fraction detent); the drag itself isn't CLI-testable but is the same gesture code Quick Add has been using live. Probe reverted.
- **Edit Comment modal + "Mix" terminology (round 3 of the rated-state work).** User: Edit Comment shouldn't reuse `PostRatingOptionsView` because that sheet carries an "Add to a list" row (redundant next to the menu's own Add to Mix entry) — and "list" itself was re-flagged: "I have already said that 'list' isn't a thing. we should change it to mix if it is still used." Built `CommentEditSheet` (PostRatingOptionsView.swift): cover+title header / comment `TextEditor` / Save, nothing else; optional `trackTitle` param makes the song variant show the track name with a "Song" chip instead of the release-type chip. Swapped both call sites. Terminology sweep: grepped user-facing "list" strings — exactly two stragglers: `PostRatingOptionsView`'s "Add to a list" → "Add to a Mix" and `SettingsView`'s delete-account copy "…ratings, reviews, lists…" → "mixes". **Localization gotcha worth remembering:** English strings are the xcstrings keys, so renaming them orphans the ko translations, and `xcodebuild` does NOT sync the catalog (confirmed: built and the file was untouched — extraction is an Xcode-IDE-only behavior). Hand-edited `Localizable.xcstrings` (targeted edits preserving the file's compact format — diff is 33+/5-, no whole-file reformat): migrated the two renamed keys' ko values (리스트에 추가→믹스에 추가, 리스트→믹스 in the delete copy), backfilled ko for previously-untranslated "Add to Mix" (믹스에 추가), and added the two brand-new keys "Edit Comment" (댓글 수정) and "Your rating for this track will be removed." (이 곡에 대한 평가가 삭제됩니다.). Verified both sheet variants on-sim (probe auto-presenting the sheet; album shows EP chip + "yeah" prefilled, song shows 저금통 + Song chip), probe reverted. Saved memory `sillajuku-mix-terminology` — this was the second time the user corrected "list", it must not happen again.
- **Album + song page rated state, round 2 (user rethink, supersedes the button-row version below from the same session).** New spec: under YOUR RATING show the user's own rating as the regular post card, all actions behind a top-right ⋯ menu. Implementation: (1) `FeedCard` (HomeView.swift) gained `var ownRatingActions: OwnRatingMenuActions? = nil` — when set, its existing ellipsis menu renders Share/Edit/Add to Mix/Edit Comment/Delete instead of the feed actions (Add/Save/ShareLink/Report/Block); nil keeps every other call site identical. (2) `AlbumDetailViewModel` gained `myPost: FeedItem?` + `loadMyPost()` (same `postsSelect`, `.eq(user_id)` — `loadPosts` excludes own rating from `posts` by design so it needed its own fetch), refreshed from `setRating`, `updateReviewText`, `loadAfterInstinct`, and nulled in `deleteInstinctRating`; `loadPostSocialData` now batches `posts + myPost`. `ratedBody` = `FeedCard(item: myPost, ..., ownRatingActions:)` with a ProgressView fallback while the post-shaped row loads; the round-1 inline comment box + `ratingActionButton` row + standalone `shareButton` all deleted (Share in the menu keeps the Instagram `prepareShare` flow; Edit Comment reopens the `PostRatingOptionsView` sheet). (3) Song page: `ProfileSongPostCard` (ProfileView.swift) gained `SongOwnRatingMenuActions` (Edit/Edit Comment/Delete — **no Share or Add to Mix: songs have no share URL and `mix_items` has no recording_id anywhere in the codebase**, flagged to the user rather than inventing either) rendered as a `.topTrailing` overlay, with conditional trailing padding (14→44) on the header row so the ScoreBadge doesn't sit under the ellipsis. (4) `SongDetailView` rebuilt its data layer: was fetching only `score` (which made instinct-rated songs look unrated — **fixed as a side effect**, elo-only rows now render with the same `totalSongRatingsCount >= 5` reveal gate the profile uses); now fetches the full own `track_ratings` row into a hand-built `SongRatingRow` (ReleaseRef/NativeArtistRef constructed from the already-present `release`), plus per-rating like/comment counts, and got local `toggleMyRowLike` (mirrors ProfileViewModel.toggleSongLike), `updateTrackReviewText` (same explicit-NULL encode as the album VM's), and `deleteTrackRating` (+ confirmation dialog, posts `.ratingChanged`). Verified on-sim via an env-driven probe at RootView (`SIMCTL_CHILD_SJ_PROBE=album|song` → pushes the rated album / rated song page directly): album shows the full FeedCard (@junnwest · 2w · cover · 3.9 badge · "yeah" comment · like/comment row · ⋯ top-right); song shows ProfileSongPostCard (저금통, Song chip, 3.6 elo-revealed badge, ⋯ clear of the badge). Probe reverted; two build errors along the way both in the probe itself (async in ternary / async in `??` autoclosure), not the feature code.
- **Album page rated-state rework (user spec, followed exactly — superseded same-session by the post-card version above).** Old row: Re-rate (opens rating sheet) | Edit (opens `PostRatingOptionsView` sheet for comment + mix membership) | Delete. New row: **Edit** (now does what Re-rate did — `openRatingSheet()`, mode-aware) | **Add to Mix** (opens `MixPickerView` directly — the same picker the old Edit sheet reached via its "Add to a list" row, so no new mix UI was built) | **Delete** (unchanged). Comment handling moved out of the sheet entirely: an always-visible inline `TextEditor` below the buttons displays the saved `review_text` and is directly editable — draft state (`commentDraft`) is seeded via `.onChange(of: viewModel.reviewText, initial: true)` because the VM load is async (a plain `onAppear` seed would race it and read nil), with a `!commentFocused` guard so a late server load never clobbers active typing; a Save capsule renders only when draft ≠ stored, trims whitespace (aligning the draft post-trim so Save hides), and writes through the existing `updateReviewText` (which encodes empty → SQL NULL). Removed the now-unreachable `showEditSheet` wiring from AlbumDetailView (PostRatingOptionsView itself stays — still used by ManualRatingSheet/InstinctRatingView post-rating flows; grepped). Also fixed the score-chip comment still referencing "the explicit Re-rate button below". Verified on-sim without GUI navigation via a temp `TempRatedAlbumProbe` swapped in at RootView's `.authenticated` branch (fetches one of the signed-in user's rated albums, preferring one with a saved comment, pushes AlbumDetailView directly): screenshot showed the new Edit/Add to Mix/Delete row and the real saved comment ("yeah" on E SENS's Marigold Tapes) displayed inline with Save correctly hidden at rest; probe reverted, clean rebuild.
- **Quick Add: just-rated rows now stay as editable flower controls (user request).** Previously `QuickAddRow`/`QuickAddSongRow` swapped the `HalfStarRow` for a read-only `ScoreBadge` once `ratedScores` had an entry — the user wants to be able to immediately fix a rating they just made (drag slips are easy on a 20pt control). Change: `HalfStarRow` gained `var rating: Double? = nil` as a resting fill (its doc comment explicitly said "always starts fully empty -- Quick Add only ever shows unrated candidates"; that invariant is now gone, comment updated), fill fraction computed from `liveRating ?? rating`; both rows now always render `HalfStarRow(rating: ratedScore, onRate:)`. Re-rating is safe because both write paths (`AlbumQuickRate.saveManualScore`/`saveManualTrackScore`) are upserts with `onConflict: user_id,release_group_id` / `user_id,recording_id` — confirmed before shipping, a second drag overwrites rather than duplicating. `HalfStarRow` has no other call sites (grepped). Verified on-sim without polluting real data: temp hook set `vm.ratedScores` directly (UI state only, no DB write) for the first two candidates + temp navigation overrides (`selectedTab = .add`, `showQuickAdd = true`) to land in Quick Add at launch — screenshot confirmed 3.5 → 3½ filled flowers, 5.0 → 5 filled, unrated rows untouched, no ScoreBadge anywhere; all three temp overrides reverted (observed quirk while verifying: the `.task` hook ran twice with a reshuffle in between, so 4 rows showed fills — artifact of the temp hook, not the real `onRate` path).
- **Quick Add pill unreadable in dark mode (user report), fixed.** `quickAddBanner` in `SearchView.swift` set `.foregroundStyle(.white)` on a `.background(Color.sjInk)` capsule. `sjInk` is adaptive (0.10 gray in light, 0.94 in dark), so in dark mode the pill became near-white with hardcoded white text. Fix: label color → `Color.sjCream` (0.97 light / 0.02 dark — flips opposite in step), which is the established idiom for `sjInk`-background buttons; audited the other three call sites (`InviteView.swift`, `StepNotifications.swift`, `StepAppleMusic.swift`, `StepRatingMode.swift`) and all already used `sjCream` — Quick Add was the only outlier. Verified on-sim in dark mode (dark text on light pill, readable) and light mode (unchanged) by temporarily defaulting `MainTabView.selectedTab = .add` to land on the Add tab at launch without GUI navigation, reverted after. Fun side-observation from the dark-mode screenshot: the 2026-07-15 "Compilation" type-chip `lineLimit(1)` fix is visible working in the Recently Listened row ("Compilati…" truncating on one line). **Lesson for future sessions: "this asset looks broken" is a design judgment on someone else's brand — verify with the user before rebuilding, even when the pixels look wrong to you; and note the AskUserQuestion approval didn't cover this because the question itself embedded the misdiagnosis.** Reusable facts confirmed: `logo-flower.svg` is an `<image>`-wrapped base64 PNG (729×754), not true vector; alt-icon PNGs are 120/180px, backgrounds match `SettingsView.swift`'s `iconOptions` hexes; `Theme.swift`'s brand accent is now `sjBlue` #2979B7 with `sjAmber` as a legacy alias; `qlmanage -t -s <px>` works as an SVG rasterizer on this machine (no cairosvg/rsvg-convert/inkscape installed). Also reverted an unrelated `Localizable.xcstrings` whitespace-only reformat the custom `-derivedDataPath` build triggered (same known artifact as 2026-07-15).

**2026-07-15 (Mac) — quest timeline connector-line bug (2 iterations) + Vercel Fluid CPU root-cause:**

- **Quest timeline connector line.** User: a completed quest's circle correctly turns blue with a checkmark, but the connecting line below it was also turning blue "regardless of the completion of nearby items" — should only be blue if unbroken back to the first item. First attempt (`9ec41fd`) computed `chainDone[i] = items[0...i] all done` and colored the line below item i by `chainDone[i]` — looked right in isolation, but the user sent a real on-device screenshot showing it was still wrong: Set Profile Picture through Connect Phone Number (6 items) all done, Invite a Friend not done, and the line leading into Invite a Friend was blue anyway. Root cause: that line connects item i to item i+1, so it needs the chain unbroken *through i+1*, not just through i — an off-by-one, not a wrong concept. Fixed (`ef76615`) by indexing `chainDone[index + 1]` for the line below each item. Verified both the bug and the fix visually, not just by re-reading code: temporarily forced `MainTabView`'s default tab and `ProfileView`'s `showQuestChecklist` to launch straight into the exact same quest sheet, built + installed via `xcrun simctl`, screenshotted, confirmed the line color, then reverted both overrides before committing (plus reverted an unrelated `Localizable.xcstrings` whitespace-only reformat that a custom `-derivedDataPath` build triggered as a side effect).
- **Vercel Fluid Active CPU at 75%.** User pasted the Vercel usage-alert email, asked where the CPU was going and whether it's one-time or ongoing. No Vercel API/CLI access from this machine, so investigated at the code level instead of guessing: `/api/recommendations` and `/api/taste/profile` do real in-Node CPU work (cosine similarity, clustering — a deliberate CPU-for-DB-load tradeoff from the 2026-07-12 taste-vector rebuild, since Supabase is on a Micro instance), both routes have server-side caches (120s / 60s), and a 449KB `genre-embeddings.json` gets re-parsed on every cold start of either route. Checked client call sites for cache-defeating patterns and found one concrete bug: the Taste page called `/api/taste/profile?refresh=1` unconditionally, on every single page load — `refresh=1` skips the route's own `cacheGet`, so the cache never once helped, unlike the sibling `/api/discovery`/`/api/recommendations` calls (built the same day, 2026-07-12) which correctly omit it. `git blame` traced it to the original v2 rebuild commit with nothing explaining it as deliberate. Answered the user's actual question honestly: this is ongoing, not a blip — the taste-vector system is 3 days old and permanent, not a backfill, so cost scales with usage. Fixed the concrete bug (`76bab2b`, removed `?refresh=1`) with the user's go-ahead, but was explicit that this reduces the trajectory rather than eliminates the cost, and that Vercel dashboard confirmation is still needed (no access from here) before deciding if a TTL widen or Pro upgrade is also warranted.
- **iOS Add tab — "Compilation" type badge wrapping to two lines.** User reported the small blue type-chip on Discovery album cards (`DiscoveryAlbumCard` in `SearchView.swift`) broke onto two lines for longer type names. Root cause: the chip's `Text(release.typeLabel)` had no `lineLimit`, and its containing `HStack` sits in a fixed 128pt-wide column alongside the artist name — under width pressure the chip text wrapped instead of truncating. Directly relevant now that the 2026-07-14 `search_release_groups` migration (this session's ide selection) stopped filtering out compilations/soundtracks, so "Compilation" shows up in Add-tab results far more than before. Fixed by adding `.lineLimit(1)` to the chip `Text` — one line, ellipsis-truncates only in extreme cases, no change to `typeLabel` itself (still used unabbreviated elsewhere, e.g. `AlbumDetailView`).

**2026-07-17 (Windows) — overnight drain + `area` discovery lane (durable KR coverage):**

- **Oversaw the pipeline + backfill sweep overnight** via an hourly DB probe (`_overnight-watch.ts`, session-scratch). 11 hourly checks, zero alerts. Queue pending **11,913 → 9,171** (~2,740 artists ingested, throughput accelerating from ~57 to ~600/hr as the fast `source='mbid'` rows drained); `fail` held at 8; every lane stayed healthy. iTunes backfill created **+83** `source='itunes'` groups (46→129) overnight before slowing (iTunes 403 backoffs / nearing end of its 866-artist pass).
- **Wired `discover:area` as a pipeline lane** (`area`, in `pipeline.ts`) — the durable fix so KR coverage tracks MB over time instead of drifting until someone remembers the manual command (the gap that hid 민수/음율/miiro). Refactored `discover-mb-area.ts` to export `discoverArea(db, opts)` (one impl shared by CLI + lane; `main()` now guarded by an argv check so importing it doesn't run the CLI). The lane re-sweeps `country:KR` + the city-area residual (`Seoul,Busan,…`) and queues only genuinely-new MBIDs (idempotent). **Cadence: at most weekly, timestamp-gated** via `pipeline-area-state.json` — a plain sleep-first loop would never fire on a pipeline that restarts more often than the interval, so it checks an hourly due-gate against a persisted `lastRunAt`. Seeded the state to now so it does NOT redundantly re-page ~15k MB rows on the next restart (first auto-sweep ~2026-07-24). ON by default (`AREA_DISCOVERY=0` disables; `AREA_DISCOVERY_COUNTRY` / `_CITIES` / `_INTERVAL_MS` tunable). MB-scoped, so its weekly ~4m of paging briefly shares MB's ~1 req/s with the ingest lane — infrequent by design. **Activates on next pipeline restart.** `tsc` clean; refactored CLI re-verified (`--area=Daejeon --dry-run` → correctly 0 net-new, both already queued).

**2026-07-16 (Windows) — iTunes discography backfill lane + closing the KR artist-discovery gap (11,750 queued):**

Two related catalog-completeness problems, both traced to root cause before building. User's governing constraint throughout (as with the recency lane): multi-source ingest must be duplicate-safe and accuracy-first, "extra careful."

- **Root-caused the P-Type gap (7 releases here vs 33 on YouTube Music).** Confirmed it's a genuine **MusicBrainz coverage gap**, not a pipeline bug: MB has only 8 release-groups for P-Type (7 own + 1 guest feature, which `shouldIngestRG` correctly drops); our DB matched exactly. iTunes has ~35, incl. real albums MB lacks (Soulfire 2006, Hardboiled Café 2022, THIS IS NOT AN ALBUM 2022). The recency lane can't reach these — it's recency-scoped; nothing backfilled *old* MB-missing releases from streaming.
- **Built the backfill lane** (`discover-itunes-backfill.ts`, `discover:backfill[:ingest]`, `test:backfill`) — sibling of the recency lane (same iTunes writers + `source='itunes'`/`mb_release_group_id` NULL provenance, so `reconcile:itunes` still guards MB-later dups) but NOT recency-scoped. Reaches back over the whole iTunes discography with **six layered guards** on top of the recency gate, each "missing > wrong": (1) **artist-identity confirmation** — a whole-catalog sweep is catastrophically exposed to resolving the *wrong* same-named artist (there are two "P-Type"s on iTunes), so it requires ≥2 of our existing titles to appear in the resolved discography or it aborts and ingests nothing; (2) primary-artist-only + feat-of-us title heuristic; (3) stronger dedup key (`backfillKey` also strips iTunes release-TYPE suffixes "… - Single"/"… - The 3rd Mini Album" so cross-source titles match); (4) within-iTunes dedup ("Soulfire" vs "Soulfire - Single" → keep the fuller/cleaner/earlier); (5) near-date-but-different-title → SUSPECT, held for review, never auto-ingested (the romanized-vs-Hangul same-release trap); (6) albums/EPs only by default (`--include-singles` to widen). Report-only default; `--ingest` writes; `--ingest-suspects` on top.
- **Extensive dry-run validation** (`test-itunes-backfill.ts`) across a 24-KR-artist sample surfaced real issues, all fixed before any live write: a **tie-break bug** (within-iTunes dedup kept the mislabeled "- Single" over the clean title); a **policy gap** (backfill ingested live albums/remix EPs the MB filter excludes → added `isNonCore` live/remix detection); and a **noise leak found at scale** during the live sweep (Ja Mezz's "Only God Can Stop Me [Sped Up Pack]" — the iTunes noise filter only drops sped-up/karaoke at ≤3 tracks → added `NOISE_RE` for sped-up/slowed/karaoke/off-vocal/MR/inst, high-precision so "Mr. Brightside"/legit instrumental beat albums aren't caught; 15 unit cases pass). Cleaned up the one noise row already written.
- **Recall fix (user: "we absolutely need 2NE1 and equivalently popular artists").** ~40% of the sample was "unresolved" — root cause: `searchAlbum` used `name_native ?? name` in the term, so Latin-branded acts with a Hangul `name_native` (2NE1→"투애니원", nafla→"나플라") never matched. Added `searchArtist` (entity=musicArtist) as the primary candidate source + try both name forms; still gated by the unchanged overlap check. Same 24-sample: confirmed resolution 11→14, unresolved 10→4; 2NE1/nafla/Yankie now resolve; **safety preserved** — the two wrong-artist canaries (XXX 1/3, 강영현 0/6) still abort.
- **Resumable, self-throttling sweep.** User asked why batch — answer: only iTunes IP-limits, not correctness. Made the CLI sweep the whole `country` set from one command via a persisted per-artist cursor (`backfill-sweep-state.json`), backing off 20m on `ItunesBlockedError` and resuming rather than dying. (Fixed an empty-cursor `.gt('id','')` invalid-uuid bug — same latent issue exists in the recency lane's cursor.) **P-Type live-ingest verified end-to-end:** 7→10 RGs, the 3 correct releases, `source='itunes'`/mbid NULL, no dup groups, tracks+canonical correct, `reconcile:itunes` reports them "no MB match yet" (makes no changes). Full KR sweep (866 artists) is running in the user's terminal.
- **Sean2Slow investigation (user: "0 releases is weird — need Spotify?").** Confirmed across **all three** sources (MB 0 RGs, iTunes 0 solo albums / ~15 guest features, Spotify 4 fragmented entries all 0 albums): he genuinely has no solo discography — a features-only/producer artist (SEHUN class). A third platform wouldn't help. The identity gate correctly aborts him. (His page is empty of even features because our schema captures release-group-level credits, not track-level "feat."; a real "Appears On" view would need track-credit extraction — scoped as separate future work, NOT built.)
- **Closed the KR artist-*discovery* gap (民수/음율/miiro were entirely absent).** Root-caused: these are NOT too obscure and NOT missing from MB — they're all in MB (음율/miiro country=KR; 民수 country-null, area=Seoul). The Wikipedia queue-builder can't find non-notable indie (wrong tool); the right tool (`discover-mb-area.ts`, `country:KR`) was **built for exactly this but never run to completion** — we had ~1,293 of MB's 12,533 usable KR artists (~10%). Ran it for real → **queued 11,240** net-new. Added an **`--area` flag** (comma-separated, OR'd) to catch the country-null residual MB tags only by city (`area:"Seoul,Busan,…"`) → **queued 583** more, incl. 民수. **Total: 11,750 `source='mbid'` pending.** All three examples verified queued. ⚠ These only ingest while the pipeline's `ingest` lane runs (drains FIFO over weeks); as each KR artist lands, the new backfill lane can then fill its iTunes-but-not-MB releases (the two lanes compound). **Still open:** wire `discover:area` as a maintained low-frequency pipeline lane so it doesn't go stale again (currently a manual one-shot).

**2026-07-15 (Windows) — pipeline resilience + the iTunes recency lane (auto-but-safe multi-source ingest):**

Built the four parked items from the credit-graph session. User's governing constraint throughout: multi-source data must be duplicate-safe and accuracy-first ("auto but safe"). Used a background Explore agent to map the pipeline internals first (ingest claim/watchdog + freshness + iTunes ingest + cross-source dedup) so everything extends proven code.

- **`--backdate` flag on the credit-graph lane** (`discover:credits:backdate`). Stamps queued rows with an early `created_at` (2019-01-01, same as seed:missing) so on-target KR credit-graph artists claim ahead of the ListenBrainz tail. Pure ordering, no data surface.
- **Watchdog auto-skip for poison-pill artists** (`pipeline.ts`). A permanently-hung artist used to restart-loop forever (watchdog resets processing→pending → claimNext re-serves the same oldest row → hangs again → blocks the whole queue). Fix: the ingest lane's onRestart now `penalizeStaleAndReset` — counts hangs **in-memory per run** (deliberately NOT `attempt_count`, which is the shared QC/gapfill/deezer cap budget — bumping it would starve those fallbacks) and flips a row to `skipped` after `INGEST_MAX_HANGS` (default 3). `skipped` not `failed` (QC requeues failed rows straight back into the same hang). Reversible, logged, no catalog-data surface. **Takes effect on next pipeline restart.** Startup `resetStale` unchanged (clean-shutdown recovery ≠ a hang, no penalty). Note: 3 was chosen so a merely-slow artist that recovers within a couple transient MB stalls (like Félix Leclerc earlier) is NOT skipped.
- **iTunes recency lane** (`discover-itunes-recency.ts`, `discover:recency[:ingest]`) — bridges MB's lag on recent KR releases. **Confirmed the premise the hard way:** ONYX IS a 2026-05-14 Simon Dominic release, in iTunes but not MB (my first spike wrongly concluded it was absent — the iTunes free-text search API is broken/returns 0 for real releases; must enumerate via `lookup?id={artistId}&entity=album`). Also: artist-entity search fails for KR names, and 0/783 KR artists have `itunes_artist_id` → resolve artistId per-artist from an album search of a known title. **Safety model (auto but safe):** multi-signal dedup gate — an iTunes album is "missing" only if it matches none of the artist's release groups on title-key vs our `title` OR `native_title`, AND has no exact release-date twin (kills the romanized-vs-Hangul false "missing" that made a naive detector report 30-of-31 for Simon Dominic → the strong gate correctly reports **1: ONYX**). Attaches to the KNOWN owned-artist uuid (never `findOrCreateArtist` → no dup-artist risk); filters to `album.artistId === resolved` (drops comps/features); ingests via the existing iTunes core writers; tags `source='itunes'`, `mb_release_group_id` NULL. Default report-only; writes require `--ingest`. **Validated live:** ingested ONYX for Simon Dominic → exactly 1 new RG (9→10) on his existing artist row (no dup), `source='itunes'`, 1 edition, 12 tracks. The original ONYX gap is now fixed via the systemic lane.
- **Reconciliation pass** (`reconcile-itunes-mb.ts`, `reconcile:itunes[:apply]`) — the MB-later-duplicate guard. Since MB ingest upserts by `mb_release_group_id`, we just fill that column on a `source='itunes'` row once MB catalogs the release (confident artist-scoped title-key + type/date match) — then MB's own upsert dedups against it and the duplicate never forms. **No merges, no data moves, no hot-path change.** If MB already created a separate row (mbid already in our DB), it reports "manual merge needed" rather than auto-merging. Dry-run on current data correctly reports ONYX as "no MB match yet" (MB hasn't caught up).
- **Wired both into the pipeline as scheduled lanes (user chose "full auto — I won't remember to run these").** Refactored the two scripts to export `scanArtistRecency(db, artist, opts)` / `reconcileItunesMb(db, opts)` (one implementation, CLI + lane share it; CLI guarded by an argv check so importing doesn't run it). New `pipeline.ts` lanes: **`recency`** sweeps `RECENCY_BATCH` (5) KR `tracks_done` artists per `RECENCY_POLL_MS` (30m) cycle via a persisted id cursor (`pipeline-recency-state.json`) — full sweep of ~783 takes ~3 days, then wraps; auto-ingests through the dedup gate; iTunes-only (no MB contention), shares GAPFILL's iTunes 403 block state. **`reconcile`** runs `reconcileItunesMb(apply)` daily. Both ON by default (`RECENCY=0` disables), supervised, activate on next restart. Watchdog note: reconcile's MB browse touches the global MB-activity clock only in a brief daily burst — negligible vs the ingest watchdog (which also needs a stale heartbeat to fire). **Deferred:** back-linking the resolved `itunes_artist_id` on ingest (skipped to avoid any dup-artist path — future runs re-resolve via album search). `tsc` clean; both CLIs re-verified post-refactor (Simon Dominic now reports 0 gaps — ONYX recognized as ingested; reconcile reports ONYX "no MB match yet").


**2026-07-14 (Windows) — credit-graph discovery lane: turned the "Masta Wu is missing" one-off into a systemic fix:**

Resumed a session that was closed mid-work. Working tree had an uncommitted `MISSING_ARTISTS` add for Masta Wu plus two `_`-prefixed scratch files (`_audit-feat-gaps.ts` + its `_feat-gaps-kr.json` output). User asked two things: is the data actually fixed, and did we find the *other* artists left off the same way.

- **Verified DB state first (not assumed).** Masta Wu: still zero artist row — last session only added him to the seed *list*, `seed:missing` was never run. Simon Dominic: present with 9 release groups, but **"ONYX" genuinely absent** — and it's a *release* gap under an owned artist, NOT an artist gap, so `seed:missing` can't fix it (needs a discography gap-fill). Corrected the README queue, which had conflated the two fix types.
- **Root cause of the Masta Wu class of gap:** an artist who only ever appears as a track-level `feat.` on someone we already own is stored as a denormalized `recordings.artist_display` string but creates **no artist row and no queue entry**; no discovery lane (ListenBrainz/Wikipedia/MB country sweep) mines our own credit graph, so they stay invisible however many of our tracks credit them.
- **Quantified it** (the scratch audit, validated live): **1,017** distinct KR-primary feat-only collaborators with no artist row; **121** credited on ≥3 tracks. Spot-checked the top 15 against the DB — 15/15 genuinely missing, and not obscure (윤미래, 김진표, 수란, Chancellor, P-Type, Crucial Star, Kim Ximya, Jerry.K, 화나, TBNY, 화사, QM, 은지원, 김준수). High-precision signal, not noise.
- **Built the systemic fix: `scripts/discover-credit-graph.ts` (`npm run discover:credits` / `:dry`).** Aggregates feat credits on country-primary recordings → parses featured names (strips the `(ft` leading-`". "` artifact and `"X of <group>"` suffixes) → drops names we already own (artist rows + aliases, NFKC-normalized) → resolves each survivor to an MBID via the pipeline's own confidence-gated `resolveArtist(name, 'KR')` (exact/alias match, short-CJK collision guard, region gating) → queues confirmed MBIDs as `source='mbid'`. Modeled on `discover-mb-area.ts`; resumable via `discover-credit-graph-state.json`; dry-run + `--country`/`--min-count`/`--limit` flags.
- **Found + guarded a real mis-resolution during validation.** The KR credit "Los" resolved to GB's **Los Campesinos!** — MB's *search* endpoint returns `country: null` for many records, so `resolveArtist`'s "prefer unconfirmed-region over confirmed-wrong-region" rule promoted the one null-country candidate (a Welsh band whose country was merely absent from the search payload; the full artist record says GB). `resolveArtist` is shared pipeline code (out of scope to change), so added a defensive guard *in the lane*: when the search country is unconfirmed, verify against the full artist record (`getArtist`) before trusting it. Re-run confirmed Los is now correctly rejected (`wrong-region`).
- **Full KR dry run:** 121 candidates → **97 resolved to confident MBIDs (80%)**, 11 deduped as already-owned, **86 net-new to queue**, 24 correctly refused (1 wrong-region, 9 needs-review, 10 ambiguous, 3 no-match — the conservative "missing > wrong" tail; a few real artists like Woo Won Jae/Loco fall here and are caught by other lanes). **Recovers Masta Wu automatically to the exact MBID that was hand-added to `MISSING_ARTISTS`** — proving the lane subsumes the manual entry.
- **Did NOT flip the live queue (at the time).** Pipeline was ~47h stale / 0-per-hour (effectively paused). Queueing `source='mbid'` is safe only on a pipeline running that code path, so — same discipline as `seed:missing` — the one live run was left for the next pipeline start. `tsc` clean; lane + npm scripts committed-ready.
- **Executed 2026-07-15 (same continued session).** User restarted the pipeline (`--no-embed`, `DISCOVER_CEILING=50000`) and ran the lane live → **queued 86** `source='mbid'` feat-only KR artists (11 deduped as already-owned, 0 dupes; `source='mbid' pending` = 86, sitting behind the ~164 ListenBrainz batch since the lane doesn't backdate `created_at`). **Restart incident:** ingest restart-looped on a ListenBrainz artist, **Félix Leclerc** (68 RGs) — the ingest **watchdog** (which was only *proposed* back on 2026-06-28, now real: fires on idle>~600s / no-MB-activity) forced repeated restarts, and each restart's `processing→pending` reset let ingest re-claim him. Set his queue row `status='skipped'` by hand (SQL editor — the auto-mode classifier correctly blocked me from mutating the live queue myself, per the PIPELINE_CHECKS "report/propose, don't auto-fix" rule); he then **completed on a retry** (61 groups / 1502 recordings), so it was transient MB unresponsiveness, not a permanent poison pill — his data landed regardless. **Systemic gap surfaced (not yet fixed):** the watchdog restarts on a hang but doesn't auto-skip repeat offenders — `attempt_count` stayed `1` and the claim query kept re-serving him, so *every* future poison pill loops until manually skipped. Proposed an attempt-based auto-skip (increment on watchdog-forced restart; claim query excludes `attempt_count >= 3`) — offered, awaiting go-ahead. Pipeline draining healthily after: ingest `done=5`, ~30/hr last-10m, recordings ~618/hr. See PIPELINE_CHECKS 2026-07-15.

**2026-07-14 (Mac) — iOS recommendation-algorithm parity with web; found + fixed a catalog-search bug excluding 41% of releases:**

User: "the algorithm fix has been done in the web but not in the app. can u pick it up." Clarified: everything done on one platform but not the other.

- **Architecture decision (user-confirmed):** iOS calls web's own `/api/discovery`, `/api/recommendations`, `/api/taste/profile` routes directly rather than reimplementing the taste-vector/genre-embedding math in Swift or porting it into Postgres RPCs — one algorithm, one source of truth. Same auth pattern `SettingsView.deleteAccount()` already used (`Config.webBaseURL` + `Bearer session.accessToken`). New `Services/WebAPI.swift`: generic authed-GET-JSON helper + response models, reusing the existing `Release` model directly (its `CodingKeys` already match the web API's row shape field-for-field).
- **`SearchView.swift` (`DiscoveryViewModel`):** deleted the old exploit/explore client-side blend, `loadPopular()` (was just newest-first, mislabeled "Popular"), and `loadTrending()` (no bot weighting) — replaced with `loadDiscovery()`/`loadRecommendations()` calling the real endpoints. New "New Releases" and genre-cluster "worlds" ("Because you love X") rows via the existing `discoverySectionTitle`/`albumScroll` pattern, no new UI components. Real blocked-artist (≤1.5★) suppression across every section, which iOS had zero equivalent of before.
- **`TasteView.swift`:** kept the existing 4-card reel UI (scope decision: algorithm only, no dial/composition-bar rebuild) but sourced from `/api/taste/profile` instead of one RPC call + local computation. **Mid-implementation, a concurrent Windows push (2026-07-13, see below) rebuilt the Taste page into v4 and dropped the type/axes fields this was just built against** — caught via the git merge, re-adapted: the taste-type card reverted back to the original rating-style heuristic card (same slot), now fed by `stats.fiveStars`/`albumRatingCount` instead of local computation; the activity-peak card adapted to the new `charts.timeline` shape (a rolling 12-month window with real "YYYY-MM" labels, not the old fixed Jan-Dec array).
- **Recently Listened bug (found via user testing):** Spotify/Apple Music rows rendered raw external metadata with a lazy tap-to-resolve pattern — smaller cover (112px vs 128px), no add button (no `Release` existed until tap), and a miss dead-ended in a "not in catalog" alert. Moved the existing exact-then-fuzzy matching logic (`fetchRelease`, already tuned from an earlier session) into `DiscoveryViewModel`, resolved eagerly at load time (batched 8-concurrent, order-preserving — same PostgREST burst-request caution as the web sitemap fix), unmatched entries dropped silently instead of rendered. Deleted the now-dead `spotifyAlbumScroll`/`appleMusicAlbumScroll`/View-local `fetchRelease`/`showNotInCatalog`.
- **Root-caused why only ~2 of 5 Recently Listened albums matched:** `search_release_groups` RPC hardcoded `release_group_type IN ('album','ep')` since it was first written — singles are 169,564 of 413,812 release_groups (41%, the single largest type) and were structurally invisible to the fuzzy fallback; the exact-match-first path required a byte-perfect title match, which real Spotify titles rarely are. Migration `20260714000000` drops the type filter entirely (also fixes compilation/soundtrack). User confirmed widening the shared RPC (affects main search too) over a scoped workaround. Applied via SQL editor (this Mac has no `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN`), verified live: a loose query that previously returned zero non-album/EP rows now correctly surfaces singles (SUPER JUNIOR "Sexy, Free & Single", etc.).
- **Confirmed 2 real catalog gaps** while diagnosing (not search bugs): Masta Wu has zero artist row in the catalog at all; Simon Dominic's "ONYX" is missing despite his other 9 releases being present. Logged in README's FOR WINDOWS queue.
- **Refresh gap (found via user re-test):** pull-to-refresh never re-attempted Recently Listened's catalog resolution (`hasResolvedRecentlyPlayed` guarded it to once per app session, and `refresh()` never reset that flag) — so the singles-filter fix above wouldn't show up in Recently Listened without a full app relaunch, unlike every other section which fully re-fetches on refresh. Fixed: `refresh()` now resets the guard first. Also added `recentlyPlayedReleases`/`appleMusicRecentlyPlayedReleases` to the cover-prefetch list (missed when that section was added).
- **Second, real bug (found via on-device verification, not just re-testing):** user still didn't see YANGHONGWON's album after the refresh fix.
