Rather than guess again, actually built + installed + drove the app on-device (`xcrun simctl` + a temporary `FileHandle`-based debug trace written into the app's Documents directory, since `print()`/`log stream` weren't reliably capturing output for this process) to see the real state. Proved the matching pipeline itself was correct — the album resolved and matched on every run. The actual bug: switching Recently Listened onto the shared `albumScroll` component (for a consistent cover/add-button) also silently picked up that component's "hide already-rated releases" filter, appropriate for recommendation sections but wrong for a listening *history* — confirmed via direct query that the album had in fact been rated (Instinct mode, 4 days earlier). `albumScroll` now takes a `hideRated` flag (default `true`, existing callers unaffected); Recently Listened passes `false` and shows a checkmark instead of a misleading "+" for prior-session ratings. Verified visually via simulator screenshot post-fix (temporarily defaulting the launch tab to Add for the check, reverted after).
- **Verified:** iOS build clean at every step (multiple full `xcodebuild` runs), CI green on both jobs after each push. Web/DB changes verified live via direct REST calls (discovery response shape, RPC constraint names, search results before/after the migration).

**2026-07-13 (Windows) — Taste page rebuilt as a graphical analysis report, MBTI 4-letter type scrapped:**

User request: "rebuild the taste page… scrap the 4 alphabet mbti reference, I was thinking of a graphical analysis report of musical taste."

- **API (`/api/taste/profile`, cache `taste:profile:v4`).** Dropped `type` (the 16-type E/F–N/T–M/U–W/S code + composable name) and the `axes` object. Added a `charts` block computed from the same single ratings fetch (no new DB work): `decades` (release-decade histogram, contiguous zero-filled), `scoreDist` (10 half-star buckets), `scenes` (kr/jp/west/other counts by primary-artist country via `sceneOf`), `timeline` (last 12 *calendar* months of rating activity — the old `months` array bucketed by month-of-year across all years, so "peak month" mixed 2025 Jan with 2026 Jan). `stats` gained `meanYear/sdYears/prestigeShare` (previously buried inside `axes`). Worlds/standings/disliked/topAlbum/upsert unchanged — iOS reads `user_taste_profiles`, not this route, so the payload change is web-only.

Each chart keeps a rule-based plain-language sentence (reused `eraWide/Narrow`, `scoreWide/Narrow`, `reachText` strings; new `sceneLeadText`).

Replaced with the validated fixed-order 5-slot palette, separate light/dark steps, swapped via `.taste-report{--viz-1..5}` / `.dark .taste-report{...}` CSS vars; scenes use fixed slots 1–4 (color follows the entity). Both modes re-validated against the app's real surfaces (#fff / #111): pass (light-mode aqua/yellow are sub-3:1 contrast, which is legal here because every series is also named in a legend or direct label). **2026-07-13 (Windows) — web infinite-loading root cause: auth-lock deadlock in `SessionContext` (NOT a cache problem):**

User report: web content "takes infinite time to load; on hard refresh it sometimes works but breaks again next load." Diagnosed as a Supabase auth-lock **deadlock**, not caching.

- **Mechanism.** The whole app gates on `useSession().ready`; the home feed's `load()` only runs `if (ready)` and `loading` starts `true`, so "infinite skeleton" ⟺ `ready` never flips true. `ready` was being set *inside* the `onAuthStateChange` callback, **after** `await loadProfile(uid)` (a `supabase.from('profiles')` query). Confirmed `@supabase/auth-js` **2.103.2** uses `navigatorLock` (`navigator.locks`) by default: the callback fires while auth-js holds the session-token lock, and the awaited query tries to re-acquire the same lock → it queues behind the callback that's awaiting it → deadlock → the `await` never resolves → `setReady(true)` never runs → app stuck on the loading skeleton. This is the documented "don't await Supabase calls inside `onAuthStateChange`" footgun.
- **Why it was intermittent.** Two paths raced on load — `getSession().then()` and `onAuthStateChange`'s `INITIAL_SESSION` — both fetching the profile and setting `ready`. Whether the callback's inner query deadlocked depended on lock-acquisition timing between them, so it was nondeterministic (a sticky cache bug would be consistent — the tell that it wasn't caching). The server-side caching layer (`/api/feed`, Redis/CDN) never applied here: the hang is in the client auth bootstrap, *before* `load()` (and any cached route) is ever called.
- **Fix** (`components/sj/SessionContext.tsx`, no migration, web-only): the `onAuthStateChange` callback is now synchronous and only does `setUserId(session?.user?.id ?? null)` — no awaited Supabase call inside the lock. A **separate** effect keyed on `userId` fetches the profile off the lock and flips `ready` once it settles (signed-out → immediate). This also removes the redundant `getSession()` + `onAuthStateChange` double-fetch (`INITIAL_SESSION` delivers the initial session on subscribe). `ready` still waits for the profile before the onboarding-redirect effect can act, so no signed-in user is bounced to `/onboarding` against a not-yet-loaded profile. `tsc` clean (source-side; the only `tsc` errors are pre-existing stale `.next/types` route stubs).
- **Shipped:** pushed to `main` (auto-deploys via Vercel). The same deadlock shape does not exist elsewhere (grep: `onAuthStateChange` is only registered here).

**2026-07-12/13 (Mac) — App Store submission completed; Quick Add shipped (Albums/Songs subtabs) then two real bugs fixed from live feedback; Profile-page swipe overhaul; a disk-usage investigation:**

**App Store submission completed.** Finished the full App Store Connect metadata Q&A begun in an earlier session (build 6 already exported/screenshotted): promotional text, description, keywords, copyright ("© 2026 Jun-Seo Yang"), Routing App Coverage File (N/A), App Review sign-in notes, release timing, the full App Privacy Data Collection questionnaire (per-type purpose/linked-to-identity/tracking answers for Name/Email/Photos/Other User Content/User ID/Device ID/Product Interaction/Crash Data/Performance Data), Privacy Policy URL, Subtitle, Content Rights, all 7 Age Rating steps (calculated 18+ from honest answers about UGC/profanity/no under-13 social gating), App Encryption Documentation, Pricing/Availability. **Submitted for Apple review** — no further action needed until a decision arrives.

**Quick Add: Albums/Songs subtab split + redesigned entry banner.** User asked for the same Explore/Following-style subtab split Home already has, applied to Quick Add. `RankingsView`'s existing Albums/Songs `ChartMode` toggle was already this exact pattern (same `HStack(spacing: 28)` tab buttons, same `TabView(selection:).tabViewStyle(.page(indexDisplayMode: .never))` swipe wiring) — copied directly rather than reinventing it. Songs had no candidate pipeline before this (song search was a raw client-side two-hop join). New `get_quick_add_song_candidates` RPC (migration `20260712000009`) mirrors the existing `get_quick_add_candidates` album RPC's shape exactly — artist-name-array match, `NOT EXISTS` exclusion (against `track_ratings` this time, keyed on `recording_id`), priority-ordered, offset-paginated — joined through `release_tracks → releases (is_canonical) → release_groups` since `recordings` carries no cover/prestige of its own. New `AlbumQuickRate.saveManualTrackScore` (`Components/AlbumContextMenu.swift`) mirrors the existing album-rating write helper, writing to `track_ratings`. `QuickAddViewModel` restructured into fully separate per-mode state (`albumCandidates`/`songCandidates`, independent `isLoading*`/`isLoadingMore*`/`hasMore*` flags) so pagination on one tab can never cross-contaminate the other. Entry banner redesigned per explicit spec: "Setting up?" hook text + a standalone "Quick Add" pill button on the right (previously the whole card was one big tappable button with an icon circle + chevron).

**Bug 1 — the new song RPC timed out live (57014) on first real verification call, not just design-reviewed.** Following the session's established "verify live, don't assume" discipline, ran a real `curl` against `get_quick_add_song_candidates` right after the user applied the migration — it timed out. Diagnosed by comparing against why the *album* RPC never had this problem: `recordings` (3.16M rows) has an index on `primary_artist_id` but **none on `artist_display`**, so `WHERE r.artist_display = ANY(p_artist_names)` forced a full sequential scan of the entire table before the 3-way join and `DISTINCT ON` sort could even start. `release_groups` (the album table) has an equivalent trigram index (`idx_release_groups_display_trgm`) and is ~10× smaller — that's why the album-side RPC was fine without one. Fixed with a single `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY idx_recordings_artist_display` (migration `20260712000011`, applied as its own SQL-editor statement since `CONCURRENTLY` can't run inside a transaction block). Verified live: 0.67s → 0.33s on repeat calls (previously 57014 every time), and confirmed already-rated tracks (checked against the test account's real `track_ratings` rows for E SENS) are correctly excluded.

**User feedback round after actually using it — 3 real issues, all fixed and verified live:**
1. **"Star icons are inconsistent with the rest of the app — maybe make them the flower icon trace."** Checked first: `HalfStarRow`'s SF Symbol `"star"` turned out to be the *only* star-based control anywhere in the app — `ScoreBadge` and `ManualRatingSheet` both use the `icon-flower` mark (a slider for the latter, no stars at all). Swapped to the same `icon-flower` asset, template-rendered and outlined in `sjBorder`, same half-tap gesture logic.
2. **"Albums: there are too few/narrow suggestions — only 2 artists and 15 albums."** Checked the real data before assuming it was a code bug: the test account's `profiles.spotify_artists`/`spotify_recently_played` already had **7** distinct artists (Fred again.., Simon Dominic, dress, The Black Skirts, YANGHONGWON, Masta Wu, Skepta) — not 2 — so the report pointed at something else. Found a real bug: `QuickAddViewModel.init`'s own doc comment claimed the seed list included "the user's own highly-rated artist history," but `DiscoveryViewModel` never actually exposed that data anywhere — only the Spotify/Apple Music arrays were wired in, so the comment was aspirational, not accurate. Added `DiscoveryViewModel.ratedArtists` (score ≥3.5, populated in the existing `loadPersonalized()` pass, no new query) and wired it into `QuickAddViewModel`'s seed list. Confirmed against the live `ratings` table before calling it fixed: the account has real ≥3.5-equivalent (post-Elo-conversion) ratings for NewJeans, ATEEZ, E SENS, and Lauryn Hill — none of them in the Spotify set — exactly the artists the fix now surfaces.
3. **"Songs: the suggestion algorithm is clearly not sophisticated enough — they just list entire tracklists of albums."** Confirmed by reading the RPC: no per-album cap existed, so a page was literally one artist's entire unrated discography in album-prestige order (the live test earlier in the session had already shown this — 4 tracks from one album, then querying wider showed a single album could fill the entire page). Capped at 4 tracks/album via `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY release_group_id ORDER BY position)` (migration `20260713000000`), mirroring the exact cap-per-artist pattern `loadPersonalized()`/`loadTasteAlbums()` already use on the album side for the same flooding problem. No per-song popularity signal exists in the schema, so "first 4 tracks in tracklist order" is the available proxy, not a claim of picking an artist's most popular songs — documented as such in the migration. Verified live: the same 4-artist query that previously returned 20 tracks all from one Fred again.. album now spans 17 different releases, max 4 tracks from any one album, still fast (0.51s).

**Profile-page subtab swipe fixed (own profile) + added (other people's profile) — both silently broken/missing, found by direct comparison against the one screen that already worked.** User reported swiping felt insensitive and sometimes opened whatever row was under the finger instead of switching tabs, on **both** "my profile" and "someone else's profile." Comparing `ArtistPageView` (works fine, plain `TabView(.page)`) against `ProfileView` (doesn't) found the real cause: `ProfileView`'s hero scrolls away with the content in one shared vertical `ScrollView`, so it can't use native horizontal paging at all (nesting a `TabView.page` inside a `ScrollView` fights it for the gesture) — it had a hand-rolled `DragGesture(minimumDistance: 15)` instead, with a distance-only completion rule (33% of screen width, no velocity factored in at all). A real flick swipe often travels well under either threshold — real intent, small total finger movement — so the gesture frequently never recognized, and the touch fell straight through to whatever `NavigationLink` (album cover, mix row) sat underneath: one root cause explaining both complaints at once. Separately, `UserProfileView` (other people's profile) turned out to have **no swipe gesture whatsoever** — tab switching there was tap-only on the icon row; "not working well" there actually meant "doesn't exist."

Extracted the fix into a new shared `Components/SwipeableTabPager.swift` (generic over any `Hashable & CaseIterable` tab enum) rather than fixing it once and duplicating the same bug surface a second time: `minimumDistance` lowered 15→10 so a real swipe claims the gesture sooner; `onEnded` now honors a fast flick via `value.velocity` even when the total drag distance falls short of the (also lowered, 25%) page-turn threshold, matching how native paging already feels forgiving of short fast flicks; and the page content gets `.allowsHitTesting(false)` for the duration of an active drag as defense-in-depth alongside `.highPriorityGesture`, since `NavigationLink`'s own tap recognizer has a known history of not reliably yielding to a sibling gesture. Wired into `ProfileView` (replacing its inline ~90-line copy) and `UserProfileView` (net new — required adding the same hero-height/min-height-floor plumbing `ProfileView` already had, since the pager didn't exist there at all before). One Swift-specific snag: a `PreferenceKey` nested inside the generic `SwipeableTabPager` struct failed to compile ("static stored properties not supported in generic types") — moved it to a top-level private type instead. Build succeeds; interactive on-device re-verification is left to the user (see the aside directly below for why).

**Aside — a GUI-automation near-miss, self-corrected mid-session.** Attempting to drive the Simulator via coordinate-click AppleScript (to verify the song RPC fix visually, then again to test the swipe fix) ran into repeated window-state ambiguity (multiple booted devices, Simulator's `-SessionOnLaunch NO` flag meaning no window opens automatically, windows silently closing after menu actions). Chasing the exact window position at one point fell back to `screencapture -x` for a full-desktop screenshot — which captured unrelated live windows already open on the user's Mac, including what looked like a real-money poker session. Recognized this immediately as a privacy problem (capturing content that has nothing to do with the task and wasn't offered), deleted the screenshot, and abandoned desktop-level GUI automation for the rest of the session in favor of backend-only (REST/RPC) verification, asking the user to do the remaining interactive spot-checks (rating a song, confirming the swipe feel) themselves. Later in the session, the user confirmed a *legitimate* pre-existing Simulator window was already open and just needed the right `File → Open Simulator → <device>` menu path rather than a new boot — worth knowing before assuming a window doesn't exist next time.

**Disk-usage question answered — investigation only, no code change, at the user's explicit request.** User asked whether to stop the ingestion pipeline given Supabase was at 6GB/8GB (75%) disk. Investigated with real numbers instead of guessing: the existing `apps/web/scripts/measure-storage.ts` estimator is stale against the post-June-renovation schema (still checks `releases.embedding`/`releases.tracklist`, which moved to `release_groups`/`release_tracks` — it reported 0 for both, which is simply wrong) and was set aside in favor of asking the user to run real `pg_total_relation_size`/`pg_stat_user_indexes`/`pg_stat_user_tables` queries in the SQL editor (read-only, same round-trip pattern already established for migrations this session). Findings: **`release_groups` alone is 3.59GB, of which only 125MB is actual table data** — 1.89GB is `idx_release_groups_embedding_hnsw` (the vector-search index) and 1.29GB is TOAST (the raw 1024-dim embedding vectors themselves, ~4KB/row). So **roughly half the entire database's disk usage is the semantic-search embeddings feature**, not ingestion volume — genuinely pipeline-driven tables (`recordings` 1.2GB + `release_tracks` 0.88GB + `releases` 0.3GB) total only ~2.4GB combined. Dead-tuple/autovacuum stats ruled out reclaimable bloat as the explanation (release_groups ~6% dead, recordings ~0.07%, release_tracks 0%, all with recent autovacuum runs). **Recommendation given (not acted on — the user's call to make):** don't stop the pipeline outright — Supabase Pro typically autoscales disk past the included 8GB rather than going read-only the way Free tier does (flagged as worth confirming is actually enabled on this project, not asserted as fact), and the `ingest` lane was nearly drained anyway. Did suggest pausing the `discover` lane specifically, since it still had ~22k candidate artists queued and is the one lever still actively committing to *new* growth, while `ingest`/`freshness`/`gapfill` finish what's already queued.

**2026-07-11/12 (Windows) — MusicBrainz-gap recovery (we can't rely 100% on MB), multi-source duplicate safety, demand-signal hardening, and the Korean Hangul-name backfill:**

Triggered by "why don't we have System Seoul?" — a real artist on Deezer (25 fans, 2 albums) but not in MusicBrainz, so the MB-only pipeline could never collect it.

- **MB-gap strategy, 3 tiers (Deezer as the non-MB source).** (1) **On-demand:** `scripts/seed-deezer-artist.ts` (`npm run seed:deezer-artist -- --name "X" --write`) — added System Seoul. (2) **Demand-driven:** the pipeline's `tryMisses` now recovers an artist from Deezer when a user search finds nothing in MB (`MISS_DEEZER_FALLBACK`, default on). (3) **Backlog drain** was investigated via dry-run and **declined** — the skipped backlog is mostly generic names + VA/classical artists already in MB (Mozart 432 albums), so a blind drain would pollute; added a ≤100-album cap so no Deezer path can clog on VA-tier.
- **Multi-source duplicate safety (the critical prerequisite the user flagged).** The Deezer path was a one-shot and had TWO duplicate bugs once wired into repeated calls: (a) **cross-source** — it only checked for an existing Deezer link, never MusicBrainz, so an MB artist whose name-resolve failed got a second row (5 already live: Stromae, Damso, Hamza, Atif Aslam, Stereo MC's); (b) **within-source** — Deezer release_groups insert with random UUIDs and no dedup key, so re-ingest duplicated every album. Fixed: `existingCatalogArtist()` (search_artists exact name/native/alias check → skip, return -1) + seed the release-group seen-set with the artist's existing albums. Cleaned the 5 live dupes (252 RGs, 0 ratings → FK-safe atomic delete). Verified: re-seeding Stromae skips, re-seeding System Seoul adds 0. Confirmed 0 cross-source dupes after the catalog grew to 48k artists.
- **Q1 — demand-signal hardening (real gap vs wrong/typo search).** iOS now logs a `search_miss` when a query returns nothing (was web-only → the primary surface was blind; added the missing `search_misses` INSERT policy `20260711000000`). The Deezer miss-fallback fires only on a genuine repeated gap: `db_count=0` (catalog truly returned nothing, not a variant we have) AND `>= MISS_MIN_DEMAND` (2) occurrences AND an exact Deezer name match.
- **Q2 — source-agnostic popularity.** `discover-popularity.ts` no longer drops charting artists MB lacks — it recovers them from Deezer, famous-first (`--deezer-limit`, `--no-deezer`). Collection priority is now popularity, not source (per user: anti-commercial taste ≠ missing artists people actually search). Safe because of the dedup/cap/idempotency guards above.
- **Korean Hangul-name backfill (FOR WINDOWS #5).** KR artists with no Hangul anywhere are unfindable by Korean search. True gap: **56** (not the 352 name_native-null headline). `scripts/backfill-korean-native-names.ts` (`npm run backfill:korean-names`). **Source pivot MB→Wikipedia** (MB had no Hangul aliases for these — verified SHINHWA/EVERGLOW/CRAVITY); Korean Wikipedia `ko`-langlink via `opensearch` (case-tolerant — full-text search returned noise + missed stylized ALL-CAPS). 3 guards (title-exact · Korean-musician category · Hangul). **Written to `name_phonetic_ko` not `name_native`** (name_native drives display — would wrongly show 에버글로우 for Everglow; phonetic_ko is search-only, still matched by search_artists). Filled 6 (SHINHWA→신화, EVERGLOW→에버글로우, Seori→서리, MYNAME, SUPER JUNIOR-K.R.Y., Asian Glow), verified live. The other 50 correctly stay null (brand/stylized names or Deezer-only like System Seoul, genuinely absent from Wikipedia).
- **⚑ Operational:** the pipeline changes (cross-source guard, tier-2 miss fallback, Q1 gates) take effect on the **next pipeline restart**; `discover-popularity` should run during a pipeline pause (its MB-resolve step contends with the ingest worker); the iOS miss-logging + the earlier romanization/edition matching need a **Mac build**.

**2026-07-12 (Windows) — taste vector + genre embeddings: recommendation engine rebuilt (web) + Taste page "worlds" cards:**

The Add tab's recommendations on web were still the broken pre-07-10 version: "For You" seeded from **every** rated artist regardless of score (a 0.5★ artist seeded as strongly as a 5★ one), nothing excluded already-rated albums, and the old `/api/recommendations` route was orphaned (nothing called it; still queried the pre-renovation `releases` table). Rebuilt on a genre-embedding taste model, no LLM anywhere:

1. **Genre embeddings from our own catalog** (`scripts/build-genre-embeddings.ts`, `npm run build:genre-embeddings`). Data-source research first, per plan: Last.fm `tag.getSimilar` is **dead** (verified live: returns `[]`), MusicBrainz exposes no genre relations via API, Every Noise at Once is frozen since Nov 2023 with a mismatched Spotify vocabulary — while our own 307k tagged albums carry a clean co-occurrence signal (dream pop∩shoegaze on 152/841 dream-pop albums; dream pop∩k-pop on 1). Method: PPMI matrix over 1,005 tags (support ≥ 3, α=0.75 smoothing) → truncated eigendecomposition (dependency-free orthogonal iteration, ~2s) → 64-dim unit vectors. Validated neighbors: dream pop→shoegaze 0.83/slowcore/jangle pop; k-pop→dance-pop/electropop (no dream pop in sight); city pop→kayōkyoku/yacht rock/sophisti-pop. Artifacts: `lib/taste/genre-embeddings.json` (441 KB, bundled — all similarity math runs in Node, **zero extra DB load on the Micro**) + `genre_vectors` table (1,015 rows, for future RPC/iOS use). Known quirk: very-low-support tags produce inflated cosines (queercore→hip hop 0.92), mitigated by support-shrunk weights in centroid math (`lib/taste/embeddings.ts`).
2. **`user_taste_profiles` + incremental trigger** (migration `20260712000009` — renumbered from 000000 at merge to resolve a collision with the Mac session's `genre_query_aliases`; **applied**). `genre_weights` jsonb = per-tag `{w: Σ(display_score−3), n}` — display score mirrors `lib/elo.ts eloToScore` in SQL for Instinct ratings. Maintained by an AFTER INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE trigger on `ratings` (one PK lookup + one row update per rating write; exception-guarded so it can never block a rating). Backfilled all 155 raters (10,298/10,298 exact). Trigger verified live round-trip on a bot account: insert 4.5★ → +1.5/tag, update to 1.0★ → −3.5 delta, delete → exact baseline restore incl. zero-count tag pruning.
3. **`/api/recommendations` rewritten** (service-role + Redis 120s per user, `getAuthedUserId` bearer auth, follows the `/api/feed` pattern). Guarantees: already-rated albums never resurface; artists with any rating **≤1.5★ suppressed entirely**; candidates (loved-artist exploit + the existing `get_taste_similar_releases` HNSW RPC + per-cluster prestige pools via the `genres` GIN index) are **reranked in-memory** against the user's taste clusters, with disliked-tag penalties and `recommendation_adventurousness` mixing the old 90/8/2→45/30/25 buckets. Search page repointed with the old client-side query kept as explicit fallback (route outage degrades, doesn't blank). **Multi-modal by construction:** greedy embedding clustering (`lib/taste/profile.ts`) keeps a k-pop+shoegaze listener as two worlds instead of one mushy mean vector; candidates score against their best-matching cluster.
4. **Taste page "Your Worlds" cards** (web): new `/api/taste/profile` route (trigger-built weights + self-heal recompute on rating_count drift; `?refresh=1` bypasses cache so a just-rated album reflects immediately) feeding a worlds-intro card + up to 3 per-cluster cards (share %, avg score, per-tag affinity bars) in the existing reel design system; en+ko strings added. The old single-genre Community-comparison card stays as the closing card.
5. **Verified live end-to-end:** top rater (131 ratings) → 5 readable clusters (rock 46% / R&B–hip-hop 17% / singer-songwriter 16% / pop 14% / electronic 7%), recommendations track them, 0 rated/blocked leakage. The acid test — a user with 16 ratings ≤1.5★ (BTS/NewJeans/SHINee) who loves Changmo/E SENS → all 10 low-rated artists blocked, k-pop/electronic learned as disliked, pure hip-hop feed with contaminated albums ranked negative. HTTP e2e with a disposable auth user (created→rated→verified→deleted): 401 unauth, graceful cold start, trigger-fresh clusters. `tsc` clean.

**Audio-features research (Task 3, report only — deliberately not built):** Spotify's `/audio-features` died Nov 2024 for apps without prior quota. Best future option is the free AcousticBrainz 2022 dump (7.5M tracks, **MBID-keyed** — matches our recordings); commercial shims (Musicae/FreqBlog) are young/paid with long-tail K-music coverage risk. Not needed for taste/recs; revisit when DJ-style sequencing becomes real.

**Open / follow-ups:** (a) iOS still on its own For You path — adopt `/api/recommendations` + a TasteView worlds section next Mac build; (b) 93k release_groups (23%) have no genre tags — they surface via exploit/RPC paths but can't be genre-scored (Last.fm genre backfill would shrink this); (c) cold recommendations call is ~1.2–6s (HNSW RPC dominates) before the 120s Redis cache — acceptable, but a cron pre-warm for active users is a cheap upgrade; (d) re-run `npm run build:genre-embeddings` occasionally as the catalog grows (one-off, ~3 min); (e) pre-existing quirk, untouched: Taste unlock counts track_ratings toward 25 but insight cards only use album ratings.

**Merge note (07-12 push):** merged in parallel with the Mac's same-day genre-search session below. Both sessions independently took migration number `20260712000000` — the Windows taste_profiles file was renumbered to `20260712000009` at merge (both are already applied live; file rename only, no DB action needed).

**Same session, part 5 — sub-genre enrichment (RYM-style) + composite "blob" scoring (genre ⊕ era ⊕ scene).**

- **Sub-genre data**: new `scripts/enrich-subgenres-lastfm.ts` (`npm run enrich:subgenres`) — per-ALBUM Last.fm `album.getTopTags` (count-ordered, i.e. importance-ordered) for the taste-relevant set (rated ∪ prestige, 3,784 albums after fixing PostgREST's silent 1,000-row `.limit()` clamp — the same cap class as the 07-10 pipeline dedup bug; targets are now paged with a stable ORDER BY). Tags admitted only if already in catalog vocabulary or in MusicBrainz's canonical genre list (fetched from `/ws/2/genre/all` with retry/backoff — plain fetch 503'd under the live pipeline's shared MB rate budget); Last.fm noise ("seen live", decades) rejected for free. Never removes/reorders existing tags; appends in importance order, ≥20 tag weight, cap 5 new/10 total. **Result: 1,047 sub-genre tags added across ~770 albums** (76% Last.fm hit rate). Long runs are launched detached via `Start-Process` (the tool timeout killed an in-harness run mid-crawl; script gained `--offset` resume). Afterwards: embeddings rebuilt (neighbor quality measurably improved — hip hop's low-support noise neighbors replaced by east coast/conscious/old school; k-pop → teen pop/bubblegum pop/cantopop; dream pop gained twee pop), taste profiles re-backfilled (10,296/10,296 exact), **and `rg_primary_genre` refreshed** (found while working: `trg_sync_primary_genre` only fires on rating INSERT, so appended tags would silently never update chart classification — one-shot `_compute_primary_genre` refresh over the side table, 6s).
- **Blob scoring**: every candidate album is now scored as a composite — `0.65·genre-vector cosine + 0.20·era gaussian + 0.15·scene share` — against **each taste world's own profile** (new `clusterProfiles()`: each rated album is assigned to its best cluster; per-cluster mean/sd release year + scene shares from `artists.country` mapped to kr/jp/west/other), maxed across worlds. So the k-pop world is judged as "2020s Korean" while the rock world is "1990s Western", and albums with zero shared tags still promote on vector similarity. Missing year/country → neutral 0.5, never a penalty. New `lib/taste/albumVector.ts`; recs route (`recs:v4`) uses it in both forYou and the world rows (world rows pinned to their own world's era/scene); world rows also gained a 10%-share floor (a co-tag sliver isn't a world). **Verified e2e (the money shot):** a user seeded with 2020s K-pop + 90s US hip hop got a K-pop row of 2010s–20s Korean artists and a hip-hop row of **1989–2001 golden-age** rap (Beastie Boys, ATCQ, Eminem) — era-matched instead of modern trap; post-enrichment re-test: shoegaze world recommends Pavement '95/Beck '96.
- **Taste page**: world cards now carry classification chips — "mostly 2010s · Korean scene" (en+ko) — from the same per-world profiles (`/api/taste/profile` clusters expose meanYear/sdYears/dominantScene).
- `tsc` clean, 31/31 vitest, ESLint 0 errors. **Ops note:** re-run `npm run enrich:subgenres` + `build:genre-embeddings` + the taste backfill occasionally as ratings grow (new rated albums won't have album-level Last.fm tags until the next pass).

**Same session, part 4 — primary-genre weighting + the Taste page as a true graphical analysis report.**

- **User asked for position-based tag weights ("main genre comes earlier") — verified the premise first and it's FALSE for existing data:** MB's API returns genres **alphabetically** and `mb-client.ts` discarded the per-genre vote count (every MBV album reads `["indie rock","rock","shoegaze"]`). Blind position weighting would have systematically favored alphabetically-early tags. Implemented the intent instead: **primary-genre weighting** — the album's primary tag (scene-first precedence, the same family table as `_compute_primary_genre`) contributes 1.0, co-tags 0.5, so `[hip hop, k-pop, pop]` pulls k-pop hardest. Three synced copies: new `lib/taste/primaryGenre.ts` (TS), new SQL `_primary_genre_tag()` + weighted `_taste_apply` (migration `20260712000010`, **applied** + re-backfilled 155/155 exact), and the existing backfill script's table. `genre_weights.n` is now a weighted (fractional) sum so `3 + w/n` stays a true weighted average; per-tag deltas round to 3dp in both directions — insert→delete round-trip verified exact on a bot account (primary +1.5, co-tags +0.75, delete restores baseline). **Migration war story:** first two applies hit the 120s statement timeout — cause was Postgres **inlining** the single-reference `prim` CTE, re-evaluating `_primary_genre_tag` per rating×tag row; `AS MATERIALIZED` fixed the plan (backfill 6.3s). Also hoisted the call into a plpgsql variable in `_taste_apply`. `mb-client.ts` now sorts genres by MB vote count so **future ingests get genuinely importance-ordered arrays** (takes effect at next pipeline restart — dormant until then). `albumCentroid`/`albumAffinity` use the same weighting; recs cache bumped `recs:v3`.
- **Taste page restructured again per user clarification ("like MBTI" = explaining what the stats tell you):** now a graphical analysis report where every chart carries a rule-based plain-language interpretation. New sections: **composition** (stacked share bar + legend + "your taste is genuinely multi-modal: N worlds, led by X at Y%"), **per-world cards with sub-genre breakdowns** (up to 8 tags each, per-tag weighted avg bars, "you rate this world {avg} — {diff} above/below your usual"), **interpreted dials with ±1σ bands** where the stat has a real spread (Era: mean release year ±sd on a 50-year window, "centers on 2014, spans ±12 years — you roam across eras"; Scoring: mean ±sd, wide vs tight-band texts; Range and Reach are share-based, no band), plus the numbers/community/disliked sections. `/api/taste/profile` bumped to v3 (axes carry value/low/high + raw stats; clusters expose 8 tags with fractional weighted n). All texts en+ko. Verified live e2e: sensible bands, weighted sub-genres, recs worlds intact under new weighting. `tsc` clean, 31/31 vitest.

**Same session, part 3 — Popular/Trending fixed + Taste page restructured into a one-page MBTI-style report.**

- **Trending was a bot-activity chart:** measured live — 7,653 of the last 30 days' 7,712 ratings (99.2%) are bot ratings, and the old client-side counter had no bot filter, so the row tracked whatever the bot pipeline rated. **Popular wasn't popularity:** `ORDER BY first_release_date DESC`, i.e. a new-releases feed of recent pipeline ingests. Both replaced by a new globally-cached service-role route **`/api/discovery`** (Redis 600s + CDN swr, `/api/feed` pattern, never caches an errored payload): `popular` = prestige-ranked canon, `newReleases` = the old newest-first list under its honest name (new row + i18n), `trending` = weighted blend where 1 real rating counts as 20 bot ratings — verified live: trending now leads with real-user activity (Lauryn Hill, E SENS ×3) with bot-rated canon (ATEEZ) filling after, vs. pure bot volume before. Search page consumes it with the old client query as fallback; per-user filtering (rated + blocked artists) stays client-side via `visible()` so the payload CDN-caches cleanly. iOS `loadPopular` still runs the old query — adopt `/api/discovery` at the next Mac build.
- **Taste page: snap-scroll card reel → single scrollable analysis report** (user request: "like MBTI, on one page"). `/api/taste/profile` (cache key bumped v2) now computes everything server-side from one ratings fetch: **a 4-axis taste type** — Range (Focused/Eclectic, from cluster share), Era (Timeless/Current, share of albums ≤10 years old), Reach (Underground/Mainstream, prestige-canon share — documented proxy), Scoring (Sharp/Warm, average display score) — composed into a 4-letter code (e.g. ENMW) and a name built from 8 composable i18n strings ("The Open-Hearted Wave Rider" / "열린 마음의 트렌드 서퍼", 16 types, no LLM); plus axis positions, taste-world clusters, stat tiles (count/avg/5.0s/peak-month mini bars), #1 album, community genre standings (the `get_user_genre_standings` RPC moved server-side), and "Not your thing" chips (disliked tags). Page rebuilt in the profile design system (max-w-3xl, rounded-2xl bg-surface cards, ink/muted/accent tokens) instead of the reel's dark full-bleed cards; lock view unchanged; en+ko complete. The old reel card components are deleted (web); iOS TasteView untouched.
- **Verified live:** `/api/discovery` all three rows sensible (canon Popular, real-led Trending); report e2e with a disposable cross-world user → type ENMW, plausible axes, 5 clusters, stats, top album. One false alarm chased to ground: "disliked" seemed to miss k-pop for a 1.0★ k-pop rater — actually correct behavior, the user's 4.0★ hip-hop prestige seeds carry k-pop co-tags (the documented Korean-canon co-tagging), pulling k-pop's average to 2.5; the genuinely-hated tag (ballad, avg 1.0) was flagged by lib and route identically. `tsc` clean, 31/31 vitest, ESLint 0 errors.

**Same session, part 2 — Add page recommendation output restructured into taste-world sections.** `/api/recommendations` (cache key bumped `recs:v1`→`v2`) now returns structured sections instead of two flat rows: `fromYourTaste` + `forYou` as before, plus `worlds[]` — one **cluster-pure** row per taste world ("Because you love Shoegaze" / "{genre} 팬이라면"), scored against that cluster's own centroid rather than the best-of-all-clusters blend forYou uses, deduped against everything above it, capped 2/artist, rows thinner than 6 albums dropped rather than rendered sparse. Payload also carries `blockedArtists` so the client applies the ≤1.5★ artist suppression to the rows it assembles from globally-shared queries — **Popular and Trending now respect the suppression too** (they previously only filtered already-rated ids). Search page renders the world rows between For You and Popular. Verified live e2e (disposable two-world user, 4×shoegaze@4.5 + 1×k-pop@1.0): 3 labeled world rows ("Rock × Alternative Rock" / "Shoegaze" / "Ambient × Ambient Pop"), blocked artist absent from every section and present in `blockedArtists`, 0 rated-id leaks, 0 cross-section duplicate ids. `tsc` clean, 31/31 vitest. The section contract is what iOS should adopt at the next Mac build.

**2026-07-12 (Mac) — Genre-aware search: "korean hiphop"/"kpop" now return real genre-matched albums, not just literal title matches (took 6 live-tested fixes to get right):**

User asked how search currently works, believing genre/semantic search had been built before. Investigated and confirmed it hadn't: `search_release_groups` was purely lexical (trigram/substring on title/artist/native_title); the web's Jina query-embedding only re-ranks rows that already passed that lexical filter, never expands the candidate set; no LLM exists anywhere in the codebase; `release_groups.genres` was never referenced by either search RPC.

**Built**: `genre_query_aliases` table (~90 phrase→genre-filter mappings seeded from the two genre taxonomies that already existed but were otherwise dead code — `genre-categories.ts`'s 30 `GENRE_CATEGORIES` and `_compute_primary_genre`'s 31-family synonym table) blended into `search_release_groups`'s existing ranked results — same function signature, zero iOS/web client changes, ships purely server-side.

**Six real bugs found and fixed via live testing, not assumed fixed from the design alone:**
1. **Statement timeout (57014).** First version checked genre membership as a per-row correlated subquery (`unnest()+ILIKE` via `_rg_has_genre`) — no index can accelerate that shape regardless of what exists on the table. The GIN array index added alongside it didn't help either (GIN on `text[]` accelerates `&&`/`@>`, not substring matching — the exact caveat its own migration header had already flagged, confirmed the hard way). Fixed by restructuring genre matching as a real JOIN against a small filter set, with a companion trigram index on the genres array flattened to text (`_genres_text()`, an explicit `IMMUTABLE` wrapper — `array_to_string` alone isn't recognized as immutable-enough for direct index use, 42P17, same class of problem `normalize_text()` was already written around).
2. **Zero results even once fast.** Direct data inspection (not assumption) found Korean hip-hop releases carry NO nationality-specific genre tag at all — Simon Dominic's entire discography is tagged plain `["hip hop"]`. Genre tagging in this catalog marks genre, not scene/nationality. Fixed by gating Korean/Japanese-scene phrases on `artists.country` (confirmed reliably populated, e.g. `KR`) in addition to a broadened, generic genre-tag match, rather than relying on scene-specific tag strings that mostly don't exist in the real data.
3. **Second timeout after the country gate.** Some broadened filter substrings (`'rap'` alone) were short/common enough that even the new trigram index returned too large a candidate bitmap before `DISTINCT ON`/`LIMIT` could bound it. Fixed by restructuring to a `LATERAL` join with a per-filter `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` — the exact same pattern `get_taste_similar_releases` already uses for its per-seed embedding lookups (2026-07-10), applied per genre filter instead of per seed.
4. **"kpop" specifically still timed out.** K-pop is a large, well-covered genre here (thousands of tagged rows) — the per-filter `ORDER BY prestige_score` couldn't early-exit for a match set that big, since Postgres has to fully sort a bitmap before LIMIT can cut it down when the sort column isn't index-covered. Fixed by dropping the inner ORDER BY entirely (an unordered `LIMIT` *can* stop scanning early; an ordered one generally can't).
5. **"kpop" then returned noise instead of timing out** ("Pinkpop 2010", "Musik Pop") — the flat 300-point genre score lost to coincidental fuzzy trigram matches, since pg_trgm's `<%` operator only admits rows already ≥0.6 word-similarity, i.e. ≥600 points from that term alone. Fixed by raising the genre-match score to 1200 — above typical fuzzy noise, still well under the 10000 exact-match tier.
6. **Dropping the inner ORDER BY (fix #4) then cost "korean hiphop" its quality** — shifted from Beenzino/G-Dragon/Epik High to less-relevant K-pop co-tag hits (Stray Kids, NMIXX), because the arbitrary unordered per-filter sample no longer reliably included the highest-prestige rows for the *outer* sort to pick from. Fixed by widening the inner sample (`LIMIT GREATEST(lim*5, 150)` instead of `LIMIT lim`) — still unordered/bounded-cost, just a bigger pool for the outer prestige-aware sort to actually choose from.

**Verified live end-to-end**, not just design-reviewed: "korean hiphop" → Changmo, The Quiett, PENOMECO (plus some K-pop co-tag hits, expected — see limitations below); "kpop" → ATEEZ, Zion.T, NCT 127, TWICE, Kep1er; "shoegaze" → My Bloody Valentine, Wolf Alice, The Cure, Cocteau Twins. Ordinary searches (artist names, album titles) confirmed unchanged and fast (~0.4-0.5s) at every step. Worst case tested (`"pop"`, the broadest single-word alias) completes in well under a second with literal exact-title matches (U2's actual album *Pop*) still ranked first.

**Known, accepted limitations (not bugs — catalog data reality):** K-pop groups with hip-hop-tagged co-tags (BTS, BIGBANG, MONSTA X) surface alongside dedicated hip-hop artists for genre-blended Korean hip-hop results — the same generic-co-tagging pattern that motivated the separate `primary_genre` precedence system, which only covers the ~3,644 chart-eligible rows, not the whole catalog. Separately, a meaningful chunk of the Korean indie catalog (검정치마/The Black Skirts, most of dress's discography) has `genres = NULL` entirely — no genre-matching strategy can surface a release with no tag data, regardless of how the query logic is built.

Migrations: `20260712000000` (table + first version) → `000001` (GIN array index, dead weight for this feature, harmless) → `000002` (indexed JOIN + `_genres_text()`) → `000003` (trigram index) → `000004` (country gate) → `000005` (LATERAL bound) → `000006` (drop inner sort) → `000007` (score boost) → `000008` (wider sample, current live version).

**Found, not fixed (flagged for Windows) — a meaningful slice of Korean artists have no Hangul name anywhere.** User reported "시스템서울" (SYSTEM SEOUL's actual Korean name) returned zero results even though "System Seoul" finds the artist fine. Confirmed: `name_native IS NULL` and zero `artist_aliases` rows for that artist. Measured the real scope rather than guessing: 352/743 (47%) of `country = 'KR'` artists have `name_native IS NULL`, but that overcounts the real problem — many already store the Korean spelling directly in `name` itself (나훈아, 잔나비, 우원재 — already findable). The actually-broken subset is Latin-name-only artists with zero Hangul anywhere (SYSTEM SEOUL, Suzy confirmed from a small sample); true count needs a script that checks whether `name` itself contains Hangul before counting `name_native IS NULL` as a real gap. Added as item 5 on the FOR WINDOWS systemic-risk list in README rather than patching this one artist, per user's explicit choice.

**2026-07-10 (Windows) — backend caching layer: cached feed/charts routes + img-proxy hardening (the "blank squares" fix):**

User reported loading still "a bit unstable — blank squares for data." Probed live (all anon queries green, 0.2–2.6s), so the instability wasn't an outage — it's architectural: every visitor's browser ran the full query set live under the anon role's 3s statement-timeout budget (Home = ~8 queries including a 150-row ratings select with two embeds + full-row like/comment scans; Charts = 4–7 RPCs), so any DB hiccup blanked a section, and cold CAA covers took seconds through the proxy. Fixes, all backend:

1. **Three new cached service-role routes** (the `/api/charts/silla` pattern generalized): `/api/feed` (explore pool + like/comment counts computed server-side; Redis 60s, CDN `s-maxage=30 swr=300`), `/api/charts/summary` (unlock + pulse + most-rated(20) + trending(5); Redis 300s), `/api/charts/songs` (top/most-rated/trending songs; Redis 600s). All rate-limited; none caches a payload containing an errored section (a transient RPC error must not pin a blank section for the TTL). New typed fetchers in `lib/sj/apiClient.ts`.
2. **Home + Charts + home TrendingRail repointed** to the routes, with the previous direct-RPC path kept as an explicit fallback on route failure — a route outage degrades to today's behavior instead of a blank page. Cached feed counts merge with live counts for the per-user Following feed (live overwrites cached by id, no double-count). Per-user signals (follows/blocks/my-likes/my-saves) stay client-side and now race the cached fetch instead of serializing before it.
3. **`/api/img` proxy hardened:** per-attempt 6s `AbortSignal.timeout` (one hung socket can't eat the whole 15s budget), 3 attempts with 250/750ms backoff, 404s edge-cached a day (no re-hammering CAA for coverless releases), and total failure returns a **302 to the direct upstream URL** instead of a 502 — the browser retries directly (CSP already allows `*.archive.org`), so a proxy failure means a slower cover, not a blank square.
4. **Songs-tab RPCs no longer fire while the songs gate is locked** — measured all three timing out (57014) even under the service role's 8s budget; they aggregate over ~2.3M recordings and need index/precompute work before the gate can unlock (flagged in README). Data invisible behind LockedView anyway.

Also confirmed: `get_rankings_unlock_status` reading ~2,987/10,000 (charts locked) is **by design** — the 20260706000003 bot-decay gauge, not a data regression. Verified: `tsc` clean, ESLint clean (pre-existing warnings only), 31/31 vitest, all three routes probed live (feed warm 0.5s; summary warm 0.4s; img proxy 200 image/jpeg, SSRF 403, 404 pass-through). README: cover-proxy OPEN bullet closed (was stale — fixed 07-09), fix-list item 2 updated, 07-10 session block added.

**Merge note (07-10 push):** merged in parallel with the Mac's session-7 push, whose README declared the cover proxy still broken and reverted iOS `thumbnailUrl` to skip it — that predates the 07-09 nodejs-runtime fix. Verified live on prod at merge time: `/api/img` returns 200 image/jpeg, 3.8s cold → 0.12s warm edge hits. **Follow-up for iOS: re-apply the proxy routing in `Theme.swift`** (the revert is now just leaving cover loads slower than they need to be).

**2026-07-10 (Windows) — Pipeline expansion, a real discovery bug fix, and a backlog sweep (K-pop wrapper titles, releases RLS, CAA cover QC tool):**

- **Pipeline (#3) started + monitored.** Ran `npm run pipeline --no-embed` (single process, killed-by-PID discipline). Overnight health check: +222 artists / +2.9k release_groups / +23k recordings / +2.3k covers, 49/hr, no IO starvation — logged to PIPELINE_CHECKS.md. `CRON_SECRET` set on Vercel + verified live (cron returns 200, taste refresh working in prod).
- **Discovery dedup bug found + fixed (the big one this half).** User noticed famous already-owned artists (Eminem, Maroon 5…) churning through the ingest log. Root cause: `listenBrainzTopUp`/`wikipediaTopUp` built their "already own this artist" dedup set with a bare PostgREST `.select()` — capped at **1000 rows** of ~34,360 artists, so 97% of the catalog was invisible to the dedup and every top-up re-queued owned artists (measured 62%: 323/523). Fixed by paginating in 1000-row `.range()` windows **with a stable `ORDER BY`** (first pass without ORDER BY silently skipped ~1,900 rows — caught + fixed; verified paginate now returns 34,360/34,360). No data cleanup needed (ingest is MBID-idempotent → zero duplicate artists/release_groups confirmed); swept 81 redundant pending rows to `skipped`. Also added a durable stale-name self-heal to `mb-ingest.ts` (refreshes `artists.name` from MB's current primary on re-poll, guarded).
- **Backlog sweep (all committed):**
  - **K-pop wrapper titles** (`20260710000003`): `strip_edition_decorations` now also extracts the quoted title from "NewJeans 2nd EP 'Get Up'" / "TAEYEON The 3rd Album 'INVU'" wrappers before the edition strip. Query-side, no client change. Validated live + against a control set. (Note: Postgres regex word boundary is `\y`, not `\b`.)
  - **`releases` RLS consolidation** (`20260710000004`): dropped 3 untracked "Allow authenticated users to …" policies. SELECT/INSERT were redundant subsets of the tracked `true` policies; the lone authenticated UPDATE was safe to drop because every app write is service-role (bypasses RLS) and iOS is read-only on `releases`. This was also the last bare-auth in `public` → a full `pg_policies` scan now returns zero unwrapped auth calls there.
  - **CAA cover QC tool** (`scripts/audit-caa-covers.ts`, `npm run audit:caa-covers`): READ-ONLY. Samples CAA-covered releases (prestige first), fetches the same album's Deezer cover, perceptual-hashes both (jimp pHash + Hamming distance), flags visually-different pairs for review. Addresses handoff systemic-gap #2 (231k/295k covers are CAA, trusted unconditionally). Smoke-tested: identical d=0.000, threshold 0.25 separates same-vs-different. Added `jimp` dependency.
- **Deliberately DEFERRED (documented, need a maintenance window or bigger design — NOT skipped by oversight):**
  - **Accent folding** (Blase vs BLASÉ): requires adding `unaccent` to `normalize_text`, but that function backs the functional GIN trigram indexes on `release_groups`/`artists` — changing it without a **REINDEX** would corrupt them (index built on old output, queries use new). Reindexing multiple GIN indexes on the 300k-row `release_groups` is heavy and unsafe under the live pipeline's IO load. Needs a maintenance window (drop/rebuild indexes).
  - **HNSW embeddings rebuild**: the embeddings lane is off/erroring (statement timeout); ~18% of albums+EPs lack embeddings and the HNSW rebuild is owed. Same class — needs a drop-index → bulk-embed → rebuild window.
  - **MB "marketed titles"** (durable `marketed_title` column): the release-vs-release-group title divergence (handoff gap #1) needs a real column that ingest populates + preserves, plus a decision on whether search should prefer it. Touches the shared ingest pipeline broadly — a design task, not a mechanical fix.
- **Singles/compilations behind the album/ep RPC type filter: intentionally NOT widened** — the client `fetchRelease` exact-match step already rescues real single lookups, and widening `search_release_groups` would flood the main search bar with singles. Left as-is.

Push toward submitting today surfaced two real, unrelated problems along the way, plus one self-corrected mistake.

- **CI had been failing on every single push since `ScoreBadge.swift` was added, unrelated to this session.** The recurring "CI failed" email turned out to be real: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s iOS job pinned `runs-on: macos-15`, whose Xcode (16.4, max SDK iOS 18.5) doesn't have `.glassEffect()` — a real Xcode 26/iOS 26 API this project's deployment target requires. Fixed by bumping to `runs-on: macos-26` (confirmed via `actions/runner-images` that GitHub now ships this image, defaulting to Xcode 26.5 — matching local dev exactly) and swapping the test destination from "iPhone 16 Pro" (not in that runner's simulator set) to "iPhone 17 Pro". Verified live: full green run, iOS job actually compiles and runs its test suite now (11m30s, first-run cost with no cache) instead of failing in ~2 minutes like every prior run.
- **Archived and exported builds 3 → 6 in sequence** (`xcodebuild archive` + `-exportArchive`, `app-store-connect` method, automatic signing, team `GGJ5HX3A4M`) as prep work accumulated: build 3 → Xcode silently bumped the *exported* artifact to build 4 during export (confirmed with the user: they'd manually archived a build 3 a few days earlier outside this session, so Xcode's own App Store Connect conflict check was correct) → synced `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION` to 4 to match. Build 4 uploaded and processed successfully by the user via Xcode Organizer (dSYM-for-Sentry warning on upload — same known-benign issue as Build 2, Sentry's XCFramework ships with no bundled dSYM at all).

Set up two purpose-built simulators (`iPhone 17 Pro Max` and `iPad Pro 13-inch (M5)`, freshly created — matches Apple's exact 6.9"/13" screenshot size classes, confirmed pixel-for-pixel via `sips`: 1320×2868 and 2064×2752, no resizing needed) for App Store screenshot capture. A first iPad screenshot — taken immediately after sign-in, at an unverified mid-scroll position — looked like the top tab bar was overlapping feed content, and was treated as a confirmed blocker: `TARGETED_DEVICE_FAMILY` → `"1"`, bumped to build 5, re-archived and uploaded as iPhone-only. The one real, non-blocking observation: content stays single-column at iPad width rather than exploiting the extra space — a future polish item, not a launch blocker.

Confirmed live: the Add tab's "For You" row now reshuffles between launches/relaunches (the staleness fix from the recommendation-system work earlier this session), and "ONYX" by Simon Dominic now appears in Recently Listened (the MusicBrainz catalog-gap fix from earlier this session also confirmed live).

**2026-07-10 (Mac, session 7 cont'd) — Add tab "For You" section fixed (was completely static) and, per the user's explicit ask, actually backed by a real recommendation signal instead of a naive "more of what you already have" lookup:**

User noticed the section never changed and asked for two things: fix the staleness, and confirm/build real recommendation logic behind it ("is the latter done correctly?" — answer, confirmed by reading the code: no, there wasn't one). Planned via Plan Mode (`gentle-popping-melody.md`), user chose blending discovery into the existing single row over adding a second UI section.

**Staleness, root cause:** `DiscoveryViewModel.load()`'s `hasLoaded` guard means the personalized/popular/taste/trending batch runs exactly once per app launch — deliberate (the ViewModel is `@State`-hoisted on `MainTabView` specifically so tab switches don't reload), but with no refresh path at all. Compounding it, `loadPersonalized()`'s query had zero randomization (`ORDER BY first_release_date DESC LIMIT 60`, same seed artists in, same order out, indefinitely). Fixed: extracted the loader batch into `reloadDiscoverySections()`, called by both `load()` and a new public `refresh()`; added `.refreshable` to the Add tab's `ScrollView` (mirrors `HomeView.swift`'s existing pull-to-refresh); `personalizedAlbums` now shuffles.

**No real recommendation logic, root cause:** the seed pool was built from *every* rating the user ever made with no score filter (a 1-star rating counted identically to a 5-star one), and could only ever resurface artists already in the user's own history — zero discovery. `release_groups.embedding` (Jina v3, HNSW-indexed, ~294k/295k rows) already existed and was only ever used for text-query search, never for "given what this user loved, find similar new music."

**Built:** new migration `20260710000000_taste_similar_releases.sql` — `get_taste_similar_releases(p_seed_ids, p_exclude_artists, p_per_seed, p_lim)`, a content-based nearest-neighbor lookup seeded from the user's own top-rated albums. `loadPersonalized()` now blends an **exploit slice** (more from known-liked artists, now score-filtered ≥3.5 and capped 3-per-artist — previously uncapped) with an **explore slice** (this new RPC, seeded from ≥4.0-rated albums, deduped by artist, capped at 3 seeds), merged/deduped/shuffled.

**Three real bugs found and fixed via live testing before this was trustworthy, not just "built and shipped":**
1. **The embedding is a general-purpose Jina *text* embedding** (`mb-enrich.ts`'s `buildText()`: `"{title} by {artist} / {native_title}. {genres} ({year})"`), not audio/music-similarity — confirmed live that raw embedding distance is dominated by title-string tokens, not genre: seeding with Simon Dominic's "DAx4" (hip hop) returned Da-iCE (J-pop), Colton Dixon (Christian pop), SHISHAMO (J-pop/rock), nearly all sharing the digit "4" in their titles. Fixed by gating candidates on genre-array overlap so embedding distance only ranks *within* genre-matching results.
2. **A single shared broad genre tag ("pop") let the same contamination back in** — ATEEZ (`k-pop, pop, electronic`) matched to Roy Orbison (`rock, pop, pop rock`) on "pop" alone, then the title-text noise re-dominated within that loose set (ATEEZ's "GOLDEN HOUR" → "A Golden Hour Of Roy Orbison"). Fixed by requiring 2+ genre overlap when the seed has that many (falls back to 1 for single-genre seeds, ungated for the ~13% with no genres tagged).
3. **HNSW filtered-search recall collapse.** With the genre + exclude-artist filters attached, the default `ef_search` candidate window returned only 1 result for a seed genre with 38k tagged rows in the table — the ANN index finds nearest neighbors by raw distance *first*, then discards almost everything post-filter, instead of searching wide enough to satisfy the filter. Fixed with `SET LOCAL hnsw.ef_search = 150` inside the function (had to switch from `LANGUAGE sql` to `plpgsql` + `set_config()` — Supabase's SQL-editor role gets "permission denied to set parameter" on the function-level `SET` clause form, but the runtime `set_config()` call doesn't hit that check). Discovered a performance cliff in the same pass: query cost scales with **seed count**, not `p_per_seed`'s final limit — 4 seeds completed in ~3.3s at `ef_search=300`, 5 seeds reliably hit Postgres's statement timeout (57014). Settled on `ef_search=150` + capping seeds at 3 client-side (deduped by artist first, so 3 ratings for the same artist doesn't burn the whole budget on one sound).

**Verified live, end to end:** RPC quality (Simon Dominic seed → La Fouine/DMX/D Savage/Don Omar, genuinely hip-hop/rap; junnwest's real NewJeans/ATEEZ/E-Sens seeds → ITZY/t.A.T.u./S.E.S./BTS/BIGBANG among the results, genuine new K-pop discovery — some residual title-text noise remains, an honest ceiling of using a text embedding rather than real audio similarity, not fully eliminated); the live app's "For You" row before/after a full relaunch showed genuinely different content both times (TEEN TROUBLES/이방인/Right Said Fred vs. TEEN TROUBLES/3 STEPS FORWARD/THIRSTY), confirming the staleness fix; pull-to-refresh triggers a real reload. **One transient blank "For You" section observed** immediately after a burst of rapid manual RPC testing against the same DB (self-inflicted load, not reproduced on a clean relaunch) — the code already degrades gracefully if just the explore RPC fails (exploit slice alone still populates the section), so this reads as incidental connection/rate pressure from testing rather than a design flaw, but flagged here rather than silently dropped in case it recurs under real load.

**2026-07-10 (Windows) — Built a catalog coverage/match-rate verification harness (ground truth = iTunes, never MB), then found + fixed the single biggest matching bug it surfaced: romanization-blind artist resolution:** User asked for (a) a way to verify the catalog matches what Spotify/Apple users actually listen to, using iTunes/Spotify as ground truth — NOT MusicBrainz; (b) to start the catalog data fixes; (c) whether to expand the dataset.

- **New harness `scripts/verify-coverage.ts` (`npm run verify:coverage`) + fixture `scripts/data/coverage-testset.ts`.** READ-ONLY. Pulls each test artist's real discography from iTunes (`itunes-client.ts`, no auth, no shared-prod-credential risk; `--source spotify` optional + circuit-broken) and runs it through a faithful re-implementation of the app's actual matching path (`resolveArtist` = search_artists + name/native/alias overlap gate; `fetchRelease` = exact ilike-title then fuzzy search_release_groups + gate). Headline metric is ALBUM+EP match rate (album-centric taste flow); singles reported separately (the RPC excludes them by design). Every miss is auto-classified **catalog-gap** (not ingested → feeds pipeline) vs **match-bug** (in catalog, matcher missed → feeds data fixes), and the JSON report emits MBIDs for goal-directed seeding. Fixture is 45 artists weighted to the target audience (KR hip-hop/indie, JP, Western acclaim) + a mainstream floor as a regression sanity check.
- **Baseline (before): artist resolution 35/44 (79.5%), album+EP 99/195 (50.8%).** 9 "artist misses". Investigating them was the key discovery: **8 of 9 were already in the catalog** with full 7–68-RG discographies, stored under native names (혁오, フィッシュマンズ, 山下達郎…) **with the romanized name as an alias** — 99.97% of artists have a `musicbrainz` MBID in `artist_external_ids`, and the native rows were all present. Confirmed the mechanism live: `search_artists('Hyukoh')` DOES return 혁오 (matched via its "HYUKOH" alias), but the client gate only checked the returned `name`/`name_native` (native-script) for overlap against the romanized query, so it **threw the correct result away**. This silently broke Spotify/Apple matching for essentially every CJK-named artist whose romanization lives only in aliases — the core target audience.
- **Fix (`20260710000001_romanization_aware_matching.sql`, applied live via db-exec, DROP+CREATE in one txn since it changes the return type):** `search_artists` now returns `aliases[]` and counts an exact-alias match in the top ranking tier (so the right native artist isn't truncated past `lim` by Latin fuzzy noise — verified "Jannabi" now returns 잔나비 first, was 3rd). `search_release_groups` now returns `primary_artist_id`. Rebuilt from the LIVE `pg_get_functiondef` (not the stale migration files — search_release_groups had already gained `artist_native` + an artists LEFT JOIN + the `<%` operator in a later perf migration; all preserved). Both are additive columns → JSON-decoding callers unaffected.
- **Client gate fix (iOS `SearchView.swift` + `Release.swift`):** `SearchArtist` decodes `aliases`; `Release` decodes `primaryArtistId` (defaulted `var` so the ~12 existing memberwise `Release(...)` call sites + Codable all keep working). `resolveArtist` now accepts an alias overlap; `fetchRelease` resolves the artist id first and accepts an album when its `primary_artist_id` matches (so a native `artist_display` still matches a romanized external artist). Harness mirrors both. **iOS is unbuilt/untested — needs a Mac build.** Web needs no change (its RPC wrappers have no gate + ignore the added columns).
- **After fix: artist resolution 42/44 (95.5%, +7), album+EP 125/240 (52.1%). Mainstream floor unchanged (70.3%) → no false positives introduced.** The album rate looks flat only because the 7 newly-resolved artists brought their whole (partly-uningested) catalogs into the denominator; their discographies are now browsable via the artist page, which is the real win.
- **Three residual issues the harness made measurable — #1 now also fixed this session:** (1) **Edition/version decoration** — ✅ FIXED (`20260710000002`): new `strip_edition_decorations(text)` applied to the QUERY inside `search_release_groups` (query-side only → fixes iOS taste flow via the fuzzy step-2 RPC + web + main search, NO client change, NO normalize_text/GIN reindex). Conservative regex validated live against the real match-bug titles + a legit-title control set. Result: album+EP match **52.1% → 58.8%**, match-bugs **32 → 18**. Does NOT cover singles/compilations behind the album/ep type filter or K-pop wrapper titles ("NewJeans 2nd EP 'Get Up'"). (2) **Accent folding** — still open: "Blase" (query) ≠ "BLASÉ" (catalog); normalize_text keeps the é. Needs an `unaccent` in normalize_text + a GIN reindex maintenance window (the functional indexes are built on normalize_text, so changing it without reindexing corrupts them) — deliberately deferred. (3) **Real coverage gaps** — the remaining 81 alb+ep misses are now mostly genuine (Japanese-heavy, ~34% album match); the report's `catalogGapArtists` MBID list is the goal-directed seed set for the pipeline (#3).
- **Two blockers flagged (not code): `CRON_SECRET` still not on Vercel** — only 1 of 167 profiles has any collected Spotify taste data, so `/api/cron/refresh-spotify-taste` is not actually running in prod. And the durable stale-name fix ([mb-ingest.ts:206](apps/web/scripts/mb-ingest.ts) early-returns without refreshing `artists.name` on re-poll) remains open.

**2026-07-10 (Windows) — Applied the two "ready to apply" advisor migrations from the Mac handoff (Security Definer View + RLS auth-InitPlan), fixed two stale policy names + one missed policy the migration author couldn't see from the Mac, and consolidated `ratings`' untracked duplicate policies:** Picked up the "Still open, ready to apply" bucket in README's START HERE. All applied live via `scripts/db-exec.ts` (Management API), each verified against `pg_policies` before and after.

- **`20260708000000` (critic_affiliation → `security_invoker=true`)** — applied clean; confirmed `pg_class.reloptions = {security_invoker=true}`. View exists as expected; both underlying tables have permissive reads so no visibility change.
- **`20260708000001` (RLS auth-InitPlan wrap)** — first run **failed loudly and atomically** (`BEGIN/COMMIT` rolled the whole thing back — nothing partially applied) on `policy "list_item_tracks_insert" does not exist`. Rather than fix-and-retry one error at a time, dumped every policy name for all 30 tables the migration touches and diffed against the `ALTER POLICY` targets up front. Found exactly **two stale names** the Mac author guessed wrong (both were tables tracked only in the top-level `supabase/migrations` dir, so the author never had the real names): `list_item_tracks_insert`/`_delete` → live names `lit_insert`/`lit_delete`; `reports_insert` → live name `"users can report"`. Verified each one's live clause first (`lit_*` join shape and `reports.reporter_id`) so the rename preserved the intended column, edited the migration file, re-ran → clean. Then a correct bare-`auth.` scan of `public` surfaced **one policy the migration missed** — `daily_answers_delete_own` (it wrapped select/insert/update but not delete) — added it to the same file and re-ran (ALTER POLICY is idempotent, so re-running the whole file just re-set already-wrapped clauses). Post-state: only 3 bare-auth policies remain in `public`, all of them the documented out-of-scope `releases` ones.
- **`20260710000000` (NEW — drop 4 untracked `ratings` duplicate policies)** — Mac header note #5 predicted `ratings` had ~7 policies, not 4, because of untracked `"Allow authenticated users to …"` policies created via the SQL editor (in no migration file). Confirmed live: **8 policies**. Pulled all their `qual`/`with_check` and proved pairwise that each untracked one is fully superseded by its tracked `ratings_*` counterpart (DELETE/INSERT byte-identical; the untracked SELECT is `auth.uid()=user_id`, a strict subset of `ratings_select`'s `true` public-read, so dropping it changes nobody's visibility; the untracked UPDATE equals `ratings_update`, whose null `with_check` Postgres fills from its identical USING). Wrote it as a **tracked** migration (the whole problem was untracked SQL-editor drift) and applied → `ratings` down to **4** policies, all confirmed wrapped.
- **Found but deliberately NOT fixed (scope): the same untracked-duplicate pattern exists on `releases`.** Its untracked INSERT/SELECT policies are likewise redundant subsets of `releases_insert`/`releases_select` (both `true`), **but** `"Allow authenticated users to update releases"` is the **only** UPDATE policy on the table — no tracked `releases_update` exists — so dropping it would remove authenticated UPDATE entirely. Consolidating `releases` requires first creating a proper tracked `releases_update`, which is a decision, not a mechanical drop. Left for a dedicated pass; documented in the `20260710000000` migration header and README. (Also still unwrapped, intentionally: the optional `storage.objects` avatar block at the bottom of `20260708000001`.)

Wrote a dedicated **"⚑ FOR WINDOWS"** block at the top of README's START HERE checklist (ahead of the individual fix entries below it) covering: (1) MB release-vs-release-group title divergence — unaudited scope, needs a sampling script + a durable storage decision since a raw `title` overwrite gets reverted by the next freshness re-poll; (2) no cross-source QC on Cover Art Archive images (~224.5k of ~295k covers) — one confirmed bad image, no idea how common; (3) artist canonical names can drift stale from MB's current primary name with nothing to catch it — flagged that a systematic backfill needs a stable MB-artist join key first, which doesn't obviously exist on `artists` today (worth checking `mb-ingest.ts`'s artist-insert paths); (4) `search_release_groups` RPC's hardcoded `album`/`ep`-only type filter, patched around client-side but not fixed at the source. **2026-07-09 (Mac, session 7 cont'd) — Data fix: "3 STEPS FORWARD, 2 STEPS BACK" (YANGHONGWON) was in the catalog under a different title than Spotify/the user knew it by, plus a wrong (community-sourced) cover from Cover Art Archive — both root-caused against live MB/Deezer/Spotify data and corrected:**

Follow-up to the earlier "why is Onyx not in the catalog" investigation — a second real "why is X missing" report, but a genuinely different root cause this time, not a repeat of the pipeline-staleness/MB-coverage-gap class.

- **Title mismatch, not a missing row.** The album *is* in `release_groups` (`id bceb9fc2…`, correct artist link, correct 2021-06-19 date) — but its `title` was `"오보에"` (Korean, "Oboe"), which is MusicBrainz's **release-group** title for this record. Checked MB directly: the release-group's own alias list is empty, but the underlying MB **release** entity (a distinct object one level down, MBID `b1b81e59…`) carries a different title — `"3 STEPS FORWARD, 2 STEPS BACK"`, disambiguation "OBOE". Our ingest (`mb-ingest.ts`) only ever captures the release-*group* title into `release_groups.title`, never the individual release's own (occasionally different) title — so the English name Spotify displays never had anywhere to land. This is why `fetchRelease()`'s exact-match found nothing (Spotify's string vs. our Korean-only title) and the fuzzy fallback also found nothing (no lexical overlap between an English and a Korean string for the fuzzy RPC to catch).
- **Cover was wrong at the source, not a matching bug.** `cover_url` correctly pointed at Cover Art Archive's entry for the *correct* release MBID — CAA just has exactly one (community-approved) image for it, and that image doesn't match the real artwork at all (confirmed by downloading and viewing both: CAA's image is unrelated silhouette art on black; Spotify's and Deezer's own covers for this exact release/track-count match are identical to each other — the real striped "오보에" collage cover). A straightforward case of a bad contribution accepted into CAA's review process for an obscure non-Western release, unrelated to anything in our own pipeline.
- **Fix applied as a direct, verified data correction** (no code change needed — the app's existing exact-match/fuzzy logic and `displayTitle` Hangul-preference logic already handle this correctly once the data is right): `title` → `"3 STEPS FORWARD, 2 STEPS BACK"` (matching MB's own release-level title and what Spotify calls it), `native_title` left untouched (`"오보에"`, still correctly Hangul so `displayTitle` keeps showing it in-app — verified live, the album's nav title still reads "오보에"), `cover_url` → Deezer's matching cover (`dzcdn.net`, cross-verified identical to Spotify's own art), `source` left as `"musicbrainz"` (confirmed this is the existing convention — ~5.3k other rows already have a Deezer-hosted `cover_url` with `source: musicbrainz`, since `source` tracks catalog provenance, not cover-art provenance). Verified live via idb: the row now opens the real album page with the correct cover and full Korean tracklist, no rebuild required.
- **Durability caveat, flagged not fixed:** `mb-ingest.ts`'s upsert always writes `title: rg.title` (the release-*group* title) on conflict, keyed by `mb_release_group_id`. If this specific artist is ever freshness-re-polled in the future (currently inert — the whole pipeline is paused, see the same-day Simon Dominic/Onyx entry), this one row's `title` would get silently reverted back to `"오보에"` by the ingest script itself. A fully durable fix would mean teaching the ingest to also capture and preserve a release-level "marketed title" separate from the release-group title — a real but out-of-scope enhancement for this session (touches the shared ingest pipeline broadly, not just this one reported row).

**Follow-up, same investigation — a second, unrelated data bug caught by the user ("Young B is YANGHONGWON's old name"): the artist's own canonical `artists.name` was stuck on his retired stage name, breaking name search for his current one entirely.** Checked live: `artists.name = "Young B"` for this artist's row (`id fda8aa33…`), even though MusicBrainz's own primary name for the same MBID is `"YANGHONGWON"` (disambiguation: "Korean rapper, formerly Young B") and every other source (Spotify, Deezer) files him under the new name too. Confirmed the breakage directly: `search_artists('YANGHONGWON')` returned **zero rows** before the fix — the main search bar, `resolveArtist()`, and any other name-based artist lookup would have silently failed to find him by his current, more recognized name. Root cause is upstream of this session (some earlier ingest pass captured the artist via an older/aliased credit before his current MB primary name was read) — not something introduced today. Fixed with a direct one-row correction: `artists.name` → `"YANGHONGWON"`, matching MB's own canonical name. Lower risk than the title fix above: confirmed `mb-ingest.ts` only ever `.insert()`s new artist rows, never re-upserts `name` on an existing one, so this won't get silently reverted by a future re-poll. Verified live via idb: `search_artists('YANGHONGWON')` now returns the artist (previously 8, and the RPC's own count field disagrees with the identity-aware release list — worth another look sometime, not chased further here); navigating album → artist now shows "YANGHONGWON" as the page title with all **10** releases correctly unified under one artist page — both his "Young B"-credited releases (Stranger, Dingo X Indigo Music, 아침에, SOkoNYUN, TEEN TITANS) and his "YANGHONGWON"-credited ones (오보에/3 STEPS FORWARD, SLOWMO, drugonline gate, Hey, The End) now show up together, where before the identity-based lookup would have worked fine via `artistId` once resolved, but *resolving* that id by name search was the part that was broken.
