**2026-07-09 (Mac, session 7 cont'd) — Album↔artist↔song cross-navigation fixed (double-push bug: tapping through to a new screen silently re-showed the previous one on top), plus the Artist page's Songs tab made functional (was fully inert, no tap, no add button):**

User report, three symptoms on the same underlying bug: (1) tapping the artist name on an album page ("Fred again.." from USB) appeared to reopen the album page instead of going to the artist; (2) tapping a song in an artist's Songs tab did nothing, and rows had no add/rate affordance at all; (3) tapping an album from an artist page reopened the artist page instead of the album.

**Root cause, found in two layers, both real and both fixed:**

1. **Duplicate `.navigationDestination(for:)` registrations for the same type within one `NavigationStack`.** `AlbumDetailView` declared its own `.navigationDestination(for: ArtistDestination.self)` in addition to `SearchView`'s root-level one; `ArtistPageView` (also in `SearchView.swift`) declared its own `.navigationDestination(for: Release.self)` in addition to every hosting stack's root-level one. SwiftUI does not support two registrations for the same type coexisting in one stack — this is undefined/buggy, not just redundant. Fixed by normalizing to the convention already used correctly for `Release` everywhere: every stack that can host `AlbumDetailView`/`ArtistPageView` (`HomeView`, `RankingsView`, `ProfileView` ×2, `SearchView`, plus the `HomeView` "add rating" sheet's standalone `NavigationStack`) now declares **both** `Release` and `ArtistDestination` once at its own root; `AlbumDetailView`/`ArtistPageView` themselves no longer declare either.
2. **The deeper cause of the actual double-push symptom: stale item-based navigation bindings.** `SearchView`'s `recentlyPlayedNavTarget`/`spotifyArtistNavTarget` (`.navigationDestination(item:)`, used for Spotify-suggested rows that need an async lookup before navigating — see the earlier same-day fetchRelease/resolveArtist entry) were set once and never reset to `nil`. Confirmed live via idb + the accessibility tree: tapping "USB" (item-bound) → tapping "Fred again.." (path-based push) landed on the *correct* artist page, but the still-non-nil `recentlyPlayedNavTarget` binding got spuriously re-presented on top of it, hiding the artist page behind a fresh, not-yet-loaded second copy of the album page (visible in the wild via reset "credits" styling and a missing tracklist mid-transition). Fixed by resetting each item binding to `nil` in `.onDisappear` on its own destination closure — applied to `recentlyPlayedNavTarget`/`spotifyArtistNavTarget` (`SearchView`), the new `songNavTarget` (`ArtistPageView`, see below), and `AlbumDetailView`'s pre-existing `selectedSong`, since all four have the same shape (an item-based destination whose content can itself push further).

**Songs tab was previously fully inert** — `ArtistSongRow` had no `NavigationLink`, no tap handler, and no score/add indicator at all (confirmed by direct code read, not just the user's report). Built out to match `ArtistReleaseRow`'s existing pattern:
- New `songNavTarget` item-based nav target (`SongNavTarget: Identifiable, Hashable`) on `ArtistPageView`, pushing `SongDetailView` with a `Release`/`TrackEntry` synthesized from the already-fetched `ArtistSong` data (both are plain memberwise-init structs, so no new query needed for the navigation itself).
- `loadSongs()` extended to also fetch each track's real `position` (from `release_tracks`, same canonical-preference logic already used for picking the album) and each user's own per-track score (`track_ratings.user_id`, mirroring the exact pattern already used for `myRatings` on the Albums tab) — so the song detail page shows the correct track number and "my score" instead of a placeholder.
- `ArtistSongRow` rewritten as a `Button` with the same trailing indicator convention as `ArtistReleaseRow` (my score in blue → community avg in amber → "+" circle if unrated).
- Added a lightweight `loadRatingMode()` fetch (same shape as `AlbumDetailViewModel`'s) so `SongDetailView` gets the user's real rating mode instead of defaulting to "manual" for every artist-page-originated song tap.

Verified live end-to-end via idb, all four paths: USB → Fred again.. (artist, direct, no double-push) → Lights Burn Dimmer (album, direct, no double-push back to artist) → Songs tab (now shows "+" on every row) → ".five" (opens real `SongDetailView`, correct "9번 트랙" position, correct artist, rate button) → tapped the artist link from *inside* the song page (the same bug class, pre-emptively fixed) → correct artist page again, no double-push.

**Known deferred gap, unchanged from earlier today:** `appleMusicArtistScroll` still uses the old ungated `artistId: nil` pattern (out of scope — today's reports were all Spotify/organic in-app navigation, not Apple Music rows).

**2026-07-09 (Mac, session 7 cont'd) — Add tab: Spotify-suggested album/artist rows fixed (were showing "not in catalog" or empty/wrong artist pages), three real bugs found and fixed in sequence via live testing:**

User report: tapping a Spotify-suggested album showed "카탈로그에 없음" (not in catalog) even for albums that are genuinely in the catalog; tapping a Spotify-suggested artist showed either an empty or an erroneous artist profile. Root cause for both: Spotify's title/artist/name strings rarely match the catalog's stored strings byte-for-byte (punctuation, romanization, capitalization), and the existing lookup paths (`fetchRelease` doing an exact `.ilike()`, artist taps using a bare `NavigationLink(value: ArtistDestination(artistId: nil, ...))`) had no tolerance for that. Fixed by routing both through the fuzzy `search_release_groups`/`search_artists` RPCs already built for the main search bar — but three further real bugs surfaced live along the way, each found by testing against real accepted/rejected cases rather than assumed:

1. **Blind fuzzy fallback showed confidently wrong results.** First attempt returned the fuzzy RPC's top hit unconditionally. Confirmed live: "Father EP" by Masta Wu (genuinely not in the catalog) fuzzy-matched to Vampire Weekend's "Father of the Bride" and would have silently navigated there. Fixed by requiring the candidate's artist to actually substring-match the query artist before trusting any result — no match, no fallback, honest "not in catalog" instead.
2. **Same class of bug on the artist side.** `search_artists("Masta Wu", lim: 1)` confidently returns "Masta Killa" — an unrelated artist sharing one word. Fixed `resolveArtist` (new function) the same way: only accept a candidate if its `name` *or* `name_native` overlaps the query (equality or substring, either direction) — the native-name check is what lets a Korean-rendered Spotify name like "사이먼 도미닉" still correctly resolve to "Simon Dominic."
3. **Fuzzy RPC's own type filter excluded real catalog hits.** "USB" by Fred again.. (genuinely in the catalog) still showed "not in catalog" — `search_release_groups` hardcodes `WHERE rg.release_group_type IN ('album', 'ep')`, silently excluding this row because it's stored as `'compilation'`. Fixed by restructuring `fetchRelease` to try an unrestricted exact `.ilike()` against `release_groups` directly first, only falling back to the type-restricted fuzzy RPC if that finds nothing.

Verified live end-to-end via idb after all three fixes: "USB" now opens the real album page (Compilation, Fred again.., 2022, correct tracklist); "사이먼 도미닉" now opens the real Simon Dominic artist page (10 releases, 3.2 avg); "dress" (a deliberately-ambiguous common-word artist name, chosen as a stress test) correctly opens the real "dress" artist page (17 releases) rather than a fuzzy false positive.

**Known deferred gap, not yet fixed:** Apple Music-suggested artist rows (`appleMusicArtistScroll`) still use the old `artistId: nil` pattern and would show the same empty/wrong-artist symptom — out of scope for this fix since the user's report specifically named Spotify-suggested rows; Apple Music-suggested *albums* already benefit since they share `fetchRelease`.

**2026-07-09 (Mac, session 7 cont'd) — Apple Music: one real gap fixed (`load()` never retried it); an in-app "connect" banner was built, then explicitly rejected and reverted per user feedback:**

Follow-up to the Spotify `appMetadata["provider"]` bug fixed earlier the same day — asked to check whether the same class of issue applied to Apple Music. It didn't in the *literal* sense (Apple Music's gate is `MusicAuthorization.currentStatus`, an on-device permission with no Supabase identity involved — no `provider`/`providers` mismatch is possible there), but reading the surrounding flow surfaced one real, separate gap, kept:

- **`load()` never retried Apple Music.** The existing `else if !hasSpotifyData { await loadSpotify() }` fallback (for a user who reconnects mid-session) had no Apple Music equivalent — `loadAppleMusic()` ran exactly once per `DiscoveryViewModel` lifetime. Fixed by retrying both independently in the `else` branch: `if !hasSpotifyData { await loadSpotify() }; if !hasAppleMusicData { await loadAppleMusic() }`. This is fully invisible to the user — it just means if Apple Music access is ever granted some other way (e.g. directly via iOS Settings), the app picks it up on the next tab visit instead of needing a full relaunch.

Also built, then reverted: an in-app "Connect Apple Music" banner (mirroring `spotifyReconnectBanner`) plus a `MusicKitService.isDenied`-aware Settings-deep-link for the case where the system dialog won't reappear. Verified fully working live before removal — tapped "연결하기," got the real iOS MusicKit permission dialog with the exact `NSAppleMusicUsageDescription` text, granted it, banner correctly cleared. **Explicit user feedback: not wanted.** Reasoning given — cross-connecting a second listening service post-onboarding is low-value (few users have both Spotify and Apple Music), and the intended design is everything wired up once during signup/onboarding with the user never prompted again afterward. Fully reverted: the banner view, `connectAppleMusic()`, `needsAppleMusicPrompt`, `isRequestingAppleMusic`, `MusicKitService.isDenied`, the now-unused `import UIKit`, and the 3 Korean localization entries added for it. Only the invisible `load()` retry fix above survived.

**Same-session follow-up: the pre-existing Spotify reconnect banner (`spotifyReconnectBanner`, shipped before today) removed too, for the same stated reason — and because today's refresh pipeline made it functionally dead anyway.** User asked directly: "it is not necessary anyways if we automatically have the data updated, am I correct?" — traced the exact condition: `needsSpotifyReconnect` only ever becomes `true` when the account is Spotify-linked AND `hasSpotifyData` is `false` (nothing in local cache *or* DB) AND the client's own live token is dead. Since the pipeline now writes to `profiles.spotify_artists`/`spotify_recently_played` both on every successful client-side fetch *and* daily via cron, any account that has connected even once will have non-empty DB data indefinitely — so `hasSpotifyData` stays `true` forever after the first successful fetch, and the banner's trigger condition becomes practically unreachable. Confirmed correct. Removed `spotifyReconnectBanner`, `reconnectSpotify()`, `isReconnectingSpotify`, and the 3 now-orphaned Korean localization entries (`Connect Spotify`/`Link your account to see personalized suggestions`/`Reconnect`, all confirmed unused elsewhere before deleting). Kept `needsSpotifyReconnect` itself and `refreshSpotifyIfNeeded()` — the latter is an existing *invisible* foreground-refresh retry with no UI, same category as the Apple Music `load()` fix, not the kind of post-onboarding prompt the user was objecting to. Verified live: fresh launch, no banners at all, Spotify rows still render from real data.

**2026-07-09 (Mac, session 7 cont'd) — Spotify taste-data refresh pipeline built (prep for a future "Quick Add" tab), verified live end-to-end with a real account:**

Prompted by a "Quick Add" feature idea (an infinite manual-mode-only unrated-releases list with half-star tap-to-rate, gated with a mode-switch popup for instinct users) — before building that, investigated what real signal exists for "this user has probably already heard album X." Findings, checked directly against the live DB rather than assumed:

- **1 of 167 profiles had any `spotify_artists` data at all** (3 artists, 2+ weeks stale), **0 had `spotify_recently_played`.** The separate `spotify_connections` table (used by the existing playlist-export feature) had **0 rows** — completely unused. Root cause: the Spotify provider refresh token was already being captured client-side at login (`sillajukuApp.swift`) but only ever written to `UserDefaults`, never persisted anywhere a server could reach it — so once the ~1hr access token expired, `spotify_artists`/`spotify_recently_played` silently stopped updating for good, with no error and no user-visible signal.
- Confirmed via Supabase Dashboard that the login-flow Spotify app and the export-feature Spotify app (`SPOTIFY_CLIENT_ID`/`SPOTIFY_CLIENT_SECRET` in `apps/web/.env.local`) are the **same** registered app — meaning a refresh token captured at login *can* be redeemed with credentials already in this repo.

**Built:**
- **`20260709000000_spotify_taste_tokens.sql`** — new table, deliberately kept separate from `spotify_connections` (different OAuth scopes for a different feature; sharing one row risked one flow's token silently clobbering the other's). RLS written with `(select auth.uid())` from the start, matching the perf-fix pattern already applied elsewhere in `20260708000001_rls_auth_initplan_fix.sql`.
- **`SpotifyService.saveTasteRefreshToken`** (iOS) + one new line in `sillajukuApp.swift`'s auth observer — the refresh token that was already being captured and discarded now also gets upserted server-side.
- **`apps/web/app/api/cron/refresh-spotify-taste/route.ts`** — a `CRON_SECRET`-gated route (matching the existing `SEED_SECRET`/`PUSH_WEBHOOK_SECRET` convention) that iterates every stored refresh token, redeems it via Spotify's `/api/token` (persisting a rotated refresh token if Spotify returns one — a gap the existing playlist-export route has and this new code doesn't repeat), then calls `/me/top/artists` and `/me/player/recently-played` and writes the result into `profiles`, porting `SpotifyService.swift`'s exact dedup-by-album-id logic to TypeScript so both write paths produce identical shapes. Also fixes the pre-existing bug where `spotify_data_updated_at` was only bumped on the artists write, never the recently-played one.
- Root `vercel.json` gets a daily `crons` entry (confirmed it's the file Vercel actually reads for this deployment; `apps/web/vercel.json` is vestigial).
- Deliberately did **not** touch `app/api/spotify/export/route.ts` — duplicated its ~15-line refresh-token exchange locally instead of extracting a shared helper, to avoid modifying an unrelated, currently-working feature.

**Verified fully live, not just compiled:** confirmed the error path first (seeded a fake refresh token, hit the route, got `{"refreshed":0,"failed":1,"total":1}` with no crash) before testing the real path — signed out and back in with Spotify on the simulator to exercise the actual `signInWithOAuth` capture, confirmed a real refresh token landed in `spotify_taste_tokens`, then ran the cron route locally and confirmed `profiles.spotify_artists` picked up a genuinely new artist ("Simon Dominic") and `spotify_recently_played` went from `null` to 5 real albums (YANGHONGWON, Masta Wu, Fred again.., Skepta, Simon Dominic) with a correctly-bumped `spotify_data_updated_at`.

**⚑ ACTION NEEDED:** add the real `CRON_SECRET` value to the Vercel project's environment variables (not just `.env.local`) — Vercel only auto-attaches the cron `Authorization` header once it's set there. Without this the scheduled job will 401 against itself after the next deploy.

Quick Add itself (the actual unrated-releases tab) is intentionally not started yet — this was purely the data-foundation prerequisite, per the user's explicit sequencing call ("Spotify pipeline first, then Quick Add").

**Follow-up, same day — a real, pre-existing client bug found and fixed: the Add tab's "Your Top Artists"/"Recently Listened" rows never rendered for this account, even with the pipeline above now populating genuinely fresh data.** User noticed the rows were missing entirely. Root-caused live (not guessed): `SearchView.swift`'s `loadSpotify()` gates on `supabase.auth.currentUser?.appMetadata["provider"]` — the account's **original signup method** — equaling `"spotify"`. Checked this account's actual auth record directly via the GoTrue admin API: `app_metadata.provider = "email"` (signed up 2026-04-15), with Spotify linked *afterward* on 2026-05-15 — `app_metadata.providers` (the full identity list) correctly includes `"spotify"`, but the singular field never does for any multi-identity account, no matter how recently or how many times Spotify's actually been used to sign in. Every single load was silently calling `SpotifyService.clearCache()` and returning before even attempting the DB/live-API fetches — meaning this row has probably never worked for any account that linked Spotify after signup rather than signing up with it directly, independent of today's pipeline work. Fixed by checking `appMetadata["providers"]` (the array) for `"spotify"` instead of the singular field. Verified live: fresh app launch (ruling out any stale-state explanation) now correctly shows both rows with the real data from the pipeline above — "Fred again..", "사이먼 도미닉" (Simon Dominic), "dress", "검정치마" (The Black Skirts) for top artists; YANGHONGWON/Masta Wu/Fred again../Skepta for recently listened.

**2026-07-09 (Mac, session 7 cont'd) — rating modal: comment box no longer clips the sheet, fixed in all three post-rating flows:**

User report: "rating modal > add comment > when i click add comment and the text box shows up, the size of the modal should increase respectively, but it doesn't... cuts off the top part of the modal." Root cause: `PostRatingOptionsView` (the shared "Add a comment / Add to a list / Continue" sheet content, reused by 4 call sites) is hosted inside a `.presentationDetents` set to a **fixed** size for the post-rating phase — tapping "Add a comment" expands a `TextEditor` that grows the content past that fixed height, and nothing re-measures the sheet.

- **`PostRatingOptionsView.swift`**: added an optional `onHeightChange: ((CGFloat) -> Void)?` callback (default `nil`, so the other call sites need no changes) and a `GeometryReader` background that reports the VStack's real content height via `onAppear`/`onChange` — the same measure-and-feed-into-`.presentationDetents` pattern already proven in this session's `BadgeRedeemView` fix.
- **`ManualRatingSheet` (`AlbumDetailView.swift`)** — the manual-mode rating flow: was hard-locked to `.medium` on entering the post-rating phase. Now uses `.height(postRatingHeight)`, updated live from the callback.
- **`InstinctRatingView` and `InstinctTrackRatingView` (`InstinctRatingView.swift`)** — the instinct-mode flows (album and per-track): actually **more broken** than the manual one — their `.onChange(of: phase)` unconditionally reset `sheetDetent` back to `.fraction(0.36)` on *every* phase change, including into `.postRating`, so the comment box never had room to begin with. Fixed the same way, with the `onChange` now skipping the reset when the new phase is `.postRating`.
- Found by testing live: this Mac's test account (`car`) is in **Instinct** mode, so the bug as actually experienced was the `InstinctRatingView` one, not `ManualRatingSheet` — good thing both got fixed, not just the first one found by reading the code.
- Verified live end-to-end via idb on a fresh (unrated) album: bucket sheet (BAD/NEUTRAL/GOOD) → GOOD → post-rating sheet at its collapsed height, fully visible → tap "Add a comment" → sheet visibly grows to fit the expanded `TextEditor`, header through the Continue/Back buttons all stay on-screen, nothing clipped. Dismissed without saving (rating count unchanged after).

**2026-07-09 (Mac, session 7 cont'd) — Artist page: album filter/sort, Community post/list toggle, split Popular Albums/Popular Songs stats, corrected default sort orders:**

Four related asks on `ArtistPageView` (`SearchView.swift`), all verified live via idb against real artists (TVXQ, NewJeans) rather than just compiled:

- **Albums tab got a filter/sort bar** (`ArtistAlbumTypeFilter`: All/Album/EP/Single pills; `ArtistAlbumSortOrder`: Newest/Oldest/Top Rated/A–Z menu, matching `ProfileView`'s existing filter-pill + sort-`Menu` pattern). **Default is Newest** — release date descending, computed client-side in a new `filteredSortedReleases` property rather than trusting the `get_artist_release_groups` RPC's own ordering. That RPC turned out to need this: it's `SELECT DISTINCT ON (rg.id) ... ORDER BY rg.id, rg.first_release_date DESC` — since `DISTINCT ON`'s leading `ORDER BY` column must lead the *output* order too, the real row order was by `rg.id` (an arbitrary UUID), not by date at all, despite looking date-sorted at a glance. Confirmed live on TVXQ (60 releases): before, order was effectively random; after, IDENTITY (2026) → ZONE (2024) → ... → oldest, correctly descending. Not touched server-side — client-side sort was sufficient and avoids another migration in the current apply-migrations-manually bottleneck.
- **Community tab now defaults to post view, with list view as an explicit toggle** — same `list.bullet`/`newspaper` icon pair as `ProfileView`'s own list/posts switch, "just like the profile tab" per the ask. New `communityPostCard` (avatar, @handle, timestamp, larger cover, title, score, review text if present) sits alongside the pre-existing compact `communityRow`. Added `review_text` to the underlying `ratings` select and `CommunityRating` so post view can actually show reviews. Deliberately did **not** wire up like/comment buttons on these cards — that data was never loaded for this feed and doing so is a materially bigger feature than "list vs. post display density," which is what was actually asked for.
- **Stats tab split into separate Popular Albums / Popular Songs sections.** "Top Releases" renamed to **Popular Albums** (Korean was already "인기 릴리스" — literally "popular releases," so the English rename just caught up to the existing Korean copy). Added a parallel **Popular Songs** section (top 3 by avg score, min 1 rating, navigates to the song's album since there's no per-song detail route wired from this page) — gated on `songs.contains(where: { $0.ratingCount > 0 })` so it doesn't render an empty header when an artist has zero song-level ratings (confirmed both states live: hidden for TVXQ with 0 track_ratings, shown for NewJeans with "Hype boy" at #1).
- **Songs tab default order fixed to "most-rated first, ties broken by newest release."** `ArtistSong` gained `ratingCount`/`avgScore`/`releaseDate`, populated in `loadSongs()` from a new `track_ratings` query (grouped by `recording_id`, counting every row — including instinct-only rows with `score == nil` but `elo_score` set — to match the album-side `communityCount` convention of counting all ratings, not just manual-mode ones). Verified live: NewJeans' only rated song ("Hype boy," 1 rating) correctly sorts to #1 above 11 unrated tracks.
- Added 6 new Korean localization keys (Newest/Oldest/All/Popular Albums/Popular Songs/the filtered-empty-state message) — first attempt at the `.xcstrings` edit used `sort_keys=True` in the Python dump and reordered the entire multi-thousand-line file (564 insertions/442 deletions in the diff); caught via `git diff --stat` before it was ever built, reverted, redone without `sort_keys` so only the 6 new entries plus a few pre-existing empty-`{}` reformats show up in the diff.

**2026-07-08 (Windows, session 5) — covers-never-loaded root cause (CSP redirect hop) + img-proxy 502 fix + chunk-skew auto-reload; DB measured healthy:**

User reported covers never load on web and pages needing 2–3 reloads. Three separate root causes found:

1. **Covers: CSP blocked the CAA redirect chain.** `coverartarchive.org` art (≈95% of the catalog) 307-redirects to `archive.org/download/…` which 302s again to `ia…us.archive.org` / `dn…ca.archive.org` — and img-src only allowed the bare `https://archive.org` host, so browsers blocked the final hop and every CAA cover silently placeholder'd (broken since the rebuild wrote this CSP; direct curl always worked, which is why probes never caught it). Fixed: `https://*.archive.org` added to img-src + remotePatterns.
2. **`/api/img` proxy 502 (the Mac-flagged one) had TWO causes:** archive.org rejects Vercel *edge* egress (mzstatic proxied fine, CAA always 502'd) → runtime switched edge→nodejs (`maxDuration 15`); AND CAA/archive.org fails transiently on cold requests even from residential IPs (observed: first hit 502, immediate retry 200) → the route now retries once (250ms gap), passes 404 through, sends `Accept: image/*`. With the 1-year immutable edge cache each image only ever needs to succeed once. **Web `Cover.tsx` now routes CAA art through the proxy** (instant edge hits after first load instead of 1.5–3s per image through the redirect chain) with a graceful ladder: proxy → direct URL → placeholder, reset when the url prop changes. iOS gets the proxy fix for free.
3. **2–3-reloads instability = deployment chunk skew, not the DB** — measured twice under anon: every query green, `get_silla_leaderboard` 0.5–0.9s (the Mac's `primary_genre` side-table fix ended the load incident — closed in README). Eight deploys in one day meant clients holding an old HTML shell requested chunk hashes the new deploy doesn't serve → ChunkLoadError → manual reloads. `app/error.tsx` now detects chunk-load errors and reloads once automatically (sessionStorage guard against loops, cleared by a successful shell render). Consider enabling Vercel Skew Protection in the dashboard as the infra-level fix.

README updated: DB-load incident closed, fix-list item 1 struck, covers + skew items added.

**2026-07-08 (Windows, session 4) — album rating UX rework: inline auto-save editor replaces the modal; global cursor tooltips:**

The album page's manual rating flow no longer uses a modal at all — new `InlineRatingEditor.tsx`:

- **Collapsed row has one anatomy rated or not**: 5 stars (empty at 25% opacity when unrated, filled by score when rated — the old "Rate this album" button is gone) + an edit glyph. Clicking a star opens the editor preloaded with that value; hovering previews the fill.
- **The editor is an in-flow dropdown panel** (`sj-pop-in`, pushes content, no overlay/backdrop — editing never displaces the page). **No Save button: every change auto-saves** (500ms debounce, "Saved ✓" flash, pending edits flush on close/outside-click/Enter/Esc).
- **Undo** reverts to the value the session started with (session started unrated → undo deletes the rating). Trash removes outright.
- **Scroll wheel** anywhere over the panel nudges by one step (native non-passive listener — React synthetic wheel can't preventDefault).
- **The number is focused + selected on entry** so digits type straight in; "43" and "4.3" both become 4.3 (period auto-inserted after the first digit); values clamp to 0.5–5 and snap to the user's step.
- **Comment box is always visible** once rated (placeholder "Add a comment") — replaces the modal's post-rating comment step; debounce+blur auto-saves `review_text`, and `loadRatings` won't clobber in-progress typing (activeElement check).
- **Add to a list** is now its own `ListPlus` icon in the section header → `MixPickerModal` directly.
- Two modal bugs fixed while at it: `ManualRateModal`'s reset effect had `existingScore` as a dep, so saving (which refreshes the parent's score) re-ran the reset and yanked users out of the post-rating step mid-flow (the reported "save doesn't work when editing"); and the search page's quick-rate `onSave(null)` (Remove Rating) silently no-op'd — now deletes the row and clears the rated badges. The modal itself remains for search quick-rate and track rating.
- **Global `CursorTip.tsx`** (mounted once in AppShell, incl. bare pages): any element with an `aria-label`, no visible text, and no native `title` gets its label shown under the cursor after 400ms hover — covers every icon-only button app-wide (undo/trash/bookmark/bell/edit/…), hover-capable pointers only, dismisses on click/scroll.

New keys `sj.rate.undo`/`sj.rate.saved` (en/ko). `tsc` clean, ESLint clean, album/search/home 200 in mock.

- **Album rating histogram** (`RatingHistogram.tsx`, dataviz-skill-guided) — ten 0.5-wide buckets under the community stats, single-hue accent bars with rounded data ends and 2px gaps, per-bar hover values, the viewer's own bucket highlighted, `role=img` aria summary. Distribution computed from the ratings the page already fetches — zero new queries.
- **Hover peek cards** (`AlbumPeek.tsx`) — desktop answer to iOS long-press: hovering any album card/row on Charts (horizontal rails + Trending) or the Home trending rail shows a fixed-position card (cover, title/artist, live community avg + count, session-cached one query per album). Fixed positioning escapes `overflow-x-auto` clipping; any scroll dismisses.
- **Ranking drilldown filters** — `/charts/ranking` gains release-type (All/Album/EP) and era (2020s→1990s/Older) chips, filtering client-side on the fetched 100 (the silla RPC returns `release_group_type` + `release_date` since `20260706000018`; `SillaLeaderboardRPC` type extended, mock updated).
- **Tab a11y refits** — Artist page and Profile tab rows get `role=tablist/tab`, `aria-selected`, focus-visible rings; Artist's active underline switched from ink to accent to match the rest.

New i18n keys for all of it (en + ko). Verified: `tsc` clean, ESLint clean on all touched files, vitest 31/31, all 8 main routes 200 in mock mode.

**2026-07-08 (Windows) — UX pass 1: unified search omnibox (Search tab → Add), Silla badge trust fix, empty-state CTAs, accessible TitleTabs:**

Full web UX review done first (findings live in README → Known issues fix list + this entry), then implemented the top slice:

- **Unified search.** The top bar's dumb input (navigate-on-Enter only) is now `SearchOmnibox.tsx`: 200ms-debounced typeahead dropdown, ⌘K/Ctrl-K and `/` global focus shortcuts, arrow-key + Enter navigation, Esc dismiss, stale-response guard, "See all results for 'q'" → `/search?q=`. It's the single canonical search; the sidebar/bottom tab was renamed **Search → Add** (`CirclePlus` icon, `sj.nav.add` = Add/추가, matching iOS) since that page's real job is discovery + quick-rate. **`/api/search/suggest` was rewritten** — it was not just orphaned but *broken*, still querying pre-renovation columns (`releases.artist/release_type/prestige`, `artists.popularity`); now queries `release_groups` (3-arm prefix ilike incl. `native_title`, singles excluded, ordered by `prestige_score`) + `artists` (name/name_native), same Redis 10-min cache + CDN headers. Verified against the live DB (~3.8s cold on a broad prefix, cached after) and in mock.
- **Silla badge mislabel fixed** — the Ranking block's score badge (silla_score×5) was captioned "avg", which it isn't; now captioned **Silla** (실라) with a `title` tooltip explaining the score (`sj.charts.silla`/`sillaExplainer`). Drilldown badges were uncaptioned — left alone.
- **Home empty states got CTAs** — Explore-empty → "Rate your first album" button → `/search`; Following-empty → "Browse Explore" switches tabs. (The error-vs-empty distinction is still open on the fix list.)
- **`TitleTabs.tsx`** — shared page-title tab switcher (role=tablist/tab, aria-selected, roving tabIndex, arrow-key switching, focus-visible ring, animated accent underline). Fitted to Home (Explore/Following) and Charts (Albums/Songs); Artist/Profile tab rows are candidates for a later pass.

Remaining from the UX review (priority order): **Library surface for `saved_releases`** (bookmark button currently writes to a table nothing reads — biggest gap), avatar → dropdown menu (profile/settings/sign out), bell → notifications popover, error-vs-empty states, album-page rating histogram, profile Rated table view with sortable columns, hover peek cards, charts year/type filters.

**2026-07-08 (Mac, session 7) — Profile tab subtab swipe rebuilt to follow the finger; a durable idb-based UI-testing loop set up to actually verify it; artist-list-scrambling bug found and fixed:**

Continuation of an iterative Profile-tab thread (three subtabs — Rated/Lists/Stats — needed to scroll as one page but swipe independently between subtabs). After several rounds of "this still isn't right" from the user (HStack default-center alignment collapsing two subtabs to blank/endless-scroll, a static/non-interactive swipe, false clicks during swipe), the user asked directly whether there was a *systematic* way to verify this instead of guessing from static code reading — answer was yes: installed `idb`/`idb_companion` (Facebook's iOS UI-automation bridge) to actually script taps/swipes/screenshots against the simulator and look at the result, rather than reasoning about gesture-priority semantics from first principles.

- **`ProfileView.swift` swipe mechanics, final shape:** header (avatar, stats, action buttons, tab bar) stays a fixed `VStack`; the whole page (header + active subtab) sits in one outer `ScrollView` so vertical scroll moves everything together. Subtab content itself is a `ZStack` rendering only the active tab plus (during a drag) the adjacent tab being dragged toward, each offset by `dragTranslation` — so the screen visibly follows the finger and settles wherever it's released (mid-drag stop = mid-way pause), instead of a fixed-duration animated cut. `.highPriorityGesture(tabSwipeGesture)` (not plain `.gesture()`) with `minimumDistance: 15` — plain `.gesture()` does **not** out-prioritize a child's own tap/`NavigationLink`, confirmed by reproducing an actual false navigation push via idb (swiping over a post card opened the post instead of switching tabs) before the fix, and re-verifying the same swipe no longer does that after.
- **idb tooling notes worth keeping:** simulator build must be forced universal (`ONLY_ACTIVE_ARCH=NO ARCHS="arm64 x86_64"`, verified via `lipo -info`) or `idb install` rejects it with a misleading "architecture x86_64 not in supported architectures (arm64)" even on an all-arm64 Mac+simulator — idb's own arch check appears to require the fat binary regardless. Incremental builds sometimes silently stay arm64-only (Xcode skips the relink) even with the flags set — delete the `.app` bundle first to force a full relink if `lipo -info` doesn't show both slices. Build with normal automatic signing ("Sign to Run Locally"), not `CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO` — the latter broke Keychain-backed PKCE OAuth state (`"Invalid request: both auth code and code verifier should be non-empty"` on login). Screenshot coordinates from the accessibility tree (`idb ui describe-all`) can report scrolled-content coordinates that exceed the real on-screen point range — cross-check any tap/swipe coordinate against a known-visible element's frame before trusting it.
- **Bug found + fixed via this loop: Stats tab's "인기 아티스트" (top artists) list re-sorted itself on every render.** `RatingStatsSnapshot.compute()` sorted `artistCounts` (a `Dictionary`) by count only — Swift dictionaries have non-deterministic iteration order, so ties re-shuffle randomly across recomputes (visible to the user as the list scrambling between visits, reported directly). Fixed with a stable secondary sort key (artist name, alphabetical) purely to make output deterministic, not a meaningful ranking signal on its own. Verified fixed: re-navigated to the Stats tab via idb after a fresh reinstall, artist order (ATEEZ → E SENS → NewJeans, alphabetical within the tied count-3 group) held.
- **Follow-up, same day — the real "swipe from tab 2" bug, found and fixed.** The user clarified precisely: swiping over the mix rows on the Lists tab worked, but swiping over the *blank white space below them* did nothing. Root cause: `tabContent`'s `ZStack` (which carries `.contentShape(Rectangle())` + `.highPriorityGesture`) sizes itself to its content's natural height — for Lists with only 2 mixes, that's ~225pt, nowhere near the ~460pt of actual remaining screen space. The blank area below was genuinely outside the gesture's hit-test region, not a gesture-priority bug. Fixed by giving `tabContent` a `minHeight` floor (viewport height minus the hero's actual height, so short tabs still claim the full remaining screen; tabs taller than the viewport are unaffected). First attempt measured both heights via `PreferenceKey`/`onPreferenceChange` (the same pattern already working for `contentWidth`) but the values stayed stuck at 0 for reasons not fully understood — confirmed via a temporary color-coded debug overlay (`Color.yellow` background + a `Text` printing the live measured values) rather than guessing blind, which is what caught that the frame wasn't actually growing. Switched to a `GeometryReader` wrapping the whole `ScrollView` directly (for the outer viewport height) plus `onAppear`/`onChange` (not preference-relayed) for the hero's height — confirmed via the same debug overlay that real numbers now populate (`hh:267 min:461`) and the yellow region visibly extended to the tab bar, then verified via idb that swiping directly on the blank space now switches tabs in both directions. Debug overlay removed before the final build.
- **Diagnosed and fixed, same day — "album cover loading is suddenly extremely slow" traced to Windows's cover-proxy commit, already known-broken, and reverted client-side.** User asked to diagnose a sudden slowdown and to pull the other device's work first. `git fetch` showed 6 new Windows commits; pulling and reading them (plus the already-present `31d2a1a feat(covers): caching edge proxy...`) found the root cause was already root-caused and documented by Windows but not yet acted on client-side: `Theme.swift`'s `thumbnailUrl` routes **any CoverArtArchive/archive.org URL (~95% of the catalog)** through `{webBaseURL}/api/img?url=...`, a Vercel edge proxy meant to eliminate CAA's slow 307-redirect-to-archive.org hop. That proxy **returns 502 on prod** — archive.org blocks Vercel's datacenter IPs. Confirmed directly, not just from the docs: `curl`'d the live proxy (502 in 0.8s, body `upstream error`) vs. the direct CAA URL (200 in 2.4s, real image) using a real cover URL pulled from `release_groups` via the anon REST API; then watched it live in the simulator — Boyhood/TONE/Control sat on gray placeholders for 8+ seconds with zero change, confirming this is a hard failure, not just added latency, since `CachedImage`'s `URLSession.shared.data(from:)` doesn't throw on HTTP 502, so `UIImage(data:)` silently fails to parse the error body and the placeholder never resolves — and nothing caches the failure, so every render retries the same dead round-trip. User confirmed they want the fix applied now rather than waiting on Windows's proxy work: reverted `thumbnailUrl` to drop the `/api/img` routing entirely (CAA covers go direct again, ~2.4s but working, same as before `31d2a1a`). Rebuilt + reinstalled fresh in the simulator — Boyhood/TONE/Control all render real cover art now (Purple, the one iTunes-sourced cover in the visible feed, was unaffected the whole time since it never touched the proxy).

**2026-07-08 (Mac, session 7 cont'd) — notification taps silently went nowhere; root-caused to a `.navigationDestination(isPresented:)` / `.navigationDestination(for:)` conflict, fixed:**

User asked for notification-tap-to-relevant-page behavior; the code already had it fully built (`AppNotification.albumRelease`/`actorDestination`/`mixDestination` computed properties + matching `NavigationLink`s in `NotificationsView`), so this became a "why doesn't the existing code work" investigation rather than a build-from-scratch. Confirmed live via idb that tapping any row (follow → profile, like → album) visibly did nothing — screen never changed. Almost wrote this off as a testing artifact (the simulator was going idle between tool calls with 20-30 min real-world gaps, briefly making an unrelated reload look like the culprit), but ruled that out with an atomic tap-then-screenshot sequence in one shell call and it still failed. Compared against `MixLibraryView`'s `NavigationLink(value: mix)` inside a plain `LazyVStack` (confirmed working via idb) to isolate what was different about `NotificationsView`.

Root cause, caught via the accessibility tree rather than the screenshot alone: after tapping a follow row, the back button's label silently updated to the target user's handle (proving the `NavigationLink(value: UserProfileDestination)` *did* fire and compute the right destination) but the visible screen never changed — `HomeView`'s single `NavigationStack` mixed `.navigationDestination(isPresented: $showNotifications)` (used to present `NotificationsView` itself) with sibling `.navigationDestination(for: Release.self)` / `.navigationDestination(for: UserProfileDestination.self)` handlers (used for pushes *from within* it) — a combination that partially updates navigation-adjacent state (the back button title) without ever completing the actual push. Fixed by converting the Notifications entry point to the same value-based pattern already used for `FindPeopleDestination` elsewhere in this file: new `struct NotificationsDestination: Hashable {}`, bell button changed from a plain `Button { showNotifications = true }` to `NavigationLink(value: NotificationsDestination())`, and the `isPresented:`-based modifier replaced with `.navigationDestination(for: NotificationsDestination.self)`. Verified live via idb, both destination types: follow → `@lllllll`'s `UserProfileView` (Linus Kim), like → `ICEMAN`'s `AlbumDetailView` — both now actually render, not just update the back button.
- **Follow-up bug, same feature: album pages reached via a notification showed a permanently blank cover ("why is the ICEMAN cover taking forever to load").** Not a re-run of the earlier CAA-proxy incident (already reverted and confirmed working for every other cover this session) — this album's direct CAA URL loaded fine in under 2s via `curl`. The real cause: `AppNotification.albumRelease` builds its `Release` value with `coverUrl: nil` hardcoded, because `NotificationsView.load()`'s select string never fetched `release_groups.cover_url` in the first place — so `CoverImage`'s underlying `CachedImage` had a `nil` URL and never even attempted a fetch, showing the shimmer placeholder forever with nothing to eventually resolve (not slow — permanently empty). Fixed by adding `cover_url` to the notification query's embedded `release_groups` select and to `RatingInfo.ReleaseInfo`'s decoded fields, then threading it through into `albumRelease`. Verified live: the ICEMAN cover (and TONE, reached via a different notification) both render correctly now.

**2026-07-08 (Mac, session 7 cont'd) — mix page edit UX consolidated: 수정 sheet removed, 편집 now does inline title/description/visibility editing + a default-visible delete icon:**

Design discussion first (no restrictions on `mixes.name` length anywhere, DB or client; `description` has a DB check `char_length <= 500` with no client-side counter — flagged as pre-existing, not touched). Agreed shape: delete the separate "Edit Mix"/수정 button + its `EditMixView` sheet entirely; the system tracklist `EditButton()` (편집) now does double duty — entering edit mode turns the title/description `Text`s into live `TextField`s (bound to new `editableName`/`editableDescription`/`editableIsPublic` state, populated from `mix` via `.onChange(of: editMode?.wrappedValue)` on transition to `.active`) and reveals a Public/Private `Toggle` next to the existing globe/공개 label; exiting edit mode (Done) auto-saves all three to `mixes` in one PATCH, same convention as the tracklist reorder/delete already auto-committing with no separate Save step (confirmed with the user first, since this drops the old sheet's explicit Cancel affordance). A trash icon was added next to the Share button, *not* edit-mode-gated (always visible for `isOwnMix && !mix.isDefault`, matching "the default Listen Later mix can't be deleted"), wired to the same confirmation dialog the old sheet used.

`EditMixView.swift` deleted outright (its only caller was the sheet just removed) — safe since the project uses Xcode 16's `PBXFileSystemSynchronizedRootGroup` (no manual project.pbxproj entry to clean up). Verified live end-to-end via idb: typed into the title `TextField` (confirmed via the accessibility tree that it's a real `AXTextField`, not just unstyled `Text` — `.textFieldStyle(.plain)` looks identical to `Text` until focused, so this needed checking rather than assuming), tapped Done, watched both the nav bar and hero title update, then confirmed the write actually landed via a direct DB read (service-role REST query) before reverting the test edit the same way.
- **Follow-up, same feature: user flagged that the plain-style TextFields gave no visual hint they were editable** (exactly the failure mode the verification above should have caught but didn't, since `.textFieldStyle(.plain)` renders identically to `Text` when unfocused). Fixed by wrapping both the title and description fields in a bordered box while editing (`Color.sjSurface` background, `Color.sjBorder` 1pt stroke, 8pt corner radius) — only shown in edit mode, so the normal read view is untouched. Re-verified live; while testing, discovered the test mix's name was still "my mix 1TEST" from the earlier verification pass — the first DB-revert `PATCH` earlier in the session hadn't actually taken (unclear why; a second `PATCH` + immediate `GET` confirmed it that time). Left the app mid-edit-session with the stale in-memory name still loaded, so backed out via the nav back button (not the Done checkmark) to avoid re-saving the stale value over the now-correct DB row — confirmed via a final `GET` that `mixes.name` stayed "my mix 1" afterward.

**2026-07-08 (Mac, session 7 cont'd) — artist page load: conservative speed pass, two changes, one deferred:**

User asked to look for ways to speed up the artist page (`ArtistPageView` in `SearchView.swift`, ~2-4s to load) but explicitly asked for conservative changes only ("making bugs is worse than being slow"). Traced the full query path first rather than guessing: `load()` does a releases RPC, then a separate artist name/avatar row fetch, then (concurrently) a ratings aggregate query and `loadSongs()`, then `isLoading = false`, then a community-feed fetch. Ranked four options by risk:
1. **Done:** the releases RPC and the artist name/avatar fetch were sequential despite being fully independent — converted to `async let` + `await (a, b)`. Zero behavior change, pure latency win.
2. **Done:** `loadSongs()` was gated behind the same `isLoading` flag as everything else, but it's the single slowest query on the page — `recordings.artist_display` has **no index at all** (checked the migrations: `recordings` has a btree on `primary_artist_id` and a trigram index on `title`, nothing on `artist_display`), so its `ILIKE` match is a full scan of ~2.3M rows every time, identical in kind to the pre-fix search-page bug from earlier this week. Deferred it: `loadSongs()` now runs in an untracked `Task` with its own `isLoadingSongs` flag instead of blocking `isLoading`; the Songs tab shows its own `ProgressView` while it's still in flight instead of a premature "no songs" empty state. The default (Albums) tab never depended on song data, so this is a real, safe win, not just a relocation of the cost.
3. **Not done, flagged only:** switching the identity-aware branch's song lookup from `artist_display ILIKE` to `recordings.primary_artist_id = artistId` (indexed, fast) would fix the root cause of #2's slowness directly — but `artist_display` string-matching also catches songs where this artist is a featured/secondary credit (e.g. "Drake feat. Travis Scott"), which `primary_artist_id` alone would silently drop from the Songs tab. Real behavior change hiding in what looks like a perf fix; left for a deliberate decision rather than done silently.
4. **Not done, flagged only:** two separate round-trips to `ratings` for overlapping release-group-id sets (one light aggregate-only query, one heavier query for the community feed with different columns/limit) could be merged into one fetch computed two ways client-side — real duplication, but touches both consumers, more surface area than the "be conservative" ask covered.

Verified live via idb, both changes together: on a fresh load, the header (avatar, canonical name, release/rating counts) and the Albums tab's full list (60 releases, accurate community avg/count) rendered in ~1.5s, down from the earlier ~2.5–4.5s baseline measured on the same artist (Drake) before these changes. Separately confirmed the Songs tab genuinely shows its spinner (not a stale/incorrect empty state) when switched to before `loadSongs()` finishes, then populates correctly once it resolves.

**2026-07-08 (Mac, session 7 cont'd) — quest-completion badge built end-to-end (design → schema → claim flow), Instagram Story share unblocked (real Facebook App ID registered), Verified badge design picked:**

Three threads, same session:

**Instagram Story share — the last blocker is gone.** User created a Meta developer account and a Facebook app ("Create an app without a use case" — correct call, confirmed nothing in `InstagramShare.swift` calls any Graph API, it only needs a registered `source_application` ID for the pasteboard/URL-scheme handoff). Walked through Meta's dashboard live: business-portfolio prompt correctly skipped (no data access needed), the "iPhone/iPad Store ID" field hit a dead end (Meta tries to resolve it against the *public* App Store, which fails until sillajuku has been publicly live 24h+ — TestFlight doesn't count) but confirmed that field is unrelated to the sticker-share feature and safe to leave blank/defer. `Config.instagramFacebookAppID` set to the real App ID (`2171948313662107`), builds clean. **Cannot be fully verified from here** — the simulator can never install Instagram, so whether the share actually goes through (vs. the old "doesn't currently support sharing to Stories" rejection) needs the user's physical device once a Lightning/USB-C cable is available. Also clarified for the user: the existing `stickerImage` pasteboard key (not `backgroundImage`) already means this is a real movable/resizable "widget" overlay on the user's own photo/video, not a flat merged image — no extra work needed there, it was already built that way.

**Badge designs reviewed via an Artifact dashboard** (5 options each for Verified and Quest-completion, rendered at real in-app scale next to a sample username, with a "both at once" preview). Picked: **Verified = V1** (`checkmark.seal.fill`, sjBlue — the industry-standard seal shape, not yet wired to real data since granting still needs a decision on the manual-grant workflow discussed earlier). **Quest badge = the flower shape** (matching Q2, "Full Bloom") but user-chosen color rather than a fixed gold.

**Quest-completion badge built end-to-end:**
- `20260708000002_quest_badge.sql` — `profiles.badge_color` (nullable text) + a `CHECK` constraint against a curated 5-color palette (gold/coral/violet/mint/rose) + a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger (`prevent_badge_color_change`) that hard-blocks changing a non-null `badge_color` — the one invariant ("permanent once claimed") that can't be left to client-side discipline alone. Deliberately does NOT re-validate quest completion server-side; that's several tables' worth of logic already living in `QuestChecklistViewModel`, not worth duplicating for a cosmetic feature.
- `QuestBadgeColor` enum + `QuestBadgeView` (reuses the exact `Image("icon-flower")` + `.renderingMode(.template)` pattern `ScoreBadge` already uses, just tinted the claimed color) + `VerifiedBadgeView` (`checkmark.seal.fill`, sjBlue — trivial since it's a system symbol, no custom shape needed) in new `Components/QuestBadge.swift`.
- `QuestChecklistViewModel.shouldOfferBadgeRedeem` (`personalQuestsComplete && badgeColor == nil`) and `claimBadge(_:)`.
- `BadgeRedeemView` — the actual redeem sheet: live-updating flower preview, 5-swatch picker, permanence warning copy, disabled Claim button until a color is picked.
- `MainTabView` auto-presents it every launch (not once-ever — no dismiss-and-forget state, by design) while `shouldOfferBadgeRedeem` is true.
- Badge renders next to the display name in both `ProfileView` (own profile) and `UserProfileView` (viewing someone else) headers.
- Added proper Korean localizations for all 5 new color names + the sheet's copy — caught that "Mint" alone was rendering in Korean (a coincidental match with a pre-existing unrelated catalog entry) while the other four fell back to English, which would've read as an inconsistent half-localized screen.

**Real regression caught and fixed before it shipped.** First pass added `badge_color` to three `.select()` calls (`ProfileView`, `QuestChecklistViewModel`, `UserProfileView`) alongside the Swift model changes — reasonable-looking, but confirmed live via idb that this **broke the entire Profile tab** (generic avatar, "프로필" instead of "@junnwest", name/bio gone entirely), because PostgREST 400s the *whole* query when a select references a column that doesn't exist yet, and `try?` was silently swallowing that into a fully-nil profile. Root cause: the migration above hasn't been applied live (this Mac still doesn't have `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN`, confirmed again this session — no `supabase` CLI, no DB connection string either), so `badge_color` doesn't exist in the actual database yet. Reverted the three `.select()` additions (kept everything else — the Swift-side `badgeColor: String?` fields are harmless as long as they're never requested in a query, since Codable just decodes a missing key as nil) and reverified the Profile tab loads correctly again. **Those three `.select()` lines need `badge_color` added back in once the migration is live** — they're marked with `TEMP` comments pointing at each other.

**⚑ ACTION NEEDED (Windows or manual SQL editor): apply `20260708000002_quest_badge.sql`, then restore `badge_color` to the three `.select()` calls flagged with `TEMP` comments** (`ProfileView.swift`, `QuestChecklistViewModel.swift`, `UserProfileView.swift`) so the badge actually persists/displays. Verified live otherwise: the redeem sheet (temporarily force-presented for testing, reverted after) renders and picks colors correctly, the live flower preview updates instantly, the Claim button enables only once a color is chosen, and tapping Claim against the *not-yet-migrated* DB fails silently with no crash (exactly the expected pre-migration behavior) rather than corrupting state.

**Follow-up, same day — redeem sheet sizing fixed, and a live look at the Verified badge.** User flagged a large gap between the color swatches and the Claim button in `BadgeRedeemView`. Root cause: the sheet had no `.presentationDetents`, so it defaulted to (near-)full height with a `Spacer()` shoving the button to the bottom, away from everything above it. First fix attempt used a guessed fixed detent (`.height(430)`) — rebuilt and caught live via idb that 430pt undershot the real content height, leaving the Claim button clipped off the bottom of the sheet entirely (worse than the original bug: now unreachable, not just visually loose). Replaced the guess with a measured value: a `GeometryReader` + `onAppear`/`onChange` (not a `PreferenceKey` — same pattern already proven more reliable earlier this session for an unrelated sizing fix in `MixDetailView`) reads the VStack's real intrinsic height into `contentHeight`, fed directly into `.presentationDetents([.height(contentHeight)])`. Verified live: sheet now sizes to content exactly, drag indicator down to the Claim button with even spacing and nothing cut off.

Also temporarily force-rendered `VerifiedBadgeView()` next to the display name on the Profile header (reverted after) so the user could see the actual V1 pick (`checkmark.seal.fill`, sjBlue) in context rather than only in the earlier design-options Artifact — confirmed it reads cleanly at 14pt next to a bold name, matching the mockup.

**2026-07-08 (Mac, session 7 cont'd) — both badge migrations applied live; a real "badge missing from Explore feed" bug found and fixed:**

User applied `20260708000002_quest_badge.sql` and `20260708000003_verified_badge.sql` directly via the SQL editor. Restored `badge_color`/`is_verified` to all five `TEMP`-marked `.select()` calls (`ProfileView.swift` ×2, `QuestChecklistViewModel.swift`, `UserProfileView.swift`, `HomeView.swift`'s `feedSelect`/`mixShareSelect`) — clean rebuild, both features live end-to-end for the first time. Flagged own account `is_verified = true` via a direct service-role `PATCH` (user's choice, for live visual testing) and verified via idb: `@junnwest ✓` renders correctly in the Profile nav bar and in the Profile tab's own mix-share posts.

**User then reported the badge missing from the Home tab's feed posts — a real bug, not the expected pre-migration dormancy.** Root cause: `HomeView.swift`'s Explore tab doesn't use `feedSelect` at all — it uses a separate, deliberately lighter query (`feedSelectLiteBotFilterable`) split into parallel human/bot fetches, because embedding `release_groups` in a `created_at`-ordered query against the ~10k-row bot-dominated `ratings` table blows Postgres's statement timeout (documented in an existing code comment). That select was never given `is_verified` — the earlier propagation pass had explicitly decided this was safe to skip with the reasoning "bots won't be verified," which is true but irrelevant: the *human* arm of that same split-fetch is exactly how real (verifiable) users' posts reach the Explore tab, so the badge could never show there for anyone. Fixed by adding `is_verified` to `feedSelectLiteBotFilterable`. Verified via a direct query matching the app's exact select shape against the live DB (not just a UI screenshot, since Explore's ranking/recency window made it unreliable to re-find one specific 6-day-old post live): confirmed `is_verified: true` now comes back correctly for `@junnwest`'s rating rows. **Lesson for future select-scoping calls:** "will this ever apply to a verified account" is the right question, not "is this select currently only fetching from an unverifiable population" — the second framing missed that this query's whole purpose was fetching real (non-bot) users.

**2026-07-07 (Windows, session 3) — offline mock backend for the web app (`npm run mock` + `npm run dev:mock`); fix list added to README Known issues:**

*Charts performance pass (same session, deployed):* with the DB incident over, re-measured live: everything recovered **except `get_silla_leaderboard` — ~7.2s even under service role** (live bayesian calibration over all ratings), i.e. permanently over anon's 3s timeout, so the web Ranking block 57014'd on every load (this was masked during the incident; "silla completes via API role" had only ever been checked without the anon budget). Web-side fix, no DB surgery (the silla function has a 4-round timeout-saga history and the Mac just rebuilt it for primary_genre): new **`/api/charts/silla`** route runs the RPC under the service role (8s budget, `maxDuration 15`) and caches via existing `lib/cache.ts` Redis (15 min TTL) + CDN `s-maxage`/`stale-while-revalidate` headers — first hit per filter combo is slow, everyone after is instant. `lib/sj/sillaClient.ts` adds a per-combo client memo; both `RankingBlock` and `/charts/[slug]` ranking now use it. Charts page also loads progressively now: only the unlock gauge gates the layout (was: whole page waited on `Promise.all` of 7 RPCs), the 3 songs-chart RPCs fetch lazily on first Songs-tab open, the Ranking block shows skeleton rows instead of "…", and filter switches dim the stale list in place instead of blanking it.

*Charts layout fix (same session, deployed):* below `lg` the charts grid stacks and the 10-row Silla Ranking block pushed Trending below the fold ("out the window"). `TrendingCard` (pure presentational, no fetching) now renders first in the stacked layout via a `lg:hidden` slot at the top of the grid, with the original right-rail copy wrapped in `hidden lg:block` — wide-screen layout unchanged.

*Follow-up fix (same session):* first in-browser run showed empty feed/charts even in mock mode — the CSP in `next.config.mjs` had `connect-src … https://*.supabase.co` only, so the **browser blocked every request to `http://localhost:54321`** (Node-side probes bypass CSP, which is why all 27 checks passed anyway). Fixed by appending the `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` origin to `connect-src` **only when it starts with `http://localhost`** (mock mode) — prod/normal dev CSP unchanged. Also made the mock's CORS preflight echo `Access-Control-Request-Headers`. Verified: served CSP now contains `http://localhost:54321`, preflight 204 with correct allow headers.

Built a standalone fake Supabase so the web app can be tested with zero network/database — motivated by prod being unusable during the `primary_genre` DB-load incident (Explore showed *"No ratings yet — be the first!"*, which is actually error-swallowing: the feed query times out and pages destructure `{ data }` ignoring `error` — added to the fix list). New files: `apps/web/scripts/mock/server.ts` (implements enough GoTrue + PostgREST: implicit-flow OAuth `/authorize` → hash tokens, `/user`, refresh/password grants, logout; REST filters eq/neq/in/gte/is/ilike/cs/ov + order/limit/offset, `Accept: vnd.pgrst.object` single-row semantics incl. PGRST116, `Prefer: count=exact` Content-Range, insert/upsert with per-table conflict keys, PATCH/DELETE, `/rpc/*`) and `apps/web/scripts/mock/data.ts` (pre-embedded dummy rows in the app's exact select shapes — FEED_SELECT embeds, notification `actor:`/`rating:` aliases, `releases→release_tracks→recordings` tracklist chain — 12 release groups, 8 artists, 6 profiles, ~24 ratings, likes/comments/follows/mixes/notifications, 16 RPC handlers; covers are data-URI SVGs, already allowed by the CSP's `img-src data:`). Plus `.env.mock` (committed, no secrets — empty overrides keep PostHog/Upstash/Spotify off) and package.json scripts `mock` / `dev:mock` (port 3100, `node --env-file=.env.mock` so it wins over `.env.local`). Sign-in mapping: Spotify/Apple → seeded `demo` user (onboarded), Google → fresh `newbie` (tests onboarding end-to-end; profile upsert works against the mock). Verified with a 27-check supabase-js probe using the app's real query strings (all pass) + Next dev in mock mode: /login, /, /charts all 200, `/api/check-username` correctly resolves against the mock server-side. Also added a prioritized **⚑ FIX LIST** to README → Known issues (stop the `primary_genre` backfill; error-vs-empty states; visibility web parity; replace hand-maintained `lib/db/types.ts` with `supabase gen types`; session-4 feature parity; `search_artists` at-risk) — `gh` CLI isn't installed on this machine, so the note lives in the repo rather than as a GitHub Issue.

**2026-07-07 (Windows, session 2) — web "stuck on onboarding / blank tabs" root-caused and fixed (schema drift from the Mac's visibility overhaul):**

Web bounced every signed-in user to `/onboarding` and all tab pages were blank. Root cause: `SessionContext`'s `PROFILE_COLS` still selected `profiles.listen_later_visibility`, but the Mac session-4 migration `20260706000012_profile_visibility_overhaul.sql` (applied live) renamed it to `library_visibility` — the select failed with 42703, `loadProfile` **silently ignored the error**, so `profile` stayed `null` and the "no username → /onboarding" redirect fired for everyone. Classic parallel-session merge casualty: web was rebuilt (Windows 07-06) against the pre-overhaul schema while the Mac shipped the rename the same day. Fixes, all in `apps/web`:

- `SessionContext.tsx` — `PROFILE_COLS` now selects `library_visibility, stats_visibility`; `loadProfile` logs errors and sets a `profileError` flag; the onboarding redirect is skipped when the fetch *failed* (fetch-failed ≠ never-onboarded — this is what made the bug a lockout instead of a console error).
- `lib/db/types.ts` — `ProfileRow.listen_later_visibility` → `library_visibility`, added `stats_visibility`.
- `settings/page.tsx` — privacy picker writes `library_visibility`; dropped the `'Followers only'` option (000012's new check constraint only allows Public/Private — saving it would 23514).

Verified with a live-DB probe (exact `PROFILE_COLS` select, anon + service): failed 42703 before, passes both roles after. `tsc --noEmit` clean. Full visibility-overhaul web parity (general toggle + Advanced inherit-when-NULL overrides + RPC enforcement) remains open — this was the minimal unbreak. Env vars were checked first and were NOT the issue (`.env.local` already complete on this device).

Follow-up (same day): user reported still stuck — because they were testing **production** (www.sillajuku.com), which it turns out HAD auto-deployed the rebuild from main (README's "not yet deployed" was stale) *with* the bug, while the fix sat uncommitted locally. Confirmed by grepping the deployed JS chunks for `listen_later_visibility` (present). Also end-to-end-verified the full onboarding `finish()` path with a throwaway auth user (sign-in → select → upsert → re-select: all pass; profiles RLS is fine — insert `auth.uid() = id`, select open). Committed + pushed (`b79212b`); polled prod until the new layout chunk went live — stale column gone, `library_visibility` present. **Do NOT revert to the pre-rebuild web** (user floated it): the old UI queries columns the 2026-06-24 renovation dropped; it is strictly more broken. Note: the `primary_genre` backfill incident was still running at this point (4 workers, 15,968/294,797 filled) — feed/trending/most-rated/silla/search still intermittently 57014 under anon until that Mac-side process is stopped; that is a separate issue from the onboarding lockout.

**2026-07-07 (Windows) — both timeout-fix migrations APPLIED live (new Management-API path); found an out-of-repo `primary_genre` backfill IO-starving the DB:**

Continuation of the 07-06 session-2 debugging. User supplied a Supabase personal access token (`SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` in `.env.local`, placeholder added to `.env.example` — **copy to the Mac's `.env.local`**), which unblocked applying SQL from this machine for the first time: new **`scripts/db-exec.ts`** posts a migration file (or `--sql "…"`) to the Management API `database/query` endpoint. No more SQL-editor-only bottleneck.

- ✅ **`20260706000016` (silla precomputed restore) applied + functionally verified** — `get_silla_leaderboard` returns rows again on both the global and country paths (was: never completed at any timeout).
- ✅ **`20260706000017` (search trgm indexes) applied + verified under anon** — the 3 GIN indexes were built with `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` (Management API runs them fine, ~37–48s each) to avoid blocking live writers. One live correction to the migration as written: hosted Supabase **denies `SET pg_trgm.word_similarity_threshold`** ("permission denied to set parameter" — extension-GUC grants, PG 15+), so the function keeps the operator's default threshold 0.6 instead of 0.5 (fuzzy-typo arm slightly stricter; substring arms + ranking unchanged). `search_release_groups` now passes under anon (~2.9s **during** the load incident below; expect far less on a quiet DB — was: hard 57014 always).
- **Why mobile "worked" while web was blank, confirmed empirically** (throwaway authenticated user, timed): `authenticated` gets the 8s statement timeout (like service), `anon` gets 3s — and the old search RPC sat at ~7.4–8.6s, i.e. on the knife's edge: iOS (authenticated, sequential queries, `try?` fallbacks) usually squeaked through warm; web (parallel queries, sometimes anon) died.
- ⚠️ **Open incident: an out-of-repo PostgREST client is bulk-updating `release_groups.primary_genre`** — a column that exists live but appears in **no migration in this repo** (nothing in the codebase references it at all; not pg_cron; no local process → likely a Mac-side script). `pg_stat_statements`: each UPDATE modifies **exactly 1 row in ~3.8s** (267 calls → 267 rows), 4 workers concurrent, 15,166 filled / **279,646 remaining** — days of runway. Every update rewrites a row in the index-heaviest table (HNSW embedding + 5+ GIN trgm indexes → non-HOT churn), IO-starving the Micro instance. This is what's behind today's across-the-board slowness (trivial PK-join embeds, feed, charts trending/most-rated intermittently 57014 under anon; `pipeline:status`'s own counts read 0 because its count(*)s time out). **Not killed** — terminating DB-side statements is whack-a-mole while the client retries; needs the source process stopped (user action) or a deliberate decision to let it run.
- Verification snapshot (anon, during the incident): search ✅ 2.9s, search_artists ✅ 1.3s, pulse/unlock ✅; silla/feed/trending/most-rated still 57014 — all load-victims now, not plan problems (silla completes in seconds via the API role). **Re-run `scripts/debug-web-queries.ts` once the backfill is stopped** — expect all green.

**2026-07-07 (Mac, session 6) — Album detail redesign + long-press quick actions, four separate statement-timeout bugs found and fixed, and a likely-runaway backfill discovered live via `pg_stat_activity`:**

Started as a UI request (unify the album detail page's manual/instinct rating UI) and widened into a full afternoon of bug-hunting after the user reported "Explore feed shows only mix posts, no ratings" — which turned out to be the first of four independent timeout bugs, all eventually traced to the same live incident.

- **Album detail page redesign.** Manual and Instinct rating modes now share one UI: community avg/count moved to the top of the rating section (was below), a unified "Your Rating" body (unrated → one styled Add button, icon swapped by mode; rated → score pill + Share/Re-rank/Edit/Delete row). **Edit** is new — opens just the comment/mix step (`PostRatingOptionsView`, now accepts an `initialComment` to prefill/allow clearing an existing review) without re-triggering scoring or comparisons; needed `AlbumDetailViewModel` to start tracking `currentRatingId`/`reviewText`. Added two new sections: **other users' ratings** on the album (horizontal scroll reusing `HomeView`'s actual `FeedCard`, made non-private for reuse, own like/comment/save/block state mirroring `HomeViewModel`'s pattern; "View All" pushes a vertical version) and **public mixes containing the album**, ranked by a popularity score (mix likes ×3 + owner follower count) instead of unordered, capped to 5 with a "View All". `AlbumPost`/`PostRow`/`StarRatingView` deleted as dead code once superseded.
- **Long-press quick-actions menu, shipped for bare album art.** New `Components/AlbumContextMenu.swift` — a reusable `.contextMenu` (cover preview + Rate/Add to Mix/Share/View Artist) wired into 17 call sites across Search (albums grid, songs list, all 4 discovery carousels), Rankings/Charts (all 10 card/row types), Profile's Rated list (merged with the existing Delete Rating item), other users' profiles, and Mix detail's album rows. Deliberately **not** added to post cards (already have an overlapping "..." menu) or tracklist rows (no cover art there, no mix/share flow for individual songs yet).
- **Bug 1 — Explore feed showing only mix posts, no ratings.** `HomeViewModel.fetchExplorePool()` splits ratings by `is_bot` before ranking (bots are ~99.8% of the 10k+ `ratings` rows); the bot-side query — filtered + ordered + embedding `release_groups`/`artists` — was hitting Postgres's statement timeout (confirmed live: identical query minus the embed returns in <1s, with it times out every time). Since the caller does `(try? await fetchExplorePool()) ?? []`, one bad branch zeroed the *entire* combined pool while the separate mix-shares fetch succeeded fine. Fixed by splitting into a lighter ratings+profile query plus a separate batched `release_groups` fetch by id, stitched client-side — no schema/migration change needed.
- **Bug 2 — Rankings/Charts tab unlocked prematurely** (should read ~2.9k/10k and stay locked). `RankingsView`'s `get_rankings_unlock_status()` RPC was *also* intermittently hitting the statement timeout; the client's `if let status = viewModel.unlockStatus, !status.albumUnlocked { locked } else { unlocked }` fails **open** when the RPC fails (`unlockStatus` becomes `nil`, so `if let` fails, falling through to the unlocked branch). Confirmed the actual unlock math was already correct (a "bot decay" migration from 2026-07-06 already discounts bot-authored ratings) — the RPC just needed to actually succeed to show it. Fixed the client to fail locked instead of open; the RPC's own occasional timeout is the same root incident as the rest of this list.
- **Bug 3 — Search "takes forever."** `SearchViewModel.search()` ran albums/songs/artists **sequentially**; songs specifically go through a raw `recordings.title ILIKE` scan (~2.3M rows, no dedicated RPC unlike albums/artists) that measured as 3+ seconds or an outright timeout for most real terms tested live (`drak`, `seoul`, `jimin` all timed out; `taylor` barely made it at 3.1s). Fixed by running all three concurrently (`async let`) and racing the song lookup against a 2s timeout so a slow/hung song query no longer blocks album/artist results that already came back. The underlying fix — songs need their own normalized search RPC like albums/artists have — still needs backend access this session didn't have.
- **The live incident, found via `pg_stat_activity`.** Prompted by a Supabase dashboard "CPU usage high" alert (separate from a "Security Definer View" advisory finding on `public.critic_affiliation`, fixed via a one-line `security_invoker = true` migration — `20260708000000_critic_affiliation_security_invoker.sql`, verified safe, not yet applied), the user ran `pg_stat_activity` and found **4 concurrent PostgREST-issued `UPDATE release_groups SET primary_genre = ... WHERE id = ANY(...)` queries**, one 9.85s into execution. `primary_genre` doesn't exist anywhere in this repo — no migration, no script, in either app — so its source is untracked (uncommitted script, SQL-editor-launched, or from a machine/branch this session had no visibility into). This is very likely the actual root cause tying together all three bugs above (all touch `release_groups` directly or via join) plus a fourth symptom: `scripts/pipeline-status.ts`'s `count()` helper doesn't check its query's `error`, so the same timeout made it silently report `release_groups 0 · recordings 0` — reading like the catalog got wiped rather than a failed count. **Full handoff written to README's START HERE** for Windows to identify/throttle/stop the backfill and decide whether it should become a tracked migration + script.
- **RLS auth-wrap audit completed and fix migration landed.** A full trace of every `CREATE POLICY`/`ALTER POLICY` across both migration directories (chronologically, to each table's currently-live definition) found **60 policies across 27 tables** calling `auth.uid()`/`auth.role()` unwrapped in `USING`/`WITH CHECK` — Postgres re-evaluates these per-row instead of once per query. `20260708000001_rls_auth_initplan_fix.sql` fixes all 60 via `ALTER POLICY` (not DROP+CREATE — preserves grants, no drop window); every clause was re-verified directly against its source migration rather than trusting the audit summary alone (caught 3 EXISTS-based ones — `list_item_tracks`, `user_ranking_entries`, `mix_items`/`mix_likes`/`mix_shares` — where the real join shape differed from what a naive guess would've produced), and 16 `FOR ALL`/`FOR UPDATE` policies got both `USING` and `WITH CHECK` explicitly restated since Postgres silently copies USING into WITH CHECK at creation when the latter's omitted — leaving only USING fixed would've left a stale unwrapped copy behind. Flagged but not fixed: 6 tables have an overlapping owner (`FOR ALL`) + public (`FOR SELECT`) policy pair (not redundant, just double-evaluated — needs a judgment call, not a mechanical fix); `daily_answers`/`daily_questions` are defined twice across the two migration directories with different names (fix assumes apps/web's naming is live); `list_item_tracks` has no migration under apps/web at all.
  - **Real discrepancy caught while double-checking the audit against the user's original advisor paste, not fixed:** the advisor screenshot showed `ratings` with policies named `"Allow authenticated users to select/insert/update/delete their ratings"` *in addition to* the tracked `ratings_select/insert/update/delete` — those names don't exist in any migration file (grepped both directories, zero hits), meaning `ratings` likely carries ~7 live policies, not the 4 this migration can see, probably created via the SQL editor directly (same untracked-change blind spot as `reconcile_prestige_scores()` and the unidentified `primary_genre` backfill). Couldn't fix blind without their exact clause text — flagged in both the migration's header and README's handoff with the `pg_policies` query needed to check, plus a recommendation to spot-check other high-traffic tables the same way.
- **Note added on reconcile:** by the time this pulled in Windows's session-3/session-2 entries above, the `primary_genre` backfill turned out to be real tracked work (`20260706000001/2` + `20260706000016-000020` migrations, `scripts/backfill-primary-genre.ts`), not a mystery — see those entries for the other half of this investigation. The bug fixes in this session (Explore feed, Rankings gate, search) stand regardless of the backfill's cause, since they fix how the client behaves when *any* query times out, not just this one.
- **Also landed this session:** `SearchView`'s empty-results state only rendered for queries 2+ characters, so a 1-character query rendered nothing at all — collapsing the whole screen's content to just the search bar, which SwiftUI then centered vertically instead of pinning to the top (reported as "why is the search bar in the middle of the screen"). Fixed by always rendering the icon+message empty state, with the message differing by query length ("No results" vs. "Keep typing to search…").
